## F-23 — Review convergence

- **Commits:** `feat/f23-review-convergence`, branched off `chore/reconcile-f24` (`9fe41ff`, the F-24 reconcile that origin/main doesn't have yet).
- **Built:** 2026-10-20. Interactive session, per BUILD-ORDER Phase 1b (hardening WOs change what judges agents).
- **Design notes:** the convergence machinery lives in `factory/l2.py` with the rest of the orchestrator side of the L2 contract, not in a new module. `finding_key` hashes (file, axis, normalised summary) — deliberately excluding `line` (drifts as the diff moves) and `severity` (the same defect re-rated is the same finding); the key is computed at parse time so every stored report carries it. `apply_convergence` classifies one round's verdict: a **prior key whose file the delta didn't touch** (or with no raise sha / no file — raised in an earlier run) is `state: "carried"` — still in the findings, so a carried *blocking* finding still fails the round, but never listed as new; a prior key on a file the delta *did* touch is a repeat after a failed fix — it stands as the round's finding and its key still counts as already raised; in delta mode a **new** finding outside the delta needs `missed_earlier` or is dropped with the reason recorded in `dropped`. The effective verdict is recomputed over what survives (a declared `fail` and a parse error both stand; a declared `pass` whose only blocker was dropped becomes the pass the reviewer declared). The orchestrator keeps the per-run state: `prior` (key → raise sha) seeded from the task's progress-log `finding_keys` entries (cross-run: sha None), `raised` (this run's findings for the next round's carried list), `last_reviewed` (the head L2 last saw). The fix prompt (round 2+ of a run) renders the failed round's numbered findings under `## Findings to fix` with the fix-the-class instruction; the round-2+ review prompt carries the carried list, the delta as `## Delta since the last review (primary scope)` and the full diff as reference. `reviewer.md` gains the standing "Fix rounds" section (pin updated in `tests/test_f09.py`, the sanctioned route). `review_not_converging` added to the schema enum and to TASK-CONTRACT.md's escalation line with its owner action (the F-10 review7 sweep test forced the doc, as designed).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f23.py::test_ac_1` — the key is stable across re-parse and text noise (case, collapsed whitespace), moves with file or axis; `apply_convergence` marks a repeat whose file the delta didn't touch `carried` in the report while its blocking severity still fails the round, and a same-key finding on a delta-touched file stays the round's own finding. Integration: a two-round run (identical impl both rounds, so the head never moves) stores `key` in each attempt's validator report, marks round 2's finding `carried`, and records `finding_keys` in both progress-log entries. `test_carried_keys_survive_text_normalisation_across_rounds` repeats the round-2 finding with different casing/spacing.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — unit: in delta mode a new out-of-delta finding without `missed_earlier` is dropped (`out_of_delta_without_missed_earlier` recorded) and a declared `pass` whose only blocker was dropped becomes `pass`; with a justification, and inside the delta, findings stand; round one (no delta) drops nothing. Integration: a moving-head run's round-2 reviewer prompt carries `## Delta since the last review (primary scope)` (containing only round 2's change) and `## Full diff (base...head, reference only)`; the one blocking round-2 finding cites a file outside the delta without justification, is dropped, and the run opens the PR. `test_missed_earlier_is_parsed_onto_the_finding` covers the field's parsing.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — round 2's implementer prompt carries `## Findings to fix` with the finding text, `sibling` and `defect class` (round 1 has no such section); round 2's reviewer prompt carries the explicit sibling check (`reviewer.md` documents it standing).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — schema half: a queue whose attempt escalates `review_not_converging` loads through the real `load_tasks` (red against the pre-WO schema: `ContractError … 'review_not_converging' is not one of […]`, run with the enum line stashed). Orchestrator half: a reviewer repeating round 1's finding verbatim (head moving each round — the repeat is on changed code, the stronger case) escalates `review_not_converging` after exactly 2 rounds with budget for a third unspent, `needs_human`, no PR. Contrast: a genuinely new finding each round runs all 3 rounds to `attempts_exhausted`; a mixed round (one carried + one new) continues and passes in round 3.
- **Cross-run seeding** proven: `test_prior_keys_seed_from_an_earlier_runs_progress_log` — a progress log from an earlier run carrying `finding_keys` makes the next run's round 1 carry the same key and, when the FAIL contains nothing else, escalate `review_not_converging` in the first round.
- **Changed existing tests (behaviour changed on purpose):** `tests/test_f10.py::test_reviewer_fail_is_retried_then_exhausted` — its scenario (the reviewer re-failing with the identical finding forever) is exactly what F-23 stops: it now scripts a genuinely new finding per round to keep proving the retry-to-exhausted path, and asserts `attempts_exhausted`; the repeat case is F-23's own AC-4 test. `tests/test_f09.py` — the `reviewer.md` pin (content-hash change from the new Fix-rounds section).
- **Evidence (red-first):** the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.l2.apply_convergence`/`finding_key` (all four refs) before any F-23 code; the schema half of AC-4 additionally proven red against the pre-change enum (ContractError above). After: 7 F-23 tests / 396 total passing locally (macOS, real srt; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-23 owner review round (PR #35; 9 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review9`)

All ten review tests (`review1` and `review1b` cover finding 1's two halves) were red against `1d525a3` for the reported reasons before any fix. Two needed fixture corrections during the green phase (review5's "fresh finding" case was scripted with an empty delta — a file-naming finding there is legitimately dropped; review8's pass-round verdict defaulted to declared-fail); the code fixes were unchanged by those corrections.

- **(1) The review frontier advanced on reviews that never happened** (correctness): `last_reviewed` and the `prior` raise-shas now move only when the reviewer session completed AND the verdict parsed (`parse_error is None`). A crashed, timed-out or invalid review reviews nothing: the next round's delta still covers that head, so code no reviewer ever saw cannot become "outside the delta" and be dropped. `test_review1_an_invalid_review_does_not_advance_the_frontier` (garbage verdict JSON) and `test_review1b_failed_session_variant` (failed session): round 2's prompt carries `## Diff (base...head)`, not the delta section.
- **(2) The delta diff crashed on rewritten history** (correctness): `_commit_work` only checks descent from base, so an amended/reset earlier round leaves `last_reviewed` unreachable in the fresh partial clone. New `_is_ancestor` (merge-base rc semantics, GIT_HARDENING; any non-zero — including a missing object — reads as "no") gates the delta: not an ancestor → this round falls back to full-diff scope (delta mode off, nothing dropped). `test_review2_delta_survives_a_rewritten_prior_round`: a round-2 `git commit --amend` now ends `pr_opened` with a full-diff review prompt, not `orchestrator_error`.
- **(3) The stop fired after one genuine fix attempt** (correctness): the stop is no longer raw key membership. `apply_convergence` computes `L2Report.no_progress` and a repeat on a file the delta **touched** is an attempt in progress (state `new`), not a bounce; only repeats on **untouched** files count. `test_review3_a_repeat_on_a_fixed_file_is_not_the_stop`: same finding re-raised three rounds on a moving head → `attempts_exhausted` after 3 rounds, not `review_not_converging` after 2.
- **(4) Fix findings duplicated; hashes leaked into history** (efficiency): `ProgressLog.render` never renders `finding_keys` (machine data for the next run's seeding) and takes `omit_last_findings` — on a fix round the newest entry renders without its findings, because the `## Findings to fix` block carries them with the sibling instruction. The entry itself still renders: F-10 AC-10 (every round receives entries 1..N) keeps its test unchanged (`test_progress_log_reaches_every_round` caught my first cut, which dropped the whole entry). `test_review4_fix_prompt_carries_the_findings_once_and_no_key_hashes`: the finding text appears exactly once in round 2's implementer prompt and no 64-hex hash appears at all.
- **(5) Four parallel structures for "already raised"** (simplification): `already_raised` snapshot, the ordering-coupled `prior` mutation, `raised` and `round_keys` are gone as separate concepts. The stop is decided inside `apply_convergence` where the pre-update prior set is in hand (`no_progress` on the report, asserted by `test_review5_the_stop_is_decided_where_the_prior_set_lives` for the in-run/touched/cross-run/fresh cases); the orchestrator reads one flag, and `round_keys`/`raised` are derived from the classified report in the single block that advances the frontier.
- **(6) Declared fail with everything dropped retried with nothing** (correctness): a FAIL whose every finding was dropped and whose declared fail stands with zero findings now sets `no_progress` (the all-dropped case), so the run escalates instead of burning the budget. `test_review6_declared_fail_with_everything_dropped_stops`: round 2 declares fail with one out-of-delta finding → `review_not_converging` after 2 rounds, round 3 never spent (red: `attempts_exhausted` after 3).
- **(7) Fileless findings were always dropped in delta rounds** (correctness): a finding that names no file cannot be shown to sit in unchanged code, and contract-level findings (missing test, unmet AC) rarely name one. The drop rule now requires a named file outside the delta; fileless findings are never dropped. `test_review7_fileless_findings_are_never_dropped`: a declared pass with a blocking fileless finding no longer flips to PASS at round 2 — the finding stands, the round fails, the PR opens only after round 3 genuinely passes.
- **(8) Log-seeded keys triggered the stop on a fresh run's first round; pass rounds logged keys** (correctness): cross-run carries (raise sha `None`) no longer count toward `no_progress` — only carries raised inside **this** run do, so a re-queued task's first review is a normal round even when it re-raises an old key (the rewritten `test_prior_keys_seed_from_an_earlier_runs_progress_log`: round 1 carried but not stopped; round 2, same key now with an in-run sha on an untouched file, is the bounce and stops). And the pass path no longer logs `finding_keys` (`test_review8_a_pass_round_does_not_log_finding_keys`): a passed round's findings were accepted as non-blocking; recording their keys would poison a later rejected-PR fix round. Failed rounds still log them — that is the seed.
- **(9) Dropped findings never reached the human** (correctness): `_numbered_findings` emits dropped L2 findings as marked lines (`[L2 dropped R-1] … out of scope, no missed_earlier — for the record, not to fix`), so they ride the numbered findings into the log; and `prbody`'s Validator report section pulls them out above the JSON blob as "**Reviewer findings dropped as out of scope** … read them before merging". `test_review9_dropped_findings_reach_the_human` asserts both the PR-body callout and the log line.
- **Also changed:** `reviewer.md`'s standing drop rule now says "a new finding **on a file** outside the delta" and states fileless findings are never dropped (pin updated through `PROMPT_PINS`); `test_f10.py::test_reviewer_fail_is_retried_then_exhausted`'s scripted findings are fileless now — the stub writes identical content each round, so the delta is empty and a file-naming new finding would be dropped (all-dropped → the finding-6 stop), changing what that test proves; fileless findings stand, keeping it a 3-round retry-to-exhaustion proof.
- **Evidence (review round):** 10 review tests, all red against `1d525a3` (review7 after its fileless fixture was corrected to actually pass `file=None`); after the fixes 17 F-23 tests / **406 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt), ruff clean.

### F-23 owner review round 2 (PR #35, head `116e16b`; 9 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review10`–`test_review17` + the reworked F-10 retry test)

All eight new F-23 review tests were red against `116e16b` for the reported reasons before any fix (review12 after its assertion was scoped to the `## Findings to fix` section — the progress log legitimately repeats old findings, so a whole-prompt check could not see the mismatch). The ninth finding (test coverage) is the F-10 test rework itself, red the moment the l2 rule changed landed.

- **(1) The rewrite fallback re-introduced the finding-3 bug** (correctness): in the fallback the round reviews the full diff with no delta, and an empty `delta_files` made every in-run repeat "untouched" → carried → stop, though the implementer had just rewritten those files. `apply_convergence` now requires **a real delta** to demote an in-run repeat to carried: without one the repeat is an attempt in progress (`state: "new"`, no stop). Cross-run carries (raise sha `None`) still carry — no continuity ever existed. `test_review10_fallback_round_does_not_carry_repeats_as_untouched`: an amending implementer re-flagged on impl.py now runs 3 rounds to `attempts_exhausted` (red: `review_not_converging` after 2).
- **(2) Dropped findings reached the implementer's fix block** (correctness): `_numbered_findings` grew `include_dropped=False`, and the next round's fix list is built with it (`_fix_findings`) — dropped findings stay in the round's full numbered record (log, PR evidence, the human's read) and never in `## Findings to fix`, where "fix the defect class" of an out-of-scope finding would pull edits into code the round ruled untouchable. `test_review11_dropped_findings_stay_out_of_the_fix_block`: round 3's fix section carries the actionable finding, not the dropped one; the log entry keeps both.
- **(3) A failed review left the carried list and the fix list describing different rounds** (correctness): a round whose review produced no verdict contributes no L2 lines to the fix list (the invalid-verdict marker is the reviewer's failure, not work); the last valid review's findings are substituted instead (`last_valid_l2`), so the reviewer's "already recorded" list and the implementer's fix list describe the same round. `test_review12_a_failed_review_keeps_the_fix_list_and_carried_list_together`: after a garbage round-2 review, round 3's fix section still carries round 1's open finding (red: only the invalid-verdict marker).
- **(4) The stop ignored the round's own L0/L1 verdicts** (correctness): new `_round_stuck(l0, l1, l2)` gates the escalation on L0 and L1 both passing — the ordering "L2 runs last" becomes enforced fact instead of wiring accident; a future advisory L1 can no longer discard fresh deterministic work as "not converging". `test_review13_the_stop_requires_l0_and_l1_to_have_passed`: pass/pass/stuck stops; fail-pass/stuck and pass/None/stuck do not.
- **(5) The stop reason was re-derived in the orchestrator** (simplification): `apply_convergence` now returns `no_progress_reason` (`carried_repeats` | `all_dropped`), and the orchestrator renders the note from a fixed mapping (`_NO_PROGRESS_NOTES`) — the classifier owns which case fired, so a future third branch can't have its note name the wrong reason. `test_review14_the_stop_reason_comes_from_the_classifier`.
- **(6) `_is_ancestor` bypassed the git helper and had no timeout** (reuse): new `factory.gates.git_rc` — hardened like `git` but rc-returning (never raises; the "no" answer must be representable), timeout-bounded (124 on expiry, so a stalled object fetch cannot hang a round). The orchestrator's local subprocess copy is deleted. `test_review15_git_rc_returns_the_code_without_raising`: 0 / 1 / fatal all come back as codes.
- **(7) Dropped finding text entered the PR body unfenced** (security): `_validator_section` whitespace-collapses every reviewer-controlled field (`_flat`) before it enters the markdown — a newline in a finding's text can no longer start a `## ` line, which could either forge a duplicate section heading (`check_pr_body` refusing the body after a passing round) or plant spoofed merge-risk text in front of the human. Inside the JSON fence the same text was always safe (newlines escaped). `test_review16_dropped_finding_text_cannot_inject_pr_headings`: a dropped finding whose text carries `\n## Merge Danger\n**Door:** …` still opens the PR, with exactly one Merge Danger heading (red: `publish_refused`).
- **(8) Fileless findings bypassed delta scoping entirely** (correctness): a fileless finding now keys by its acceptance-item references when it quotes any (`AC-2 has no test` ≡ `AC-2 is still untested` — same identity however re-worded), and a fileless finding with **no** AC anchor is subject to the delta drop rule like any out-of-delta finding (needs `missed_earlier`). The contract-level channel the round-1 fix protected stays open — anchored findings are never dropped and carried blocking still fails the round — while the re-worded unanchored bounce ("error handling in parser is weak", fresh key each round, never carried) now drops or stops instead of running to exhaustion. `test_review17_fileless_findings_anchor_or_justify`: AC-anchored keying, unanchored drop, justified stand, round-one exemption, and the integration stop.
- **(9) The F-10 retry test was rescripted fileless to dodge the new rule** (test coverage): `test_reviewer_fail_is_retried_then_exhausted` now has the implementer write different (still green) content each round — a real delta — and the findings name `impl.py`: the common file-named retry path is exercised as such. Verified red under the round-2 l2 rules with the old stub (identical content → empty delta → new findings dropped → the all-dropped stop) before the rework.
- **Also changed:** `reviewer.md` documents the anchoring rule (fileless findings must quote the AC id or carry `missed_earlier` in fix rounds; pin `3a42c5a1…`), and its carried-list wording no longer assumes a delta section exists.
- **Evidence (round 2):** 8 review tests red against `116e16b`; after the fixes 25 F-23 tests / **414 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-proven on the round-2 head `95f19d3` (scratch clone, base `9fe41ff`, node_modules-padded, unconfined): verdict `pass` — red exit 4 (collection) on base, green exit 0 on head, all four contract refs.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** unchanged — `done` once this PR's CI is green and you merge it.

### F-23 owner review round 3 (PR #35, head `95f19d3`; 9 findings — AC anchoring reverted per the owner's ruling, all fixed red-first under `test_review18`–`test_review23` + the rewritten `test_review17` and tightened `test_review7`)

The owner's mid-turn advice to revert the anchoring before push arrived after `95f19d3` had already been committed and pushed in one command; this round reverts it exactly as ruled. Their words govern the semantics now: findings with no file are never dropped and key by their wording; an in-run repeat of one is an attempt in progress, so a stuck fileless thread runs out at `attempts_exhausted` instead of stopping early. If contract anchoring is ever wanted, it belongs in the verdict schema as an explicit `ac` field validated at parse time — not a regex over prose.

- **(1) Same-AC different-defect repeats carried → stop** (correctness, reverted): `finding_key` keys every finding by (file, axis, wording) again — "AC-2's test asserts the wrong value" after "AC-2 has no test" is a new thread. `test_review18_a_different_defect_on_the_same_ac_is_not_a_carry`: 3 rounds → `attempts_exhausted` (red on `95f19d3`: `review_not_converging` after 2).
- **(2) Unanchored fileless blocker dropped → declared pass opened a PR** (correctness, reverted): fileless findings are never dropped, restoring round-1 finding 7. `test_review19_an_unanchored_fileless_blocker_does_not_flip_a_pass`: "the new endpoint has no auth check" (no file, no AC id) + declared pass → round fails, no PR (red on `95f19d3`: PR opened). `test_review7`'s fixture no longer leans on "AC-1" in its text — the owner's point that it was hiding this bug is now the test's own comment.
- **(3) Mentioning any AC id bypassed the drop** (correctness, reverted): `_AC_REF` is gone; an AC id in prose is just wording. `test_review20_mentioning_an_ac_changes_nothing`: "Unrelated to AC-3, the parser is weak" keys differently from "AC-3 is untested" and both stand in a delta round.
- **(4) Key-scheme change orphaned logged cross-run keys** (correctness, moot + noted): wording keys are restored, and `95f19d3` never ran outside tests (no unattended loop exists before F-28), so no production progress log ever carried an AC key — nothing to orphan. `test_review17`'s unit half pins wording keying for fileless (same wording ⇒ same key across re-parse; different wording ⇒ different thread).
- **(5) Anchoring inferred by regex over prose** (altitude, reverted): with the anchoring gone there is no inference; the schema-field route is recorded above and in `finding_key`'s docstring for whenever anchoring is revisited.
- **(6) An L0/L1-failed round dropped the last review's findings from the fix list** (correctness): `_fix_findings` now substitutes `last_valid_l2` whenever this round produced no verdict of its own — invalid review **or L2 never ran** — so the fix list and the reviewer's carried list describe the same findings in both gaps. `test_review21_an_l0l1_failed_round_keeps_the_open_l2_findings`: round 2 breaks the contract test (L1 fail, no review); round 3's fix section still carries round 1's open finding (red on `95f19d3`).
- **(7) An unreviewed round was hidden from the next implementer** (correctness): a round whose review session ran but produced no verdict prepends an explicit line to the fix list: "[L2 unreviewed] this round's review produced no verdict — the change was not reviewed; do not assume it was accepted." `test_review22_an_unreviewed_round_is_announced_to_the_implementer` (red on `95f19d3`: the fix block replayed round 1's findings with no sign round 2 went unreviewed).
- **(8) `include_dropped` check inside the dropped loop** (simplification): the loop is now guarded once (`if not include_dropped: continue` before it) — shape only, no behaviour change.
- **(9) Flattened dropped text still rendered live markdown/HTML** (security): the dropped list now rides inside a ```text fence — the same treatment the implementer's notes get. Flattening stays (a newline inside a fence could close it); the fence kills inline HTML and links (`<!--` could hide the JSON report and How to revert in GitHub's rendered view while `check_pr_body`'s raw regex passed; `[see fix](https://evil)` planted a link in front of the merger). `test_review23_dropped_text_is_fenced_not_just_flattened`: a `<!--` + link payload opens the PR with the payload visible as literal text inside the fence and exactly one How-to-revert heading (red on `95f19d3`: unfenced).
- **Also changed:** `reviewer.md`'s fix-round rule restored to the round-1 wording ("a finding that names no file is never dropped"); its pin is `dbab8437…` — byte-identical to the pre-anchoring state, a clean revert marker. `test_review17` rewritten from anchor-or-justify to stand-and-key-by-wording (never dropped anchored or not; in-run same-wording repeat → `new`, cross-run → `carried`; a stuck fileless thread runs out at `attempts_exhausted`).
- **Evidence (round 3):** 8 tests red against `95f19d3` (review7 with its unanchored fixture among them — proving the owner's point that the old fixture hid finding 2); after the fixes 31 F-23 tests / **420 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-proven on the round-3 head (scratch clone, base `9fe41ff`, node_modules-padded, unconfined): verdict `pass` — red exit 4 on base, green exit 0 on head, all four contract refs.

## F-25 — Sensitive-path sentinels

- **Commits:** `feat/f25-sensitive-path-sentinels` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.sentinels` module before implementation; the escalation enum additionally proven red against the pre-change schema (`ContractError … 'sentinel_failed' is not one of […]`).
- **Built:** 2026-10-21. Interactive session, after a full re-read of the repo (ledger, CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER, l0/gates/prbody/orchestrator, both schemas, TASK-CONTRACT, VALIDATOR-SPEC) per the owner's instruction to understand the project completely before starting.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/sentinels.py` — the standing built-in set is `BUILTIN_SENTINELS = (assertion_removed, credential_added, lint_suppression_added, skip_marker_added)`, sorted for deterministic result order. Two of the four predate F-25: `skip_marker_added`/`assertion_removed` stay where they are (line heuristics inside `l0.check_diff_policy` since F-07); F-25 names the set, adds the two new scanners, and records one result per sentinel. `credential_added` matches `_CREDENTIAL_SHAPES` (private-key block, AWS key id, GitHub/GitLab/Slack/Google/Stripe/OpenAI token prefixes, a generic secret assignment) on added lines in ANY file — and the finding text names the matched shape only, never the line: findings ride into reports, prompts and PR bodies, so the sentinel must not become the leak it exists to catch (pinned by a dedicated no-echo test). `lint_suppression_added` matches `# noqa`, `type: ignore`, `@ts-ignore`, eslint/ruff/flake8/pylint disables on added lines in files under a `paths.risk` glob — the same comment elsewhere is ordinary review territory. `run_project_sentinels` executes `sentinels.<risk flag>` commands (schema-validated: risk-flag keys, `{command, description}` entries) only for flags whose globs the diff touches (renames count via their old path too), through the same runner L0 passes the gate commands (the sandboxed one in production); a failure FAILs the verdict with finding `sentinel_failed` and escalates `sentinel_failed`; untouched flags record `skipped` — the human sees what was checked and what deliberately wasn't. `run_sentinels` assembles the full pass: built-in results derived from `check_diff_policy`'s findings plus the new scans, then the project results; `L0Report.sentinel_results` carries them (`to_dict` included) and `run_l0` runs the pass right after the diff policy, before the gate commands. `sentinel_failed` joined the schema's escalation enum and TASK-CONTRACT.md's escalation-reasons line in the same change, with the owner-action paragraph (the F-10 review7 sweep forced the doc, as designed); `paths.risk` keeps its shape. The attempt schema gained a `sentinels` array (name/kind/verdict/risk_flag/detail — the exact `sentinel_results` shape), and the orchestrator stamps it on the pass, fail and L0-escalated attempt records; `_L0_ESCALATION_ORDER` ranks `sentinel_failed` just under `protected_path_touched`. `prbody` gained a required **Sentinels** section (after Evidence) rendering every result pass/fail/skipped from the L0 report — project `detail` cells are flattened and pipe-escaped like the dropped-findings lines (command output is not orchestrator text). `CREDENTIAL_PATTERNS` is exposed as the reusable shape list for F-30's trace redaction. VALIDATOR-SPEC's L0 section documents the sentinel pass; TASK-CONTRACT §5 documents the `sentinels` key.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f25.py::test_ac_1` — through the real `run_l0`: a credential-shaped AWS key added in a non-test file → FAIL, finding `credential_added` naming the file; `@pytest.mark.skip` added on an existing test → `skip_marker_added`; an assertion removed without replacement → `assertion_removed`; `# noqa` on a diff-changed line under a risk glob → `lint_suppression_added` — and the same suppression outside every risk path stays quiet (verdict pass). Each case also asserts the sentinel's result entry is `fail`.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — schema: `load_profile` accepts `sentinels: {payments: [{command, description}]}` and refuses a non-risk-flag key, a missing `description`, and a non-string `command`. Behaviour: with a `touch sentinel-ran && exit 3` sentinel configured, a diff outside the flag's globs passes with the sentinel recorded `skipped` and no marker file left (it never executed); a diff touching `src/pay/**` FAILs with finding `sentinel_failed`, the escalation in the report, and the failing result carrying the risk flag.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — parametrized over `BUILTIN_SENTINELS` (with an equality assertion so a new sentinel cannot join the set without fixtures): each has a seeded fixture diff it flags (verdict fail, finding id = the sentinel's name, result `fail`) and a clean diff it passes (verdict pass, result `pass`).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — unit half: `build_pr_body` renders a `## Sentinels` section listing every result pass/fail/skipped with the failing detail visible; `check_pr_body` accepts the body and refuses it with the section stripped. End-to-end half (real repo/remote/clone/queue/L0/L1, stub seats): a passing run's attempt record carries all four built-in results `pass` and the PR body carries the section; a run whose diff touches `payments` with a failing project sentinel escalates `sentinel_failed` to `needs_human`, and the attempt's `sentinels` field carries the project entry with verdict `fail` and the risk flag — schema-validated on append, so the field's shape is proven against the real gate.
- **Extra coverage:** credential shapes flag known token/key/private-key forms and ignore prose, short values and env lookups — plus the no-echo invariant (none of the planted secrets appears in any finding text); lint-suppression pattern breadth (noqa, type: ignore, eslint-disable, ruff: noqa) inside risk paths and silence outside; `matched_risk_flags` counts a rename's old path; the escalation enum carries `sentinel_failed` (the review7 sweep holds the TASK-CONTRACT side); `to_dict` serialises `sentinel_results`.
- **Changed existing tests (behaviour changed on purpose):** none — F-07's diff-policy tests, F-10's PR-body and orchestrator suites, the F-09 prompt pins and the F-24 rehearsal all pass unchanged (no seat prompt, baseline or rehearsal fixture was touched).
- **Evidence:** 9 F-25 tests / **429 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-25 owner review round (PR #37; 10 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review10`)

All nine reproducible findings were red against `152fccb` for the reported reasons before any fix (review6 after its fixture was corrected — the first cut asserted a string that also appears in the sentinel's *command*, which the finding already echoed, so it passed vacuously; the output-only variant is the real proof). Review10 (the requested enum-equality guard) was green on arrival, as a regression guard should be.

- **(1) Project sentinels ran before setup** (correctness): `run_l0` now runs setup explicitly first; the project sentinel commands execute only after setup succeeds, and when setup fails they are recorded `skipped` ("setup failed; sentinel not run") with the remaining gates still skipped. `test_review1_project_sentinels_run_after_setup`: a sentinel reading a file setup creates passes (was: spurious `sentinel_failed` escalation), and the setup-failure shape records skipped with no sentinel escalation.
- **(2) Lint-suppression finding echoed a secret-bearing line** (security): when a line matches both a credential shape and a suppression pattern, the `lint_suppression_added` finding names the shape and does not echo the line — the same no-echo rule `credential_added` has. `test_review2_lint_finding_never_echoes_a_credential_line`: `STRIPE_KEY = "sk_live_…"  # noqa` under a payments glob produces BOTH findings, and the secret appears nowhere in the serialised report.
- **(3) Sentinel output tail stored unredacted** (security): new `redact_credentials(text)` (every `CREDENTIAL_PATTERNS` match → `[redacted]`) scrubs the output tail before it reaches the finding text or the result `detail` — and the command echo in the finding is redacted too (a command string can itself carry a credential). `test_review3_sentinel_output_is_redacted`: a sentinel whose output prints a live-shaped key stores `[redacted]`, secret nowhere in the report.
- **(4) A committed credential was retryable** (correctness, the owner's design question — resolved as they ruled): `credential_added` now ESCALATES `credential_added` (new enum value, added to the schema and TASK-CONTRACT's escalation line in the same change with the owner-action paragraph: rotate the credential — removing the line cannot un-leak the pushed history; no implementer retry). It leads `_L0_ESCALATION_ORDER` (most severe: a leaked secret needs rotation regardless of this repo). `test_review4_a_committed_credential_escalates_with_no_retry`: L0 half (escalation on the report), schema half (enum), orchestrator half (one attempt, `needs_human`/`credential_added`, no PR, the fail result on the attempt).
- **(5) A sentinel under a flag with no risk globs was skipped forever** (correctness): new `_check_profile_semantics` — `load_profile` refuses a `sentinels.<flag>` whose flag has no `paths.risk` globs (absent or empty), naming the flag and the fix. `test_review5_sentinel_flag_without_risk_globs_is_refused_at_load` covers both shapes.
- **(6) The failing-sentinel finding omitted its output** (test coverage): the finding text now carries the (redacted) output tail on a second line. `test_review6_failing_sentinel_finding_carries_its_output` asserts a string that exists only in the command's output, never in the command string.
- **(7) Risk-flag matching computed three-plus ways** (efficiency): `matched_risk_flags` is THE one implementation; `check_diff_policy` takes the precomputed set (and returns it — 4-tuple now, the only caller is `run_l0`) and its inline flag loop is gone; the builtin pass and project sentinels take it as a parameter. `test_review7_risk_flags_are_computed_once`: monkeypatch-count proves exactly one computation per `run_l0`, and the 4-tuple + a copied-in file ('C') matching its flag pin the unified semantics.
- **(8) The built-in set was split across two modules** (altitude): all four scanners (skip-marker, assertion, credential, lint-suppression) live in `factory.sentinels.builtin_sentinel_findings`; `check_diff_policy` no longer scans test-file lines at all, so a finding-id rename in l0 can no longer orphan a sentinel result. `test_review8_builtin_scanners_live_in_sentinels`: the diff policy emits none of the four on a skip+assertion diff; the sentinel scanner emits both.
- **(9) Terminal escalated attempts dropped sentinel results** (correctness): the `attempts_exhausted` and `review_not_converging` attempt records now carry `sentinels=` (None-safe when no L0 ran). `test_review9_terminal_attempts_carry_sentinel_results`: end-to-end, a 3-round new-findings-per-round run to `attempts_exhausted` and a 2-round converging stop both record the full builtin result set on the terminal attempt.
- **(10) The risk-flag enum is copied by hand across schemas** (simplification): `test_review10_risk_flag_enums_match_across_schemas` pins `tasks.schema.json`'s `risk_flag` enum equal to `project.schema.json`'s `paths.risk` and `sentinels` propertyNames enums — the mechanical rule as a test, per CLAUDE.md. (Green on arrival by construction; it exists to catch the next flag's first edit.)
- **Changed existing tests (fixture honesty, not behaviour):** `test_ac_2`'s valid-profile fixture gained `paths.risk` payments globs — the new load check (finding 5) correctly refused the old fixture, whose sentinel key had no globs; the three invalid shapes it asserts are unchanged. `test_credential_patterns_flag_known_shapes_and_ignores_prose` unchanged and still green (its clean lines match no shape).
- **Also changed:** VALIDATOR-SPEC's L0 sentinel bullets (after-setup ordering, credential escalation, output redaction, load-time refusal) and TASK-CONTRACT §5's `sentinels` row; the orchestrator docstring's escalation sentence names the credential class.
- **Evidence (review round):** 9 review tests red against `152fccb` (review10 green-as-guard); after the fixes 19 F-25 tests / **439 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox), ruff clean.

### F-25 owner review round 2 (PR #37, head `caf2786`; 1 leak + 3 smaller findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review11`–`test_review13`)

All three reproducible findings were red against `caf2786` in a scratch worktree before any fix (review11 after its fixture was corrected: the first cut glued the key directly onto the filler run, making one giant token the AKIA shape legitimately cannot match — a detector false-negative, not the cut-order bug; the owner's scenario is a delimited key the cut lands inside, and that is what the fixture now builds). The fourth item (the pushed-history claim) is a documentation error with no behaviour to test; the two notes are recorded below.

- **(1) Redaction ran after the cut and leaked key fragments** (security, reproduced by the owner): `redact_credentials` now runs on the WHOLE sentinel output before the 200-char tail is taken. The old order kept e.g. `NN7EXAMPLE ZZZ…` — a key fragment no shape matches, leaked into the finding and the result detail. `test_review11_redaction_happens_before_the_output_cut`: a 210-char output whose key straddles the −200 boundary stores `[redacted]`, no fragment in the serialised report.
- **(2) "Stays in the pushed history" was factually wrong** (correctness of the record): the branch is pushed only after a passing round (push lives in the `passed` block), so a credential found by L0 sits in the run branch's LOCAL history only. Escalating is still right — a passing retry would push that earlier commit — and rotating is still right (the model provider saw the token). But no history rewrite is needed: each run clones the default branch fresh, so re-queueing discards the run branch. Corrected in TASK-CONTRACT's `credential_added` paragraph and §5 row, VALIDATOR-SPEC's L0 bullet, and the orchestrator's escalation-order comment and docstring. The paragraph also now states the false-positive cost explicitly (round-2 note 2): a heuristic miss costs exactly this escalation — `needs_human`, no retry, a human confirms and re-queues — erring protective is the intent.
- **(3) Copies' source paths raised risk flags they never raised before** (unintended behaviour change): `matched_risk_flags` now counts `old_path` only for renames (`status == "R"`), restoring the pre-F-25 event-split semantics — a copy matches by its new path alone, so copying out of `src/pay` no longer escalates `risk_path_touched`. `test_review12_copied_source_paths_do_not_touch_risk_flags`: copy-out matches nothing (unit and through `check_diff_policy` — no escalation), copy-in and rename-away still match (both pinned).
- **(4) The no-echo rule covered only lint findings** (security): every echoed line in `builtin_sentinel_findings` — skip markers, removed assertions, lint suppressions — now goes through `redact_credentials` (the lint finding's shape-name-only special case is gone; uniform redaction subsumes it). `test_review13_skip_marker_findings_redact_their_line_echo`: `@pytest.mark.skip(reason="ghp_…")` produces both findings, the token appears nowhere, `[redacted]` does.
- **Note 1 (dead parameter, fixed):** `builtin_sentinel_pass` no longer takes `findings` — after round-1 finding 8, `check_diff_policy` produces none of the built-in ids, so the parameter did nothing. Signature is `(profile, changes, matched)`; no behaviour change, no dedicated test.
- **Note 3 (recorded, deferred to F-30 per the owner):** the L0 gate `command_failed` findings still echo up to 500 chars of raw command output (l0.py, both the no-parsable-location and marker paths) — pre-F-25 behaviour and the same leak class as round-1 finding 3. The owner scoped it to F-30 (trace redaction), where `sentinels.redact_credentials` is the ready-made scrubber; it is recorded here so the deferral is a decision, not an oversight.
- **Evidence (round 2):** 3 review tests red against `caf2786` (scratch worktree); after the fixes 22 F-25 tests / **442 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox), ruff clean.

## F-26 — Merges in order

- **Commits:** `feat/f26-merges-in-order` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.mergeorder` module; the escalation enum additionally proven red against the pre-change schema (`ContractError … 'stale_base' is not one of […]`) and the `in_review -> needs_human` edge proven illegal in the pre-change table (`illegal transition in_review -> needs_human; allowed from in_review: deploying, draft, in_progress`).
- **Built:** 2026-10-21. Interactive session, after a full re-read (ledger, CLAUDE.md, the F-26 WO with its #29 branch-protection amendment, orchestrator/state/publish/contract/runs/rehearsal, both schemas, TASK-CONTRACT §3/§5, VALIDATOR-SPEC, the f10/f23/f25 harnesses, open PRs and worktrees) per the owner's understand-first directive; branched from `origin/main` (`b5742f3`, F-25 reconciled) after the owner's mid-turn correction that this checkout's F-25 `draft` was a stale-branch artifact.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/mergeorder.py` — `Protection(checked, strict, plan_limit, detail)` classifies one `gh api repos/<slug>/branches/<default>/protection` answer (`classify_protection`, pure, unit-tested on canned gh output; `gh_branch_protection` is the real seam, gh resolved like `gh_pr_create`); `admission(profile, protection=…)` admits a strictly-protected repo, admits GitHub's plan-limit 403 **only** with `repo.branch_protection: unavailable` declared, and refuses every other answer naming the missing setting — raised as `MergeOrderError(ContractError)`, so the CLI's existing exit-2 handling needed no new wiring. `run_once` calls it after config load, **before the queue is read**: a refusal starts no session, clones nothing, spends nothing. The stale-base sweep (`_sweep_stale_prs`) runs on every `factory run` for each task `in_review` with PR and branch links: **full** (not blob-filtered — the scratch clone's lazy blob fetches go to the local source, which holds neither the moved default head nor the pushed PR branch) fetches of the remote default head and the PR branch; up to date (`merge-base --is-ancestor`) → nothing to do; stale → a local `git merge` onto the new base, then L0 and L1 on a clean clone of the merged tree under the same sandboxed gate runner a round uses — a local proof of the merge a strict setting (or the unavailable self-enforcement) would force before it happens. Failure (merge conflict, L0/L1 fail or escalate) appends an attempt (`outcome: escalated`, `escalation: stale_base`, rounds/turns/tokens 0 — no session ran, and no implementer budget spent) and moves the task `in_review -> needs_human` (new legal edge, reason "stale base failed re-validation on the new default head (F-26)"); a pass records the evidence in the progress log and the `<run-id>-revalidate.json` report and leaves the task `in_review` — readiness now proven against the current default head, with **no attempt appended** so a later fix round keeps its full budget. AC-2: after pick and the peak check, open factory PRs (queue truth: `in_review` + `links.pr`) at or above `repo.concurrency` (schema-validated, default 1) return `RunResult("concurrency_wait")` — the ready task untouched, no session, no attempt; `loop` stops on the same-named reason (CLI exit 0). The sweep runs before pick so an escalated stale PR stops the run even with an empty ready queue, and the concurrency count is taken after the sweep (a just-escalated PR frees its slot).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f26.py::test_ac_1` — admission matrix: strict passes (declaration irrelevant); protected-without-strict, protected-without-checks and not-protected each refuse naming the setting, **with and without** the declaration (it never excuses a repo that could have protection); the plan-limit 403 admits only with `repo.branch_protection: unavailable`, and without it the refusal names the declaration; any other gh answer refuses. Schema halves: `repo.branch_protection` (enum `["unavailable"]`) and `repo.concurrency` (integer ≥ 1) validate, and garbage values (`"enabled"`, `1`, `None`, `0`, `-1`, `"2"`, `1.5`) refuse at load. End to end: a not-strict repo raises before the queue is read — task still `ready`, no PR, no session ran.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — after a PR opens (T-1 `in_review`), the default profile leaves T-2 waiting: outcome `concurrency_wait`, no seat calls, T-2 still `ready` with no attempts, still one PR; the loop stops `concurrency_wait`; with `concurrency: 2` in the profile the second PR opens and both tasks sit `in_review`.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — end to end on the real fixture remote through the real sandbox: up to date → the sweep is a no-op (no attempt, no log entry); a clean remote advance (unrelated file) → re-validation runs the real L0+L1 on the merged tree, passes, and the task stays `in_review` with the pass recorded (attempts unchanged); a conflicting advance → `needs_human`/`stale_base` with the attempt's note naming the conflict and `links.branch` intact. The schema enum carries `stale_base` (red-first) and the new edge is legal while `in_review -> awaiting_owner` stays illegal.
- **Extra coverage:** a conflict-free merge whose merged tree fails the gates (the remote adds a test demanding a different answer) also escalates `stale_base` — the re-validation runs the gates, not just git-merge; the classifier matrix on canned gh output (strict / lax / null checks / 404 / plan-limit 403 / other 403); `gh_branch_protection` builds `api repos/<slug>/branches/<b>/protection` and refuses a non-GitHub remote.
- **Changed existing tests (behaviour changed on purpose):** (1) test_f03's ILLEGAL_EDGE_CLASSES loses `("in_review", "needs_human")` with a comment — the F-26 edge makes it legal; the "lateral" class keeps its other representatives. (2) test_f10 AC-5's grep drops its fourth pattern (`["']merge["']\s*,\s*["']--`) with a comment — it over-matched the sweep's LOCAL `git merge --no-edit` on a scratch clone (the simulation this AC exists to allow); the three remaining patterns still catch every real PR-merge mechanism (shell form, argv form, REST endpoint), and the factory still never merges. (3) Fixture honesty: f10's `Env.run`/`Env.loop`, its two direct `run_once` calls, the killed-run subprocess script, the f23 harness, the f25 `Env.run`, and `rehearsal.run_rehearsal` all inject a strict-protected `Protection` — the fixture remotes are local paths the real gh seam cannot answer for.
- **Also changed:** TASK-CONTRACT (the escalation line, a `stale_base` owner paragraph — update the branch or reject the PR; the report names the failed step — the §3 edge row, and §5's `repo` row documenting `branch_protection`/`concurrency`) and VALIDATOR-SPEC ("Rounds and escalation" gains the merge-order paragraph) — the review7 sweep held the doc side, as designed. `RunResult`/`LoopResult` docstrings and the CLI `_EXIT` map carry `concurrency_wait` (exit 0).
- **Worth knowing (F-28 interaction):** at `concurrency: 1` a merged PR leaves its task `in_review` (nothing mechanizes `in_review -> deploying` before F-14), so the next `factory loop` would wait on a PR that no longer exists. The mechanism F-28's owner flow already has: record the merged WO in this ledger and run `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap`, whose forward chain moves the task out of `in_review` — between dogfood merges. Flagged for the F-28 session; no F-26 change needed.
- **Evidence:** 7 F-26 tests / **449 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` proven on the branch head `7904499` (scratch clone, base `b5742f3`, node_modules-padded head differing from the real one by `node_modules/` paths only, unconfined runner): verdict `pass` — red on base for all three contract refs (`red_reason: unknown`), green on head, no findings.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-26 owner review round (PR #40; 10 findings — 9 fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review10`, 1 a comment fix)

All nine reproducible findings were red against `2569940` before any fix (review1–3 via a `pr_state` seam the orchestrator didn't have yet — TypeError/RunError/wrong outcome; review4 on the missing `links.revalidated`; review5 on the sweep running inside the window; review6 on the missing classifier; review7 as the reported IndexError at `_slice_id_of`; review9/10 on the admission and 404 messages). Findings 1–3 share one root fix, as the owner suggested: **the sweep and the open-PR limit now ask GitHub whether each PR is still open** (`factory.mergeorder.gh_pr_state`, `gh pr view <url> --json state`; injectable `pr_state` seam on `run_once`, faked in every harness alongside `protection`).

- **(1) A merged PR was escalated stale_base** (correctness): the sweep skips any PR whose GitHub state isn't `open` — a squash-merged PR (task still `in_review` until reconcile) is never re-validated; L1's red-first proof would fail on code that already landed. `test_review1_a_merged_pr_is_never_revalidated`: `pr_state → merged` + moved main → run returns `idle`, task stays `in_review`, no attempt, no log entry.
- **(2) A deleted branch wedged every run** (correctness): the state gate runs before any fetch, so the auto-deleted head branch of a merged PR is never fetched. `test_review2_a_deleted_branch_after_merge_does_not_wedge`: in_review task pointing at a branch that was never pushed, `pr_state → merged` → `idle`, no `RunError`. (An *open* PR's branch is never deleted on GitHub — the PR closes with it — so a fetch failure there stays a `RunError`: genuinely unknown state.)
- **(3) The open-PR limit counted queue statuses** (correctness): `_count_open_prs` counts PRs GitHub reports open across `ready`/`in_progress`/`in_review`/`needs_human` — an escalated task's still-open PR holds its slot; a merged/closed PR frees it before reconcile. `test_review3_the_open_pr_limit_counts_open_prs`: after a stale escalation with the PR left open, the next run returns `concurrency_wait` (was: opened a second PR); with `pr_state → merged` the slot frees and the next PR opens.
- **(4) The sweep re-ran everything with no record** (efficiency): a pass stamps `links.revalidated = {pr, default}` (new schema field, both shas) and the sweep skips an unchanged pair — no second L0/L1, no duplicate log line. `test_review4_revalidation_is_cached_per_base`: second run over an unchanged base adds no log entry; a further main move re-validates (the cache no longer matches).
- **(5) The sweep ran inside the peak window** (correctness): `run_once` now checks the window before the sweep — no sandboxed gate work, no escalations from a run that starts nothing. `test_review5_the_peak_window_skips_the_sweep`: a stale open PR + Monday 09:30 → `peak_window`, log unchanged. (Semantic note: an idle queue during the window now reports `peak_window` rather than `idle` — the window is checked before the pick-None return; loop stops either way.)
- **(6) Every merge failure read as a conflict** (correctness): `_merge_is_conflict(stdout, stderr)` — git prints the conflict report to **stdout** (probed against real git; the first cut read only stderr and broke the conflict e2e tests) — a non-conflict failure raises `RunError` naming it infrastructure, never `stale_base`. `test_review6_merge_failure_classification`: conflict text → conflict; bad object / missing blob / unrelated histories → not.
- **(7) `_slice_id_of` crashed on a task without attempts** (correctness): empty `attempts` now falls back to the task id. `test_review7_escalation_survives_a_task_without_attempts`: a hand-reconciled in_review task (no attempts, manually pushed branch, conflicting main) escalates cleanly with `slice_id` = the task id.
- **(8) Stale `LoopResult.stop_reason` comment** (conventions): the inline comment now lists all six reasons, matching the docstring.
- **(9) The 404 case lost the "does not excuse" note** (correctness): the suffix is appended whenever `branch_protection: unavailable` was declared and refused — including 404. `test_review9_not_protected_with_declaration_names_the_excuse`.
- **(10) Every 404 read as "protection is off"** (correctness): the 404 detail now names all three causes — protection off, repo/branch does not exist, or the token cannot see the repo. `test_review10_a_404_names_the_other_causes`.
- **Also changed:** `gh_pr_state` joined `factory.mergeorder` (with `PublishError` hoisted to the module imports); `tasks.schema.json` links gained the `revalidated` cache field; the f26/f10/f23/f25 harnesses and `rehearsal.py` default the `pr_state` fake to `"open"`; TASK-CONTRACT's `stale_base` paragraph and VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph document the open-PR gate, the cache, and the peak-window ordering; `run_once`'s docstring documents the `pr_state` seam. **The ledger's earlier "no F-26 change needed" note on the F-28 interaction is superseded by this round** — the state-gated sweep covers the merge-then-reconcile flow directly, and reconcile remains the way a merged PR's task leaves `in_review`.
- **Evidence (review round):** 9 review tests red against `2569940`; after the fixes 16 F-26 tests / **458 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean.

### F-26 owner review round 2 (PR #40; 8 findings — all fixed, 8 red-first under `test_review11`–`test_review17` + the rewritten `test_review6`)

All eight were red against `a76a8d3` before any fix (review11–14/17 on outcomes, review15 on the double gh call, review16 on the clone the cache still paid, review6 rewritten onto the missing `_conflict_paths`). The two blocking findings and six smaller ones share one rework of `run_once`'s ordering: **pick → idle → peak-window → resolve PR states once per run → sweep → concurrency count**.

- **(1) A rejected PR's fix round filled its own slot** (correctness, blocking): the count excludes the picked task's own `links.pr` — a fix round reuses its rejected PR's slot, so counting it deadlocked the task on itself. `test_review11_a_fix_round_does_not_fill_its_own_slot`: PR opened → rejected (`in_review→in_progress→ready`, link kept, PR open) → the next run opens the fix PR (was: `concurrency_wait` forever).
- **(2) Conflict detection was English-only** (correctness, blocking): conflicts are now read **structurally** — `_conflict_paths(repo)` = `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U` (the index, not git's translated messages) — and the merge subprocess runs with `LC_ALL=C`/`LANG=C` for deterministic note text. No unmerged paths + non-zero merge → `RunError` (infrastructure), never `stale_base`. `test_review12` (e2e, spying the merge env) + the rewritten `test_review6` (real conflicted repo lists `f`).
- **(3) The conflict note quoted an empty stderr** (correctness): the note now names the conflicting files from the index (`conflicts in: impl.py`) plus the LC_ALL=C stdout tail — never `()`. Asserted in `test_review12`.
- **(4) One unresolvable PR link aborted every run** (correctness): `_resolve_pr_states(urls, pr_state)` catches `PublishError` per URL → `None`. `None` is distinct from every GitHub answer: the count ignores it (a rotten link on an old task must not wedge the queue; a live gh outage stops the run at `gh_pr_create` anyway) and the sweep skips the task (readiness isn't judged on an unknown). `test_review13`: an escalated task's link rots (gh 404s on it) → the unrelated ready task runs to `pr_opened`, nothing raises.
- **(5) A closed-unmerged PR stranded its task silently** (correctness): the sweep escalates a GitHub-`closed` PR as **`pr_closed`** (new escalation: schema enum + TASK-CONTRACT line + owner paragraph in one change, review7 sweep held) — `in_review → needs_human`, attempt + log entry naming the URL and the owner action (re-open it, or re-queue by re-approving). `test_review14`.
- **(6) PR state was queried twice per PR per run** (efficiency): `_pr_urls` collects every unique URL once; `_resolve_pr_states` makes exactly one `pr_state` call per URL per run, shared by the sweep and the count. `test_review15` (counting fake: each URL exactly once).
- **(7) The cache was checked only after clone + two full fetches** (efficiency): `_ls_remote_heads` (one `git ls-remote refs/heads/<default> refs/heads/<branch>`) decides the cache hit **before** `create_run` — an unchanged `(branch, base)` pair on the remote skips with no clone and no fetch. ls-remote is an optimization seam only: on any failure it answers empty and the clone path (still authoritative) runs. `test_review16` (monkeypatched `create_run`: zero clones on the cached run).
- **(8) An idle queue in the peak window reported `peak_window`** (correctness): `pick is None → idle` comes back **before** the window check — a drained queue is done whatever the clock says (`factory loop` stops `queue_empty`), and the sweep (which lives after the window check) simply never runs on an idle queue. `test_review17` (run: `idle`; loop: `queue_empty`).
- **Changed existing tests (behaviour changed on purpose, per the new ordering):** `test_ac_3` and `test_stale_revalidation_catches_a_broken_merge` ride a second ready task (the sweep now runs on the way to the next pick; their pass/no-op scenarios end `concurrency_wait`, not `idle`); round-1 `test_review1`/`test_review2` assert the merged PR also frees the slot for the next task (`pr_opened`); `test_review4` keeps its third run meaningful under the new order; `test_review5` carries a pickable task (idle queues no longer reach the sweep to skip it); `test_review7` gets a second task for the same reason; `test_review6` rewritten from the text classifier onto `_conflict_paths` (the text form `_merge_is_conflict` is deleted — superseded by the structural check it existed to approximate).
- **Also changed:** `pr_closed` joined the escalation enum with its TASK-CONTRACT paragraph (the F-10 review7 sweep forced the doc side); VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph now states the full ordering (idle before window, one state lookup per PR, ls-remote cache, structural conflicts, `pr_closed`); `run_once`'s docstring and the sweep's document the states dict; `PublishError`/`os` joined the orchestrator imports.
- **CI round (fixture honesty, not a code bug):** the first round-2 push failed `test_review6` on CI only — the fixture's own `git merge` ran WITHOUT identity, and CI's fresh Linux runners have no ambient gitconfig, so git refused with rc=128 "Committer identity unknown" before ever conflicting (no unmerged paths → empty list). The production sweep's merge always carries `_IDENTITY` — which is why every e2e conflict leg passed on the same run. Verified in an OrbStack node:22-bookworm container (rc=128 without identity; with identity rc=1 and `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U` lists `f`, matching macOS). The fixture now mirrors the sweep's invocation (identity + LC_ALL=C) and asserts rc==1 so an identity refusal can never masquerade as a conflict again.
- **Evidence (round 2):** 8 tests red against `a76a8d3`; after the fixes 23 F-26 tests / **465 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean.

### F-26 owner review round 3 (PR #40; 8 findings — all fixed, 8 red-first under `test_review18`–`test_review25`, `test_review13` rewritten)

All eight were red against `3b01720` before any fix. One ordering rework carries the round: **pick → peak-window gate (idle if nothing pickable) → sweep (EVERY off-peak run, with or without a pick) → lazy state resolution for the count → fail-closed count**.

- **(1) The idle return skipped the sweep** (correctness, blocking): the sweep now runs on every off-peak run even when `pick is None` — AC-3's "every run". The round-2 idle semantics survive as the loop-stop shape: inside the peak window a drained queue still returns `idle`/`queue_empty` (no sweep, no gh lookups in-window); off-peak the sweep runs first and the run returns idle only after it. `test_review18` (single task, stale conflict, no pick → `stale_base`) and `test_review19` (closed → `pr_closed`), both red on `3b01720` (returned `idle`).
- **(2) Unknown PR state failed open on the limit** (correctness, blocking): `_count_open_prs` counts a failed lookup (`None`) as open — the limit fails closed (a permission check never fails open). The run itself still raises nothing and aborts nothing (round-2 finding 4's no-crash half, kept): a rotten link yields a clean `concurrency_wait`, not exit 2. `test_review20` (rotten link + rate-limit shape → the picked task waits, no second PR); `test_review13` rewritten to assert the no-crash half only, its "T-2 runs anyway" expectation superseded.
- **(3) ls-remote/fetch could hang on a credential prompt** (correctness): every remote git call — `_ls_remote_heads`, both `_fetch_sha` fetches, the sweep's merge, and `_build_on_remote_default`'s fetch (same class, one line more) — runs with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and a 300s timeout; a fetch timeout raises `RunError` naming the guard, an ls-remote timeout falls through to the clone path (optimization seam). `test_review21` spies every remote git call in a sweep: env + timeout asserted on each.
- **(4) pr_closed used a fixed fake run_id** (correctness): both escalation paths now stamp a real generated id — `_new_run_id()` from `factory.runs` (no session ran, but audit joins must not collide). `test_review22` (two pr_closed escalations: distinct ids, both matching the run-id pattern).
- **(5) The escalation record was copy-pasted** (reuse): `_sweep_escalation` — one builder for the escalated attempt, its progress-log entry and the transition; `pr_closed` and `stale_base` both call it, so a future required attempt field lands in one place. Behavior covered by every existing escalation test.
- **(6) The ls-remote pre-check only skipped cache hits** (efficiency): the up-to-date path now stamps `links.revalidated` too — same pair ⇒ same ancestry — so the common case (open, up-to-date PR) skips at ls-remote with no clone and no fetch on every later tick. `test_review23` (up-to-date run stamps; the next run clones nothing).
- **(7) Every run queried gh for every PR link** (efficiency): state resolution is lazy — an idle tick resolves only `in_review` links; the count's `ready`/`in_progress`/`needs_human` links resolve only once a task is actually picked (and still once per URL per run). `test_review24` (idle tick with a needs_human task holding an old PR link: only the open PR's URL is queried).
- **(8) The documented remedies had no legal transition** (correctness, blocking): two new recovery edges in `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` — `needs_human → in_review` (owner re-opened the escalated PR) and `needs_human → in_progress` (owner rejected it; fix round) — replayed via reconcile like every other human move. TASK-CONTRACT §3 carries both rows and the `stale_base`/`pr_closed` paragraphs now name the reachable remedy (the "re-queue by re-approving" dead end is gone); test_f03's `exit_from_needs_human` illegal list drops the two now-legal edges with a comment (the F-26-round-1 pattern). `test_review25`.
- **Also changed:** `_pr_urls` takes statuses (+exclude); `_resolve_pr_states`' docstring now delegates None's meaning to its consumers; the sweep docstring says "every off-peak run"; VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph rewritten to the final ordering.
- **Evidence (round 3):** 8 tests red against `3b01720`; after the fixes 31 F-26 tests / **473 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean.

### F-26 owner review round 4 (PR #40; 7 findings — all fixed, 8 red-first under `test_review26`–`test_review32` + the rewritten `test_review11`, `test_review25` adapted, test_f03 re-gated)

All seven were red against `cf0aa0e` before any fix (the red split: 9 failed — the 8 new/rewritten F-26 tests plus test_f03's re-gated `exit_from_needs_human` — 86 passing). The round's center of gravity: the recovery edges became real machinery instead of table entries nobody could apply.

- **(1) Recovery edges had no writer** (correctness, blocking): reconcile skips non-draft tasks and hand-editing tasks.json is forbidden, so round 3's "replayed via reconcile" documented an unreachable remedy. The sweep is now the one writer, reading the owner's GitHub actions: a re-opened `pr_closed` PR → `in_review` (re-validated the same run); a closed `stale_base` PR was the rejection → `in_progress`, and the next run's `reclaim_abandoned` re-queues the fix round ready. Idle ticks resolve the recovery-eligible `needs_human` links alongside the `in_review` ones, so the owner's action is readable even when nothing is pickable. `test_review26` (re-open → in_review + `recovered` log entry), `test_review27` (close → in_progress → next run reclaim/pick/fix round, new PR, old one closed).
- **(2) Recovery edges applied to every escalation** (correctness, blocking): the two edges left `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` for a separate `RECOVERY_TRANSITIONS` table gated by `recovery_task_eligible` — last attempt escalated `stale_base`/`pr_closed` **and** `links.pr` held — enforced in `TaskQueue.transition` (the one writer path) and shared verbatim by the sweep's eligibility filter, so the writer can never widen the edges. A `credential_added` task with a PR link can no longer reach `in_review`/`deploying` on a validation that never happened; test_f03's `exit_from_needs_human` illegal list re-gains both edges with a comment. `test_review28` (generic `check_transition` refuses; credential_added+PR gated out; eligible stale_base passes; eligible-but-no-link gated out); `test_review25` rewritten to drive the edges through the queue with real escalations.
- **(3) Fail-closed stall was silent** (correctness, blocking): `RunResult` carries `open_prs`/`unresolved_prs`, `_count_open_prs` returns the two lists (a failed lookup is its own bucket), and the CLI's `_run_extra` names both on a `concurrency_wait` — a scheduled tick that builds nothing now says which links hold the slot. `test_review29` (rotten link: outcome still clean `concurrency_wait`, exit 0 — the URL named in the result and the CLI line).
- **(4) Fix round opened a second PR** (correctness, blocking): round-2 finding 1's exclusion is gone — the picked task's own PR counts like any other while GitHub reports it open, so a fix round waits (naming the PR via finding 3's machinery) until the rejected PR is actually closed, instead of opening a second factory PR and orphaning the first (two open PRs is what AC-2 forbids). `test_review11` rewritten to the superseding behavior (blocked while open, no session, PR named; proceeds and opens the replacement once closed); VALIDATOR-SPEC and the run_once comment record the supersession.
- **(5) New timeouts raised uncaught TimeoutExpired** (correctness): the sweep's merge and `_build_on_remote_default`'s fetch wrap `TimeoutExpired` into `RunError` with the same wording as `_fetch_sha` — a slow remote is the usual exit-2 message, never a traceback. `test_review30` (merge half + build-fetch half; blob-filter distinguishes the build fetch from the sweep's full fetches).
- **(6) Private `_new_run_id` imported across modules** (reuse): `factory.runs.new_run_id()` is the public seam, docstring owning the fact that session-less ids (sweep escalations and recoveries) have no run dir; the private name is gone and the orchestrator imports the public one. `test_review31`.
- **(7) stale_base remedy promised the fix round would use the owner's branch work** (correctness): TASK-CONTRACT rewritten — close (reject) the PR and the sweep queues the fix round, which re-implements from the current default head on a fresh run branch ("work pushed to the old PR branch is not reused"); the unreachable "update the branch" advice and every "replayed via reconcile" claim are gone; the `pr_closed` remedy is re-open only (its fix-round direction was never sweep-reachable — the PR is already closed). `test_review32`.
- **Also changed:** `_pr_urls` lost its `exclude` parameter; the count's pending set is filtered against already-resolved states (still one lookup per URL per run — review15's `{1}` invariant); the `pr_closed` escalation note no longer offers the unreachable fix-round direction; VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph rewritten to the recovery-aware ordering.
- **Evidence (round 4):** 8 tests red against `cf0aa0e` (9 failed incl. test_f03's class, 86 passing); after the fixes 38 F-26 tests / **480 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-proven on the round-4 head `5c02be2` (scratch clone, base `b5742f3`, node_modules padded via `paths.setup_outputs: [node_modules]`, unconfined runner): verdict `pass` on all three F-26 AC refs — red-first on base, green on head.

### F-26 owner review round 5 (PR #40; 6 findings — all fixed, 8 red-first under `test_review33`–`test_review37` + the rewritten `test_review27`, `test_review25`/`test_review28`/test_f03 re-targeted to the ready edge)

All six were red against `c491676` before any fix (the 5 new tests plus the three re-targeted expectations, 8 failed in the red split).

- **(1) Sweep escalations burned the fix-round budget** (correctness, blocking): the attempt count now skips attempts whose `escalation` is `stale_base`/`pr_closed` (`RECOVERY_ESCALATIONS`) — session-less sweep records, not spent work, so a stale_base escalation after a passing round can no longer exhaust the budget before the recovery fix round's session runs. `test_review33` (max_attempts=2: pass + stale_base escalation + closed PR + gated re-queue → the fix round still opens its PR; red on `c491676`: attempts_exhausted with no session).
- **(2) Closing a healthy PR to reject it parked the task** (correctness, blocking): a pr_closed task's PR was already closed at escalation, so GitHub has no signal left to mean "fix round" — the owner's word is the new `factory recover <task-id>` command (`factory/approve.py: recover_task` + CLI wiring), which writes the gated `needs_human → ready` edge (same eligibility gate the sweep's transitions enforce) after printing the escalation, PR link, and the fresh-branch/no-reuse fact; refusals (non-eligible escalation, unknown task) exit 1 with the re-open/contract-change alternatives. The asymmetry with stale_base is documented, not hidden: a stale_base task parks with its PR open (closing it IS the rejection), a pr_closed task parked after the close. `test_review34` (refusal incl. CLI exit 1; recovery → ready; the fix round then runs and opens the replacement PR; TASK-CONTRACT documents `factory recover`).
- **(3) Recovery wrote in_progress without a lease** (altitude): the rejection edge is now `needs_human → ready` (replacing `needs_human → in_progress`, which left the recovery table entirely) — the recovered task is immediately pickable, no lease-less in_progress against the one-writer invariant, no next-run `reclaim_abandoned` hop. `test_review27` rewritten (close → `ready` the same run; the next run picks it directly and opens the fix-round PR).
- **(4) A merged escalated PR stranded its task** (correctness): the recovery pass handles `merged` too — the task goes back `in_review` (the standard merged story; the sweep's re-validation then skips it until reconcile), so shipped code never parks a task (or its dependants) in needs_human. `test_review35` (stale_base + merged → `in_review`, `recovered` log entry naming the merge).
- **(5) The merge timeout blamed the remote** (correctness): the sweep's merge is local (both commits already fetched) — its own `_MERGE_TIMEOUT_SEC` and a RunError naming "a local merge … pathological merge or disk problem, not the network"; `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` kept with a comment (the scratch clone is blobless: an unexpectedly missing blob triggers a lazy fetch from the local source, and prompt-free keeps even that path safe). `test_review36` (message says "local merge", never "credential").
- **(6) The recovery scan ran twice per run** (efficiency): `_recovery_tasks(queue)` is computed once in `run_once` and passed into `_sweep_stale_prs` — one scan, and the URLs resolved are exactly the set swept. `test_review37` (counting wrapper: exactly one call per off-peak run).
- **Also changed:** TASK-CONTRACT recovery rows rewritten (`needs_human → ready` replaces the in_progress row; the in_review row gains the merged case; the `in_review → in_progress` row now describes the pr_closed + recover flow as its writer); the stale_base paragraph gains the merge remedy and the budget-skip sentence; the pr_closed paragraph names all three exits and owns the stale_base/pr_closed asymmetry; VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph updated to match.
- **Evidence (round 5):** 8 tests red against `c491676`; after the fixes 43 F-26 tests / **485 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-proven on the round-5 head `5d6fcf5` (scratch clone, base `b5742f3`, node_modules via `paths.setup_outputs`, unconfined): verdict `pass` on all three F-26 AC refs.

### F-26 owner review round 6 (PR #40; 5 findings — all fixed, 4 red-first under `test_review38`–`test_review41`; the cli layout fix is a comment-class change with no behavioral test)

All four behavioral findings were red against `c385159` before any fix.

- **(1) The merged park promised a reconcile that never comes** (correctness, blocking): nothing advances a merged task from `in_review` (reconcile skips non-draft tasks; `in_review → deploying` is F-14, unbuilt) — TASK-CONTRACT's "reconcile owns it from there" was false. The docs now say the truth: a merged escalation returns to `in_review` and **waits there for the F-14 deploy gate exactly like every merged factory PR**, and the recovery log note names F-14 (`test_review38` asserts the note and the docs greps). Choosing the reviewer's docs option over "add the in_review→deploying move": writing deploying with no L4 machinery would park the task somewhere with fewer exits.
- **(2) Merged parks were gh-polled every run** (efficiency): `links.merged` (new schema boolean) is stamped once — by the recovery's merged branch and by the in_review sweep loop — and the stamp is terminal: `_pr_urls(..., skip_merged=True)` drops the link from the always-resolved set and the count's pending set, `_count_open_prs` never counts a stamped link (so an unresolved stamped link cannot fail the limit closed), and the sweep's open_prs comprehension skips stamped tasks. `test_review39` (merged in_review task: stamped after the first observation; a later tick makes zero pr_state calls and still returns idle).
- **(3) recover accepted a task whose PR was still open** (correctness, blocking): `recover_task` gained a `pr_state` seam (default `gh_pr_state`, resolved lazily so tests/CLI can patch it) and refuses unless GitHub reports the PR **closed** — open would hold the concurrency slot under the queued fix round forever; an unreachable gh fails closed (PublishError → refusal). The message tells the owner to close the PR first (and that closing alone queues the stale_base fix round via the sweep). `test_review40` (refusal on open incl. CLI exit 1; success on closed; TASK-CONTRACT documents the gate); round-5's `test_review34` re-targeted to inject the closed state its CLI half now needs.
- **(4) needs_human → ready skipped the dependency check** (correctness): both fix-round writers route like approval — `blocked` when `unmet_dependencies`, never a silently-unpickable ready. New gated edge `("needs_human", "blocked")` in `RECOVERY_TRANSITIONS` (same eligibility); the sweep's stale_base-closed branch and `recover_task` both compute the target. `test_review41` (dep regressed to draft while parked: sweep → blocked; recover → blocked). Fixture note: `depends_on` rides outside `acceptance_hash`, so hand-setting it post-escalation triggers no contract demotion.
- **(5) recover parser wedged between approve's arguments** (simplification, comment-class): the recover subparser block moved after approve's last argument; approve's `--queue` help restored to `%(default)s` form and recover's written that way (the round-5 insertion had hard-coded the path and split approve's block). No behavioral change; the parser-build import check covers it.
- **Also changed:** `review22` re-targeted (its incidental all-"merged" fake now terminally stamps T-1 by design; the fixture escalates T-1 first, then opens T-2's PR with the slot genuinely free — which the round-4 no-exclusion count requires anyway); VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph updated to the F-14 wait, the terminal stamp, recover's closed-PR gate, and blocked routing.
- **Evidence (round 6):** 4 tests red against `c385159`; after the fixes 47 F-26 tests / **489 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-proven on the round-6 head `21ed3fc` (scratch clone, base `b5742f3`, node_modules via `paths.setup_outputs`, unconfined): verdict `pass` on all three F-26 AC refs.

### F-26 owner review round 7 (PR #40; 2 findings — both fixed red-first under `test_review42`–`test_review44`)

Both findings were red against `c298ae0` before any fix.

- **(1) `links.merged` carried over to the task's next PR** (correctness, blocking): the round-6 stamp was a boolean on the task, and publishing keeps a task's other links (`{**links, "pr": url, "branch": branch}`), so a rebuilt task or a later slice inherited `merged: true` — its new PR was never gh-polled, never counted against the limit and never re-validated: a second open factory PR, the AC-2 breach. `links.merged` now holds the merged PR's **URL** (schema: boolean → string), and every reader goes through `_is_merged(links)` (`links.merged == links.pr`), so a new PR on the task is simply not merged. `test_review42` (T-1's PR 1 merged and stamped; T-1 rebuilt opens PR 2; a new T-2 must `concurrency_wait` naming PR 2, which is polled while PR 1 stays terminal) — red as `pr_opened` (a third PR) with the boolean stamp.
