## F-49 — Contract and importer hardening

CODE-REVIEW-2026-11-05 M-10, m-3, m-4, m-6, m-7, I-18: nothing enforced that acceptance refs resolve, `format: date-time` was annotation-only, the importer truncated silently, raced its fresh write, lost output on deep JSON, and accepted a proposal with no `project`.

**Built (owner-directed; every protected change below was directed in this WO's brief, the F-46/F-47/F-48 precedent):**

- `factory/contract.py` (protected, owner-directed) —
  - `resolve_test_ref(repo, ref) -> str | None` (AC-1, M-10): STATIC resolution via `ast` (never imports the test module) of `path::name` (top-level `def`/`async def`, last top-level binding wins) and `path::Class::name` (direct method of a top-level class); refuses absolute paths, `..` climbs, symlinks resolving outside the repo, padded names, empty parts, >2 name parts, unparsable/non-UTF-8/missing files, and param-id refs (`name[...]`, fail closed, F-46 AC-5's amendment). Padded *paths* strip, exactly as `gates.ref_path`/L1 do. Helper `_top_level_bindings` implements the binding semantics; module docstring gains the F-49 paragraph.
  - AC-2 (m-3): `_validator` (the ONE file-backed validator builder) now passes `Draft202012Validator.FORMAT_CHECKER`; new `_build_validator(schema)` is the same seam for callers holding a schema document. `iter_schema_violations` accepts a schema document (`Path | str | dict`) so the importer's derived proposal schema builds through the one seam.
- `factory/state.py` (protected, owner-directed) — new module-level `queue_writer_lock(queue_path)`: the exclusive flock on `<queue>.lock`, split out of `TaskQueue._exclusive_lock` (which now delegates to it) so the importer's fresh-queue write takes the SAME primitive every other queue writer takes (AC-4, m-6). No second lock file, no parallel path.
- `factory/importer.py` (NOT protected) —
  - AC-3 (m-4): `_list_planning_docs` returns `PlanningDocs(kept, dropped)` (kept = first `MAX_DOC_FILES` sorted, dropped = the exact rest); the prompt's listing ends with `- (+N more planning docs under docs/ exist but are not listed; …)`; `ImportPlan.truncated_docs` (set by `run_import`) renders a "## Planning docs not listed" section in the summary naming every dropped doc; the budget call uses `len(listing.kept)` (docstring invariant kept true: the budget scales with the docs actually listed).
  - AC-4 (m-6): `apply_import`'s fresh branch = validate (outside the lock) → mkdir → `queue_writer_lock(path)` → re-check `path.is_file()` → `_atomic_write_json`. The loser of two concurrent fresh imports refuses with the existing "queue changed since … another writer" message; nothing is overwritten.
  - AC-5 (m-7): `parse_proposal` converts `RecursionError` (deep nesting) and the plain-`ValueError` huge-integer-literal failure (3.11+ int/str limit) into `ImportRefused` (original kept as `__cause__`), and bounds every parsed proposal at `MAX_PROPOSAL_DEPTH = 64` via an ITERATIVE `_max_nesting` walk — so no downstream consumer of a proposal (egress scrub, schema check, `canonical_json`, the queue write) can be crashed by structure the parse accepted. `_refusal_suffix` catches the same classes (the keep path must not crash on the input that made it necessary); `plan_import`'s two schema checks and `_task_from_proposal`'s diagnostic `dumps` convert/guard RecursionError too. `run_import`'s `except` was NOT widened.
  - AC-6 (I-18): `plan_side_proposal_schema` = `build_proposal_schema` minus the F-12 defaults trio (`depends_on`, `risk`, `gates`) from the task's required list; `plan_import` applies it to the proposal AFTER the semantic checks (status/forbidden/duplicate/oos keep their readable, task-naming messages) and BEFORE the merge. The derived schema's `project` gains `minLength: 1`. One schema check, both sides: missing/empty/mistyped `project`, extra top-level keys, task keys outside the spec fields (a typo'd field name can no longer be silently dropped by the merge's spec-field copy) and a handed `status` are all refused by the schema.
- `pyproject.toml` — runtime dependency `rfc3339-validator==0.1.4` (exact pin, review round 1 finding 1: PyPI has only 0.1.x; jsonschema's date-time check is silently a no-op without it; the comment names the F-49 test that fails if it goes missing).
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f49.py` added beside test_f43–f48.
- `tests/test_f49.py` (new, protected from the day it exists): `test_ac_1`…`test_ac_6` + 9 review/edge tests.
- Re-seats (same strength, no assertion weakened; all three first surfaced by the first full-gate run):
  - `tests/test_f04.py` (protected): the done-dependency fixture's `approved_at="y"` → `"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z"` — the placeholder was schema-invalid all along (the schema always declared date-time; nothing enforced it). Fixture data legalised, no assertion touched.
  - `tests/test_f31.py` (protected): `KNOWN_DEPENDENCIES` gains `"rfc3339-validator==0.1.4"` (round 1: the exact real pin) with the F-49 comment — the pin stays an exact-set pin; the dashboard's own "no new dependency" contract is untouched (the dependency is contract validation's).
  - `tests/test_f43.py` (protected): the importer's `_EGRESS_MODULES` allow list gains `iter_schema_violations` — the F-49 proposal-schema check inside `plan_import`, under the same scrub-before-plan guard `plan_import` itself already sits behind (run_import scrubs the parsed proposal before `plan_import` sees it, so the violation messages render from scrubbed values).

**Design notes:**

- **The resolver's home (AC-1):** `factory.contract`, not a test helper, because factory callers need it too — the natural next consumer is the approve gate (`factory approve` refusing to approve a task whose refs dangle; proposed as a WO below, not built). The queue WALK (which task, which AC, which ref — the context the resolver doesn't have) stays in the CI test where the task record lives.
- **Which statuses count (AC-1):** every status except `draft` — the safe default the brief names. The queue's status set has no "superseded"; a `done` task whose test file was later deleted SHOULD fail (its record claims evidence that no longer exists). Today's population: 28 done, 32 draft.
- **AST binding semantics:** only DIRECT top-level statements bind — a `def` inside `if`, a loop or another function does not count (importing might or might not see it), a later top-level assignment (`test_ac_1 = None`) shadows an earlier `def`, and a later `def` re-wins. That is exactly the run-time binding story, read statically.
- **The format checker (AC-2):** one builder (`_validator`/`_build_validator`), `Draft202012Validator.FORMAT_CHECKER`. Both repo schemas checked: tasks.schema.json carries all six `format` keywords (all `date-time`: `updated_at`, attempt_reset `at`, attempts `started`/`ended`, `approved_at`, owner-step `done_at`); project.schema.json and toolkit.schema.json carry none — so date-time is the only format the checker turned on. The dependency is runtime, not dev: queue validation refuses malformed stamps wherever the factory is installed. Probe: with `rfc3339-validator` uninstalled, `test_ac_2` fails at its first assertion (the no-op guard) — the check is load-bearing, the test detects its absence.
- **Where the truncated names land (AC-3):** the PROMPT gets the exact count only (`+N more`, no names) — the budget scales with the listing, and naming all 205 would size the session for 205; the session is TOLD truncation happened and can rglob if it chooses. The SUMMARY (the owner's confirm surface) names the dropped docs: the session literally cannot name docs it never saw, and the owner deciding whether to apply the import is where the cap's consequence ("no task defined only in these could have been proposed") must be legible. Argued from the AC text ("the proposal shown to the owner names the truncated docs" / "or names them in the import summary the owner confirms").
- **The lock (AC-4):** `queue_writer_lock` in state.py — the brief's "extend the one primitive, not a parallel path": the lock must be takeable before the queue file exists, and `TaskQueue` can't even be constructed for a fresh queue (its `__init__` loads), so the flock moved to a module-level primitive the method now delegates to. Validation stays outside the lock (validating a candidate needs no lock); the no-queue DECISION and the WRITE are inside it.
- **What converts to ImportRefused (AC-5):** `RecursionError` (json's recursive descent; jsonschema's; json.dumps' in the diagnostic path) and the huge-int `ValueError` — the two exception shapes a hostile proposal provokes in parsing/validation, each named at its site. `MemoryError`-shaped inputs are not convertible at this boundary (a real MemoryError leaves the process unreliable; the OS-level ceiling owns those). The except in `run_import` stays `(ImportRefused, OSError)` — nothing was widened to `Exception`.
- **The project check's order (AC-6):** semantic mismatch first (it names both spellings), then the semantic per-task rules (their readable messages are pinned by protected tests), then the ONE schema check, then the merge, then the merged-document validation (still the final gate for queue-level shapes like the defaults the importer writes). The defaults trio is dropped from the plan-side required list so F-12's defaults behaviour is byte-identical; the session-side staged check stays the stricter of the two, keeping the documented one-directional agreement (staged-pass ⊆ plan-accept) intact.

**Per-AC evidence (red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, never an import error or typo; red run on untouched `30a64e5` + tests/test_f49.py only):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: RED `AssertionError: M-10: no static ref resolver exists — … (assert None is not None)`. GREEN: the real `.factory/tasks.json` passes the walk; a typo'd ref on a synthetic `ready` task fails naming task/AC/ref; the same typo on `draft` passes; `if False:`-hidden, nested, `= None`-bound and shadowed defs all fail; `path::Class::name` resolves; an outside-repo file refuses.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: RED `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ContractError` (`updated_at: "yesterday"` loaded — the m-3 defect verbatim). GREEN: `"yesterday"`/`"2026-13-01T00:00:00Z"`/date-without-time refused naming the field; `attempts.started` and `approved_at` refused likewise; `Z`, offset, fractional, lowercase-t/z and `now_iso()` accepted; the real tasks.json, project.yaml and both projects/*.project.yaml load.
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: RED `AssertionError: assert '+5 more' in "You are the factory's importer seat…"` (and the summary carried no dropped names). GREEN: 205 docs → exactly 200 `- docs/…` lines + `+5 more`; the summary names docs/d200.md–d204.md; exactly 200 → no `+0 more` anywhere; the session call receives the 200-scaled budget (`timeout_min` 60.0 ≠ 61.0, turns at the 200 ceiling — the wall clock discriminates).
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: RED `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ImportRefused` — the reproduced race: a real subprocess applied its fresh import while this process sat in the check→write window, both writes succeeded, and the on-disk queue was the loser-overwriter's. GREEN: exactly one write (the subprocess's, byte for byte — captured right after it wrote, unchanged after the loser refused), one refusal ("changed since … another writer"); plus the lock probe — with `<queue>.lock` held by another process, the fresh write blocks (no path to the disk write) until the lock frees.
- **AC-5** `test_ac_5`: RED `RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string` — the exception escaped the keep-refused path, exactly m-7. GREEN: a 100k-deep list and a 100k-deep object inside `tasks`, and a 5000-digit integer literal, all refuse with the output saved (content byte-identical, 0600); a 700-deep document that PARSES refuses by name at the depth bound; a normal shape refusal is unchanged (`.json`, violations listed); the CLI exits 1 with a message, no traceback.
- **AC-6** `test_ac_6`: RED `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ImportRefused` — the no-`project` proposal was planned (and would have been written under the profile's name). GREEN: no key, `""`, `null`, `123` all refused by the one schema check naming `project`; the right project plans; another name keeps the existing mismatch refusal; through `run_import` nothing is written.

**Spec notes (reported, not resolved):**

1. **AC-1 says "using F-01's AST resolution"** — F-01's test resolves with a `globals()` lookup over its own module only (`tests/test_f01.py::test_contract_refs_resolve`); there was no AST resolution to reuse. Built as the remediation plan intends (a real static resolver) and noted for the F-04 reconcile.
2. **AC-1 statuses/param-ids:** non-draft = every status except `draft` (the safe default); param-id refs fail closed (0 exist in today's queue — all 280 refs are `path::name`).
3. **AC-3 landing spot:** prompt gets the count, summary gets the names (design note above).
4. **AC-2 dependency:** yes — `rfc3339-validator==0.1.4` (exact pin) added as a runtime dependency (without it the checker is silently a no-op; the test's first assertion detects its absence, probed by uninstall). No other format turned on: `date-time` is the only `format` keyword in either schema. The checker is stricter than RFC 3339 on leap seconds (`23:59:60` refused — it validates through `datetime`) and year 0000; both are the fail-closed side, and `now_iso()` emits neither.

**Hostile pass (real code paths; every probe on this tree):**

- **Refs:** trailing-space name → refused (padded PATH strips, L1 parity — pinned); Cyrillic-е look-alike → does not resolve; `path::`, `::name`, no `::`, 4 parts, `tests\test_ok.py` (backslash) → all refused; param-id → fail-closed; a non-UTF-8 and an unparsable test file → refused, never a crash; a symlinked file escaping the repo → refused "resolves outside the repo"; `..` and absolute paths → refused; an untracked test file resolves locally (CI checks out the PR, so there it cannot — looser locally, never wrong in CI — probed).
- **Timestamps:** `+24:00` offset, space-for-T, hour 24, unpadded month, leap second → refused; fractional seconds, lowercase `t`/`z`, `-05:00` → accepted (all pinned in `test_review_timestamp_hostile_shapes`).
- **Doc listing:** an escaping markdown symlink IS listed (pinned, classified 3 — containment is the clone's, where its target does not exist, and F-47's read boundary); a non-UTF-8 file name never crashes the prompt build (macOS refuses to create such names — the byte-level probe runs its full course on Linux CI; an astral name stands in locally, pinned).
- **The race:** a lock left by a killed process never blocks (flock died with the holder — real subprocess probed, pinned); a queue path occupied by a DIRECTORY refuses with `OSError` at the write (never a silent overwrite, never a traceback).
- **Parsing:** a deep-fenced block (fence stripped → the same conversion), a BOM, two JSON objects back-to-back, NaN/Infinity literals (`json.loads` accepts NaN/Infinity by default — NaN-project refuses via the project mismatch, Infinity-in-an-extra-key refuses via the new schema check; a `parse_constant` boundary hook is proposed below, not built).
- **Project exactness:** `" proj"` and `"Proj"` keep the exact-match refusal.
- **CLI smoke:** `factory --help` ok; the real queue loads under the checker (`software-factory`, 0 non-draft dangling).

**Findings classified (scope rule):**

- **(1) fixed here** — three, all in the m-7/m-6 class found by sweeping the AC's own surfaces past the listed shapes: `_refusal_suffix` crashed on exactly the inputs that made the save necessary (its `json.loads` re-parse hit the same RecursionError/ValueError — the keep path is now safe for them); `_task_from_proposal`'s no-readable-id diagnostic could itself crash on a hostile entry (`dumps` guarded); a typo'd per-task field name was silently DROPPED by the merge's spec-field copy (now an `additionalProperties` refusal from the plan-side schema check).
- **(2) amended ACs** — none; every AC was achievable and achieved as written (notes above are spec notes, not holes).
- **(3) owned elsewhere** — (3a) refs whose test exists but is skipped/xfail-marked, or lives in a file the runner never collects (outside `testpaths`, a `conftest.py`, wrong name pattern): static resolution proves the FUNCTION, not the COLLECTION — L1's not-collected evidence (F-46 AC-5) catches it at spend time; a queue-wide collection check belongs in F-51's test/CI hygiene, not built here. (3b) `docs/` traversal through DIRECTORY symlinks diverges across the supported Pythons (3.11's `rglob` follows them, 3.13's does not) — listing membership can differ by interpreter; F-51/F-58 territory. (3c) the escaping-symlink doc is listed (pinned): the clone cannot contain its target and F-47's read boundary owns containment — the listing adds no policy of its own. (3d) `factory/importer.py` is still not in `paths.protected` (same class as F-48's 4a for eval.py) — an owner PR, not this WO.
- **(4) proposed WOs, not built** — (4a) **approve-time ref check:** wire `resolve_test_ref` into `factory approve` so approving a task whose refs dangle refuses at the gate instead of at CI (the CI guard stays the net; the approve gate is the earlier, cheaper catch). (4b) **strict-JSON parse boundary:** a `parse_constant` hook in `parse_proposal` refusing NaN/Infinity at the parse (today the schema refuses them downstream — correct, but named late).

**Residuals (probed, not closed):** the checker is stricter than RFC 3339 on leap seconds and year 0000 (fail-closed; `now_iso()` emits neither); at 200+ listed docs the turn budget is ceiling-capped for 200 and 205 alike — only the wall clock discriminates (pinned); a hand-edited deeply-nested QUEUE file (not session output) can still RecursionError at `load_tasks` — the queue is protected/orchestrator-written and AC-5 scopes session output.

**Not verified:** no real model session ran (the importer is driven with injected session output; the real `sdk_import_session` path is exercised only by the existing plumbing suites). Not run on Linux locally: the local gate is macOS, and F-49 touches no sandbox code — CI (ubuntu-24.04) is the first Linux run of this tree (where the non-UTF-8 filename probe also runs its full course). The Python 3.13 CI leg was reasoned about (the `rglob` divergence, 3b), not executed locally.

(The first full-gate run — before the three re-seats — read 1099 passed, 3 failed: `test_f04::test_ac_2` (the `"y"` placeholder stamp), `test_f31::test_ac_1` (KNOWN_DEPENDENCIES), `test_f43::test_ac_6` (the importer allow list); each re-seat is documented above, and the final run is the evidence.)

### F-49 owner review, round 1: three findings — the pin was unsatisfiable, the resolver proved the wrong thing, the listing was computed three times — all fixed

**Finding 1 (class 1, CI-blocking): `rfc3339-validator>=2` matched nothing on PyPI.** Verified before fixing: `pip download --no-deps "rfc3339-validator>=2"` → `ERROR: No matching distribution found … (from versions: 0.1.0, 0.1.1, 0.1.2, 0.1.3, 0.1.4)`. The worktree only worked because a bare `pip install rfc3339-validator` had already put 0.1.4 in its venv, and no local gate ever re-resolves the pin — CI's `pip install -e ".[dev]"` would have failed before any test ran. Fixed: exact pin **`rfc3339-validator==0.1.4`** in pyproject.toml and the same string in test_f31's KNOWN_DEPENDENCIES; every ledger mention of ">=2" corrected. **Proof in a FRESH venv** (`/tmp/f49-fresh-venv`, not this worktree's): `pip install -e ".[dev]"` → exit 0, `rfc3339-validator-0.1.4` in the resolved install list; `tests/test_f49.py` there → **17 passed** (incl. test_ac_2 — on that venv's jsonschema 4.26.0).

**Finding 2 (class 2, AC hole): the resolver proved "a top-level function", not "a function pytest would collect as a test".** The reviewer's probe — `resolve_test_ref(repo, "factory/contract.py::resolve_test_ref")` → None — was a real production function in a real production module passing the guard. Fixed: `_pytest_collection_config(repo)` reads the repo's pytest config STATICALLY in pytest's own discovery order (pytest.ini root keys → pyproject `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` → tox.ini `[pytest]` → setup.cfg `[tool:pytest]`; an unparseable config fails closed with a message naming it), and `resolve_test_ref` additionally requires: the file's basename matches `python_files` (default `test_*.py`/`*_test.py`), the repo-relative path sits under `testpaths` when set, the function name matches `python_functions` (default `test*`), and a class matches `python_classes` (default `Test*`) and defines no `__init__`. The reviewer's probe now returns "does not match the repo's pytest python_files patterns (test_*.py, *_test.py)"; the real queue's walk still returns 0 failures; `TestGroup::test_grouped` still resolves. New test `test_review_resolver_requires_collectability`: production fn in a production file, helper fn (`make_fixture`), non-`Test*` class, `Test*`-with-`__init__` all refused with the naming reason; a custom `python_functions = check_*` config is honoured (check_* resolves, test_* refused); a custom `testpaths = ["suite"]` is honoured (tests/ refused, suite/ resolves); an unparseable pytest.ini fails closed. **AMENDED AC-1 (for F-04 reconcile):** "A CI test resolves every acceptance ref on every non-draft task to a function pytest would collect as a test (test-tree placement, test-file naming and test-function/class naming per the repo's pytest config), using static AST resolution; a typo'd ref on a `ready` task fails CI." Skip/xfail markers and actual collection stay out of scope (3a → F-51), as the review accepted.

**Finding 3 (class 1): the planning-doc listing was computed up to three times per import** (inside `build_import_prompt`, again for the budget, again after the session for `plan.truncated_docs`) — a docs change during the session could make the summary name a different capped set than the prompt offered, breaking the module's own one-listing invariant. Fixed: `run_import` computes `PlanningDocs` ONCE before the session and threads the same object to the prompt (`build_import_prompt` gains a `files=` keyword; None computes, for direct callers), the scaled budget and `plan.truncated_docs`. New test `test_review_one_listing_across_prompt_budget_and_summary`: a doc added inside the injected session never reaches the summary — the original dropped five stand, and the late doc is named nowhere.

**Gate after round 1 (final tree, macOS, after the last code edit — an E501 line the first round-1 gate run caught at ruff, fixed):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1104 passed** (1102 + 2 round-1 review tests), 0 failed, 317 s.

## F-56 — Session record transport

Found in the 2026-11-05 jobfinderos import (F-13 step 3, BUILD-ORDER §F-56): the sandboxed entry's record crossed the srt stdout pipe as one JSON line, and three of four real importer runs lost a completed session to a truncated line. The fourth run's record line was exactly 81,920 bytes (5 × 16 KiB), cut inside `permission_denials` (the reviewer's denied Bash commands echoed back in full).

**Built (owner-directed; every protected change below was directed in this WO's brief, the F-46/F-47/F-48/F-49 precedent):**

- `factory/session.py` (protected, owner-directed) —
  - **AC-1 (file transport):** `record_path_for(settings_path)` names `<settings>-record/record.json` — a per-session dir OUTSIDE the clone (nothing there can ride a diff), sibling of the srt settings so the fan-out's per-session settings (`<base>-rN.json`) give every seat its own record path. `_run_prepared` wipes the dir fresh before the run (a fan-out retry reuses the per-session settings; a stale record can never be read as the retry's), names it in the spec (`record_path`), carves it out of the sandbox denies (`record_write=`), and reads it back through `_open_record_once` — ONE open (`O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`), a regular-file `fstat` check (a planted FIFO cannot block it, a directory or symlink is refused), read-to-EOF, strict UTF-8. `_write_record_file` (the entry's half) writes atomically through the factory's ONE atomic-write helper `state._atomic_write_json` (F-45 AC-6's no-own-rename rule holds; the private import joins importer.py's on F-55's promotion list). `_main` writes the record and prints NOTHING un-prefixed; stdout carries only `factory-trace` lines. The stdout record-line parse is gone; a record-looking stdout line is a foreign line the forwarder already ignores (`test_review_fake_stdout_record_line_is_ignored`).
  - **AC-2 (self-explaining failure):** `SessionRecordError(SessionConfigError)` — the session STARTED but the record is missing/empty/unopenable/non-UTF-8/unparsable/invalid. The ONE failure builder `_record_failure_text` names: the srt exit code; the signal name when `returncode < 0` (`signal.Signals`); srt's own kill report when its stderr carries one (`_srt_kill_fact`); a seam-scrubbed (`scrub_session_text`, the session's token) stderr tail capped at `RECORD_STDERR_TAIL_CHARS = 2000`; and the saved-streams pair. `_save_session_stdout` gained `stderr=`: both full streams are saved together under `<factory-root>/sessions/` as `unparsable-record-<id>.stdout.txt` + `.stderr.txt` (one id, 0600, O_EXCL, seam-scrubbed; the name keeps F-43 AC-3's wording). The timeout path: a record already written is RETURNED (a completed session is never lost to what happened around it — pinned); a recordless timeout re-raises the `TimeoutExpired` carrying the same helper's facts as a note (`add_note`), with the streams saved from `exc.output`/`exc.stderr`.
  - **AC-3 (bounded denials):** `DENIAL_TOOL_INPUT_MAX_CHARS = 2000`, `DENIAL_LIST_MAX = 100` (both docstringed with the jobfinderos evidence and the why). `_bound_permission_denials` runs in `run_session`'s `_carry` — where the record is BUILT, in the entry, before anything is written — on EVERY exit path (completed/failed/aborted, in-process callers too): dict entries carrying `tool_input` get that one field capped (serialised first when not a string) with a visible `tool_input_truncated: true` marker; the list is capped with the overflow on the new `SessionRecord.permission_denials_dropped` field (defaulted 0, so pre-F-56 records still round-trip). Consumers swept: `permission_denials` has no consumer beyond the record itself (grep: session.py + test fixtures); the attempt record takes turns/tokens/skills/denial-count, not the list.
  - **AC-4 (the cut):** `_SRT_KILL_SIGNATURES` quotes cli.js's own words (line 315 `failed before delivering an update`, line 458 `Process killed by signal:`); `_srt_kill_fact` scans the scrubbed tail and the helper reports the distilled fact. See the finding below — not reproduced; bounded by construction.
  - `run_session_sandboxed`'s Raises docstring and the module docstring carry the transport's contract; `_fanout_sandboxed`'s cleanup removes the per-session `-record` dirs with the settings/runner/toolkit/scratch (`cleanup_run`'s prefix sweep is the crash backstop — unchanged, it already globs every `<settings>-` sibling).
- `factory/sandbox.py` (protected, owner-directed) — `build_settings(record_write=)`: the record dir joins `allowRead` (resolved, like the scratch carve-outs) and `allowWrite` in EVERY seat shape (`_allow_write` gained the parameter: implementer, single reviewer, fan-out). This is the Linux persistence carve-out: a write under the read-denied factory root that is not bound lands in srt's tmpfs and never reaches the host (F-47 round 1, finding 1); read AND write binds are what persist — proven with real srt by `test_ac_1_real_srt_record_dir_persists_on_the_host` (runs on macOS here and ubuntu-24.04 in CI, never skipped).
- `factory/importer.py` (NOT protected) — **AC-5:** `sdk_import_session` catches `SessionRecordError` BEFORE the generic `SessionConfigError` branch and routes it into the SAME salvage helper the abort path uses (`_abort_refusal` → `_save_refused_output`): the scratch's `$TMPDIR/proposal.json` is copied out (seam-scrubbed, 0600, O_EXCL) before `cleanup_run` sweeps it, and the refusal names the saved file plus the exact `factory import --proposal <file>` command, or says plainly that no proposal was written. No second copy-out; the head is seam-scrubbed again with the session's token.
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f56.py` added beside test_f43–f49.
- `tests/test_f56.py` (new, protected from the day it exists): `test_ac_1`…`test_ac_5` + 11 review/edge tests.
- Re-seats (same strength, no assertion weakened; all first surfaced by the first full-gate run — every one is a stub that returned the record on stdout, the transport this WO replaced):
  - `tests/test_f06.py` (protected): `_sandbox_stub` writes the record file via `record_path_for` and returns empty stdout (4 tests).
  - `tests/test_f47.py` (protected): `test_session_escapee_reaches_the_trace_sink` — the fake entry writes the record file (normal path) / writes none (timeout path, which stays the raised timeout); the `from_dict` monkeypatch is gone (the real parse reads the real file).
  - `tests/test_f22.py` (protected): `test_ac_7` and `test_staged_toolkit_is_cleaned_up` stubs write the record file; the cleanup test also asserts the `-record` sibling is swept.
  - `tests/test_f09.py` (protected): `_quiet_record(cmd, settings_path)` + `_write_stub_record` helper; 6 stub sites (the reviewer-integrity suite).
  - `tests/test_f27.py` (protected): the spec-carries-ids stub writes the record file.
  - `tests/test_f38.py` (protected): `_write_stub_record` helper + 4 stub sites (the fan-out suite).
  - `tests/test_importer_schema_shapes.py` (not protected): `test_unparsable_record_saves_full_stdout` re-seated to the new failure shape (no record file + cut stdout → `SessionRecordError` naming the exit code, the stderr fact, and BOTH saved files, 0600, full content — the old `record line length` pin is obsolete, no record line exists); `test_entry_final_print_flushes` re-seated as `test_entry_writes_the_record_atomically` (same intent — the record lands whole — new channel: stdout empty, file exact, no temp left); `test_scratch_files_land_in_the_session_tmp` stub writes the record file.

**Design notes:**

- **Where the record path lives, per seat (spec note 1).** Every seat: `<factory-root>/sandbox/<project>-<run-id>.json-record/record.json` (single sessions), `<base>-rN.json-record/` / `-vN.json-record/` (fan-out — per-session settings name it, two seats can never share one), `<factory-root>/sandbox/<run-id>.json-record/` for the importer (its own run under the same root). Always a sibling of the srt settings: OUTSIDE the clone (an implementer's `git add -A` cannot pick it up), under the factory root (wiped by `cleanup_run`'s prefix glob with the settings/runner/scratch), and — the Linux invariant — bound read-AND-write by `build_settings`, which is what persists a path under the read-denied factory root (tmpfs semantics; proven with real srt on the host).
- **Stray record-looking stdout lines (spec note 2): ignored**, deliberately: the forwarder's documented rule is "transport can only lose events, never crash a run", and a foreign un-prefixed line is exactly that. The file is the one record; the stdout line has no reader left to attack.
- **Timeout-after-record (hostile decision): the record is USED**, not discarded. The wall clock's job is to stop spending; a session that already delivered its result is done. The record itself carries the outcome (completed/aborted by the in-session clock), and the runner hanging in teardown afterwards is an orchestrator fault the factory should not bill to the session.
- **The caps (spec note 3).** 2000 chars per `tool_input`: the denial is evidence of WHAT was denied, not a second copy of the command stream — the F-42 deny reasons are ~140 chars, a 2000-char command is already pathological, and the truncated JSON keeps the head of the input (the identifying part). 100 entries: the heaviest real session recorded single digits; an order of magnitude of headroom caps the whole list at ~100 × (2000 + entry overhead) — under a quarter megabyte of record whatever the session did, against the observed 81,920-byte single-field cut. Dropped counts and per-entry markers make the bound visible, never silent.
- **The forgery surface (hostile, recorded).** The session runs model-chosen code inside the sandbox and its record dir is (by design) writable from inside: a session can author a record of its choosing, or an escaped process can overwrite the file after the entry writes it. This is NOT a new trust boundary — the entry process itself runs inside the same sandbox, and until F-56 the session could equally print a fake last stdout line. What the file transport CHANGES: the freshness wipe kills cross-run planting (a stale record from a previous attempt can never be read); `O_NOFOLLOW` + single-open kills the symlink/FIFO/directory tricks at read time; and the atomic write means the entry's own write is all-or-nothing. Residual (classified 4/3 below): a same-run forgery by the session or a survivor.
- **The atomic write goes through `state._atomic_write_json`** — F-45 AC-6's mechanical pin ("no module renames on its own") forced the right shape: one helper, same-dir temp, fsync, rename, dir fsync. The record is written `indent=2` by that helper — a byte larger, a format nobody parses by eye; fine.
- **Why the srt-kill signatures are matched on the SCRUBBED tail:** the seam keeps ordinary text and srt's words are plain ASCII; only a literal secret or control byte could alter them, neither of which srt emits. Matching post-seam keeps the taint rule (session text only reaches the seam or guarded writers — the F-43 AC-6 tripwire stays green) with no behavioural risk.

**Per-AC evidence (red first — each failure quoted its AC's defect, never an import error or typo; red run on untouched `af7cf2c` + tests/test_f56.py only; 12 failed, 6 passed — the six green-on-arrival are the documentation/guard legs named below):**

- **AC-1** `test_ac_1`: RED `SessionConfigError: the sandboxed session entry produced no record (exit 0):` — the stdout transport losing a record the stub had written to the file. GREEN: the 2 MB record with a stdout cut mid-`factory-trace`-line arrives whole; the spec named the record path before the session started and it is outside the clone; the fan-out's two seats get distinct `<base>-r1.json-record` / `-v1.json-record` paths (set-compared — thread scheduling picks who writes first, spec order is in the records); a symlink planted at the record path is refused. The real-srt leg RED `TypeError: build_settings() got an unexpected keyword argument 'record_write'` (the missing carve-out — the Linux persistence mechanism) → GREEN: real srt writes into the record dir through settings built exactly as `_run_prepared` builds them and the file EXISTS ON THE HOST.
- **AC-2** `test_ac_2`: RED `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '…/sessions'` — the no-record path saved NOTHING (the defect: no exit/signal facts, no saved streams). GREEN: exit 1 + `boom: the command failed` in the message, the planted token in neither message nor files, the saved pair `[stderr.txt, stdout.txt]` 0600 with both paths named in the error; `returncode -9` → `died by signal SIGKILL`; every branch raises `SessionRecordError` (a `SessionConfigError` subclass, so existing handlers hold).
- **AC-3** `test_ac_3`: RED `AssertionError: 200` (`assert len(record.permission_denials) < 200` — the unbounded list). GREEN: 200 denials of 5 KB each → 100 kept, `permission_denials_dropped == 100`, every kept `tool_input` ≤ 2000 chars with `tool_input_truncated: true`, the whole record under the documented bound; a small list with small inputs rides through byte-identical, no markers, dropped 0.
- **AC-4** `test_ac_4`: RED `AssertionError: … 'srt reported killing the command' in 'the sandboxed session entry produced no record (exit 1): Error: control fd 3 failed before delivering an update: …'` — the raw words were there, the FACT was not. GREEN: the control-kill signature → the error says srt reported killing the command over a dead control channel; the signal signature → srt reported the command was killed by a signal; and the factory's own spawn passes no `--control-fd` (pinned on the captured argv). The real-srt legs (green on both trees, deliberately — they are the probe): an 81,920-byte and a 2 MB single line cross the pipe whole under a live reader (`test_ac_4_real_srt_large_line_survives_a_live_reader`), and a writer that outlives the killed group cuts the captured stdout mid-line at an arbitrary byte (`test_review_real_srt_reader_stopping_cuts_stdout_mid_line`, the F-47 shim pattern so it proves the drain on every platform).
- **AC-5** `test_ac_5`: RED `AssertionError: the importer session could not run: the sandboxed session entry produced no record (exit 0)` (asserting `saved to` in the message) — the completed session's proposal was thrown away with the scratch. GREEN: the refusal salvages `$TMPDIR/proposal.json` (content byte-identical, saved under `<root>/import/`, 0600) and names it plus the exact `factory import --proposal <file>` command; with no proposal in the scratch the refusal says so plainly.
- Review legs red-first: timeout-after-record RED `subprocess.TimeoutExpired … timed out after 60 seconds` (the record was discarded) → GREEN returned; timeout-without-record RED (no `__notes__`) → GREEN the note carries the stderr fact and the sessions dir; fake stdout record line RED `assert 'forged' == 'the real one'` (the forged line WON) → GREEN the file's record is returned; settings carve-out RED (the record dir absent from `allowWrite`) → GREEN both lists carry it AND the stale record is wiped; entry write RED (`is_file() = False`) → GREEN exact content, no temp left, empty stdout. Green-on-arrival (documented guards, not defects): the non-regular-file refusals (pin the NEW read path: FIFO/directory/non-UTF-8 refused, never a hang), the stderr-tail cap (pre-existing at 2000, must survive), other-fields-not-bounded (scope), the mid-line-cut repro (mechanism), single-open (guard).

**The AC-4 finding (reproduced or bounded — the ledger record):** NOT REPRODUCED as the factory's mechanism; bounded by construction, and the mechanism CLASS is reproduced and made irrelevant.

1. **Ruled out by configuration:** the factory's `run_sandboxed` spawns `srt --settings … -c …` — never `--control-fd`. The dead-control-channel kill (cli.js `onControlError` → SIGTERM, SIGKILL after `KILL_GRACE_MS`, exit 1) cannot fire in a factory run; pinned on the real argv by `test_ac_4` leg 3.
2. **Ruled out empirically (macOS, real srt 0.0.79, direct cli.js probes):** (a) a unix-socketpair fd and (b) a connected-TCP fd as the control channel both die at cli.js's open-time usability probe — `Error: --control-fd 3 is not usable: ENXIO … Refusing to run the command…`, exit 1, the command never spawns (macOS's zero-length `readv` returns ENXIO for sockets; on Linux the probe may pass — untested there, moot given (1)); (c) a pipe read end whose writer closes mid-line: the partial write is parsed as a line (`Invalid config on control fd 3: ignored`), the channel close is benign ("the command keeps running"), the command ran its full 30 s and exited 0 — the kill path is unreachable with a pipe fd.
3. **Size/pipe/pump ruled out:** one single line at exactly 81,920 bytes and at 2 MB crosses the real pipe whole under the orchestrator's real pump (`test_ac_4_real_srt_large_line_survives_a_live_reader`) — consistent with the WO's earlier 400 KB probe.
4. **The mechanism class that DOES cut a line: the READER stopping.** A writer that outlives the killed group (setsid escapee) keeps writing while the drain grace expires — the captured stdout ends mid-line at an arbitrary byte count (`test_review_real_srt_reader_stopping_cuts_stdout_mid_line`); a wall-clock kill mid-write does the same to whatever had drained. 5 × 16 KiB is one sample of "whatever had drained when the process died". Either way the file transport makes it irrelevant to the record: the entry writes the file atomically and never puts the record on stdout again, and a recordless kill is an `SessionRecordError` that names the exit path (or a used record if it landed).

**Hostile pass (real srt on macOS where named; every probe on this tree):**

- **Pre-planted symlink at the record path** → `O_NOFOLLOW` open refuses; the error names the path and why (pinned in `test_ac_1`).
- **Pre-planted FIFO** → `O_NONBLOCK` + regular-file `fstat`: refused, never a hang (pinned; a plain open would block until the planter spoke).
- **Pre-planted directory** → the same `fstat` refusal (pinned).
- **Partial record then killed** → the atomic write (temp+rename) means the file is all-or-nothing; a killed-before-write session is the AC-2 error naming the exit code; a killed-after-write session's record is USED (timeout leg).
- **Two fan-out seats on one scratch/record path** → impossible by naming: per-session settings `<base>-rN.json` make the record dirs distinct (pinned, set-compared); the guard-spanning integrity check is unchanged.
- **Record over the AC-3 bounds via other fields** (a 1 MB `result`) → NOT bounded, by scope: the AC bounds `permission_denials` (the observed failure); the file transport carries any size (pinned by `test_review_other_record_fields_are_not_bounded`). Classified 3/4 below.
- **Fake record line on stdout** → ignored; the file's record wins (pinned, was red).
- **Stdout cut mid-`factory-trace` line** → the trace loses that event at most; the record arrives whole (AC-1's own test shape).
- **Timeout after the record was written** → the record is used (decided, pinned).
- **Non-UTF-8 record file** → refused naming why (pinned).
- **A record file nested past the parse's recursion limit** (`"[" * 100_000`) → refused as an invalid record, never a crash past the parse (pinned; found by the own hostile re-read of the diff, fixed as class 1).
- **Stale record from a previous attempt** → the dir is wiped fresh before the run; the stale file is gone and the failure names the missing record (pinned, was red).
- **The session authors its own record / a survivor overwrites it** → possible, by design (the dir is the sandbox's writable carve-out); unchanged trust boundary (see design notes). Classified below.
- **srt kill reports** → both cli.js signatures distilled into the error (pinned with the quoted words).
- **Fresh `pip install -e ".[dev]"`** → not run: no dependency was touched (pyproject unchanged).

**Findings classified (scope rule):**

- (1) *fixed here* — the stderr half of the saved pair could be lost silently on an OSError (the stdout path was still returned: half the evidence beats none — the behaviour is now explicit in `_save_session_stdout`); the `with_suffix` derivation of the stderr path name was wrong for a two-dot filename (fixed by name-suffix swap, caught by AC-2's own test); the fan-out cleanup did not remove `-record` dirs (added beside runner/toolkit/scratch); a planted record file nested past the parse's recursion limit would have crashed the orchestrator out of `_read_record` (own hostile re-read of the diff — `RecursionError` now refused like every other invalid record, the F-49 parse-boundary rule, pinned in the non-regular-files test).
- (2) *amended ACs, for F-04 reconcile* — **AC-2 amended**: *"A missing or unreadable record raises an error naming the srt exit code, the signal if any, and a stderr tail passed through the F-43 seam; full stdout AND stderr are saved together under `<factory-root>/sessions/` (one id, mode 0600, both paths named in the error). The wall-clock path uses the same helper: a record already written is returned, a recordless timeout re-raises with the facts as a note."* **AC-1 amended**: *"...and the orchestrator reads it from there — one open, `O_NOFOLLOW`, refusing a symlink, FIFO or directory — after wiping the path fresh; a stray record-looking stdout line is ignored. The record dir is carved out of the sandbox's denies for WRITING on every seat shape (the write bind is what persists it under the read-denied factory root on Linux; the read bind rides along as belt-and-braces), proven with real srt to persist on the host."* (Both amendments state what the ACs' own tests demanded; the purpose was already the ACs'; the AC-1 carve-out wording corrected in review round 1, finding 4.)
- (3) *owned elsewhere* — trace events still travel over stdout and can be cut the same way: the trace is best-effort evidence by design (F-30 AC-5: a lost write is reported once and the run continues), but a `trace-file sidecar` (the forwarder appending to a file in the record dir) is a natural F-5x hardening — pointed at here, not built; the whole-record size (a multi-MB `result` rides the file whole): the entry could cap it like `permission_denials` — a decision about what the attempt record should keep, owned by F-59's provider/usage record work; the eval and SDK probe as record consumers: both go through `_run_prepared` (eval) or no record at all (the probe — one fixed import), nothing to change; the same-run forgery residual: the sandbox CAN write its own record — the structural fix is signing the record with an orchestrator-held secret staged read-only, proposed below.
- (4) *proposed WOs, not built* — **signed records:** the entry writes a MAC over the record with a key the orchestrator stages into the spec file (read-only to the session) and never writes elsewhere; the orchestrator verifies before trusting status/reason. Closes the same-run forgery for real. **trace sidecar:** move `factory-trace` events to an append-only file in the record dir (same transport, same carve-out), keeping stdout for the CLI's own noise — removes the last stdout dependency.

**Residuals (probed, not closed):** the session (or a surviving escapee) can author or overwrite its own record file before the orchestrator reads it — same trust boundary as the stdout line it replaced (freshness, nofollow, single-open and the atomic write close every cross-run and read-time trick); a planted arbitrarily LARGE record file reads whole into orchestrator memory (the AC's "whole, whatever its size" is the letter; the entry's own records are bounded by the session's real output — the cost is the forgery residual's); `cli.js`'s Linux control-fd behaviour (the open-probe result for sockets there) is reasoned, not probed — moot while the factory passes no `--control-fd`; the record file is written `indent=2` (the shared atomic helper's format) — cosmetically larger, semantically identical.

**Mutation checks** (each core behaviour flipped, its own test red, then restored): freshness wipe removed → `test_review_settings_carve_out_the_record_dir` red; `O_NOFOLLOW` removed → the symlink leg of `test_ac_1` red — the FIRST pin was weak (any record error matched; the mutation check caught it), re-seated to pin the mechanism (`cannot be opened`, the ELOOP path) and re-checked red; the denial bounding disabled → `test_ac_3` red; the srt-kill fact disabled → `test_ac_4` red; `record_write` dropped from the settings call → the carve-out test red; the importer's salvage branch neutralised → `test_ac_5` red; the timeout's record-use removed → the timeout test red.

**Process incident (recorded for the reviewer):** the first mutation-check harness ran `git checkout -- factory/session.py` on the uncommitted tree, reverting that file to `af7cf2c` and destroying its F-56 work mid-pass. Recovered BIT-IDENTICAL (same md5 before and after) from a staged-runner copy — `_stage_runner`'s per-test copy of the complete implementation under the pytest tmp dir — then re-proven: the f56 suite 18/18, ruff clean, and the gate below re-run on the recovered tree. The reworked harness snapshots to /tmp and restores from the snapshot only; no git write touches the uncommitted tree again.

**Not verified:** no real model session ran (per WO): the entry is driven by stubbed `run_sandboxed` (fake entries writing the real record file through the real naming) and by `_main` unit tests with a stubbed `run_session`; the real srt legs that DID run locally are macOS-only (`test_ac_1_real_srt_record_dir_persists_on_the_host` — the Linux persistence proof — `test_ac_4_real_srt_large_line_survives_a_live_reader`, `test_review_real_srt_reader_stopping_cuts_stdout_mid_line` via the pass-through shim, and the four direct cli.js control-fd probes); the Linux runs of those are CI's (ubuntu-24.04) on the PR, where the host-persistence leg is the one that matters. The control-fd probes were not run in a Linux container. `pip install -e ".[dev]"` not re-proven: no dependency touched.

**Gate (final tree, macOS, frozen after the last code edit — the recovery tree, after the incident above):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1122 passed** (1104 pre-existing + 18 F-56), 0 failed, exit 0, 419 s.

### F-56 owner review, round 1: four findings + one classify — all fixed

The reviewer verified on this tree: the gate log (1122 passed), the fan-out/cleanup/salvage wiring, `permission_denials`'s consumer sweep, main CI on `af7cf2c` — and ran the real-srt legs on **Linux (ubuntu-24.04 container, this tree copied in, same srt pin): tests/test_f56.py + f47 + f06 + f38 → 101 passed**, so `test_ac_1_real_srt_record_dir_persists_on_the_host` (the Linux persistence proof) is green on Linux NOW, in a container — not deferred to CI.

- **Finding 1 (class 1): the failure message named a stderr file that did not exist.** `_save_session_stdout` wrote the stderr half only when stderr was non-empty and swallowed its OSError, while `_record_failure_text` always named the pair — an error message pointing at evidence that is not there. Fixed both ways: the stderr file is ALWAYS written (empty is honest — the named pair exists as a pair), and the message names the stderr path only when it exists, else says "the stderr copy was not written". Red-first (`test_review_round1_named_saved_files_exist`): empty stderr → both named paths exist; a monkeypatched failing stderr write → the message says so and names only existing files.
- **Finding 2 (class 1): the record read had no size bound.** `_open_record_once` read to EOF — a planted 300 MB record cost ~650 MB peak orchestrator RSS per seat (reviewer's probe); the residual I had recorded is promoted to a fix (the goal includes "never crash"). `RECORD_MAX_BYTES = 64 MiB` (docstringed: two orders above any real record now that denials are bounded); the read is `RECORD_MAX_BYTES + 1` bytes and an oversize file is refused through the same `SessionRecordError` — so the importer still salvages. Red-first (`test_review_round1_record_read_is_size_bounded`): over the cap → refused naming the bound without reading the file whole; just under → parses and is used.
- **Finding 3 (class 1): the AC-3 bound was weaker than its comment claimed.** Only a dict's `tool_input` was capped; non-dict entries and dicts with other large fields passed at full size. Fixed as (a): `DENIAL_ENTRY_MAX_CHARS = DENIAL_TOOL_INPUT_MAX_CHARS + 400` caps EVERY kept entry's serialized form (an over-cap entry becomes `{"entry_truncated": true, "text": <capped>}`), making the total claim true for any shape: `DENIAL_LIST_MAX × DENIAL_ENTRY_MAX_CHARS` ≈ 240 KB of denial list. Red-first (`test_review_round1_nondict_denial_entries_are_bounded`): 150 non-dict 50 KB entries → 100 kept, 50 dropped, every kept entry capped and marked; ditto a dict with a large non-`tool_input` field.
- **Finding 4 (class 1, claim accuracy): "read AND write binds are what persist" was not what the test proved.** The reviewer's Linux mutation — `record_write` removed from `allowRead` only — still passed the persistence test: the WRITE bind alone persists the file on Linux. Corrected in `sandbox.py` (the `record_write` docstring and the allowRead comment), `session.py` (the module docstring's transport paragraph) and here: **the allowWrite bind is what persists the write on Linux (proven by the reviewer's mutation); the read bind is belt-and-braces** — nothing inside the sandbox reads the record (the entry writes it, the orchestrator reads outside), but on macOS a denyRead root also refuses writes under it (the platform semantics in sandbox.py's docstring), so the pair is kept together on both platforms. The structural assert in the real-srt test stays.
- **ALSO (classified, minimal build): the timeout path ACCEPTS whatever record the file holds.** If the entry never wrote one, the file can only be one the session planted — before F-56 that case always failed loudly. The use stays (a delivered record is the session's result; the gates still judge the diff), but it is no longer silent: `_report_timeout_record` emits one `record_used_after_timeout` event through the same tiers as `_report_escapee` (trace sink → the run's own trace file → stderr), and the event joins `trace.py`'s `EVENT_TYPES` (protected file, owner-directed; the stdout forwarder still passes `tool_call` only, so a session can neither forge nor suppress it). Red-first (`test_review_round1_timeout_record_use_is_visible`). **Residual, classified beside the forgery residual:** a planted record at timeout is now used-and-visible rather than refused; the signed-records proposal (finding 4's WO below) closes it for real.

**Round-1 test count:** tests/test_f56.py 18 → 22 (four round-1 tests, all red-first on the round-0 tree).

**Gate after round 1 (final tree, macOS, frozen after the last code edit):** `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1126 passed** (1122 + 4 round-1 tests), 0 failed, exit 0, 414 s. Mutation check after round 1: `_report_timeout_record`'s call removed → the visibility test red (restored from snapshot; the f56 suite re-run green).

**Status to set at reconcile:** unchanged — F-56 → `done` (owner, after merge), with the AC-1/AC-2 amendments and the two proposed WOs (signed records — now also closing the planted-at-timeout residual; trace sidecar) recorded above.
## F-51 — Test suite and CI hygiene

Source findings (CODE-REVIEW-2026-11-05): M-11, m-20, m-21, m-22, m-23, I-1, I-16, I-17, A-7. The goal behind the nine ACs: the gate that judges every factory change must be TRUSTWORTHY — green on any interpreter and any machine because the code is right; never red because of where Python lives, a live endpoint or a timing sleep; never quietly weaker (a silent skip, a deselected mandatory assertion, a blanket warning filter, a blanket type-ignore); never spending money by accident; and the package works as a wheel, not only from this checkout.

**Built (owner-directed; every protected change below was directed in this WO's brief, the F-46…F-56 precedent):**

- `tests/_support/` (new; not collected by pytest, imports no test module) — the ONE shared harness home (AC-7, I-17): per-origin modules `f05 f07 f12 f16 f22 f23 f30 f38`, each block cut by AST node extents from its origin at 47705d1 and re-verified AST-identical to `git show HEAD:tests/test_<origin>.py` after the move (script-compared, then the git diff read line by line — the bulk-edit rule). `__init__.py` hosts the three new shared helpers: `wait_until(predicate, timeout, what, interval)` (AC-6: polls, returns as soon as the condition holds, times out raising AssertionError naming the condition, lets a raising predicate propagate), `live_tests_enabled()`/`live_session_gate()` (AC-3: exactly `FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1`; every other value — `0`, `true`, `yes`, ` 1`, empty — is not an opt-in), and `no_bare_python3(text, executable)` (AC-1: removes the exact quoted interpreter path — shlex and bare forms — then searches for a BARE `python3`; never strips the substring).
- `tests/test_f51.py` (new, protected from the day it exists): `test_ac_1`…`test_ac_9` + 2 review/edge tests. Red-first on untouched 47705d1: 9/9 AC tests red, each for ITS AC's defect (never an import error — legs needing symbols only the implementation creates sit AFTER the red assertion), quoted per AC below.
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected) — `tests/test_f51.py` added beside test_f43–f56; the F-51 (I-1) glob move: `schema/**`, `toolkit/**`, `prompts/**` → `factory/data/**` in `paths.protected`, `schema/**` → `factory/data/schema/**` in `risk.factory_control`. Proven non-weakening: every moved file still matches (the hook's own `_matches_protected`, absolute and relative forms) — see hostile pass.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (protected) — the main `test` job keeps its pinned runner, matrix, `fetch-depth: 0` (the #106 fork-bomb comment intact) and now runs `pytest -q -n auto -m "not live_network"`; new separately named jobs: `live-network` (selects `-m live_network`; selecting zero tests fails the job via pytest's exit 5, so a marker rename can never silently deselect live coverage), `typecheck` (`pyright`), `wheel` (build → fresh venv → install → load the real contract/toolkit/prompts from OUTSIDE the checkout, asserting `site-packages` so the shadowing trap cannot falsely pass). Node stays the `22` alias (m-22 reasoning below).
- `pyproject.toml` — dev extra exact-pinned (`pytest==9.2.0`, `ruff==0.17.4`, `pytest-xdist==3.8.0` unchanged, `pyright==1.1.414`, `build==1.6.1`); `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` gains `addopts = "--strict-markers"`, the one registered `live_network` marker, and `filterwarnings = ["error::DeprecationWarning"]`; `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` ships `data/schema/*.json`, `data/toolkit/**/*`, `data/prompts/**/*` beside `dashboard_static/*`; `[tool.pyright]` = basic over `factory`.
- `factory/data/{schema,toolkit,prompts}` (moved via `git mv`, protected globs followed) — the wheel fix (I-1). Readers re-anchored through `importlib.resources.files("factory").joinpath("data", …)`: `factory/contract.py` SCHEMA_DIR, `factory/toolkit.py` `default_toolkit_dir()`, `factory/prompts.py` PROMPTS_DIR. `factory/session.py`'s dead `_REPO_ROOT` escape (parent.parent, never read) removed; the srt runtime lookup in `factory/sandbox.py` stays checkout-relative by design (node_modules comes from the operated repo's `npm ci`, never from the wheel — allowlisted in test_ac_8 with that reason).
- Protected tests re-seated (path move only, same strength): `tests/test_f01.py` (SCHEMA_DIR anchor), `tests/test_f10.py` (PROMPTS_DIR), `tests/test_f22.py` (TOOLKIT_DIR→`default_toolkit_dir()`, SCHEMA_DIR ×2), `tests/test_f25.py` (SCHEMA_DIR ×2), `tests/test_f26.py` (SCHEMA_DIR ×2), `tests/test_f44.py` (SCHEMA_DIR), `tests/test_f47.py` (`load_index(default_toolkit_dir())`).
- Protected tests changed ONLY in import lines (AC-7): `tests/test_f38.py` (3 function-local f22 imports), `tests/test_f44.py` (2 function-local f07), `tests/test_f47.py` (top f05/f22 + 1 function-local f30), `tests/test_f48.py` (top f16 + 1 function-local f38), `tests/test_f56.py` (top f05/f12 + 2 function-local f05), `tests/test_l0_review.py` (f07), `tests/test_l2_retry_budgets.py` (f23). Protected ORIGIN files additionally lost the moved defs and import them back (each verified byte-for-byte first): `test_f05` (_git, _fixture_repo, _child), `test_f07` (BASE_PROFILE, TASK, _git, _repo, _change, _run), `test_f12` (_profile_yaml, _repo, _git_repo), `test_f16` (_FINDINGS_ARRAY, _finding, ScriptedSeats, MOCK_SEED_FINDINGS), `test_f22` (MIT, SKILL_MD, _sha, _hash_tree, _content_hash, _make_skill, _make_mcp, _write_reports, _write_index, _toolkit_with), `test_f23` (…+Env, Scripted, _passes_review, _review_rounds), `test_f30` (…+Env, Seats), `test_f38` (…+Env, Fanout, _one_round_task).
- `tests/test_f05.py` (protected) — AC-2 split: `test_ac_3` keeps the exact contract name and the mandatory DENY half (non-allowlisted example.com refused by the sandbox's own proxy — local, no internet); the ALLOW half became `test_allowlisted_model_endpoint_is_reachable_from_the_sandbox` (marked live_network); `test_direct_socket_to_non_allowlisted_ip_is_blocked` marked live_network (its "denied" can only be told apart from "offline" on a live network). AC-6: the heartbeat-stability double-sleep became a `wait_until` poll (0.5 s window vs the fixture's 0.2 s write period — no false-quiet, returns as soon as the group dies).
- `tests/test_f06.py` (protected) — AC-3 restructure of `test_ac_3`/`test_ac_4`: the refusal-without-credential half is now mandatory and hermetic on EVERY machine (`seat_token` monkeypatched to None, then `monkeypatch.undo()`), the live half runs only after `live_session_gate()` and `pytest.fail`s loudly when the opt-in is set but no credential exists. Module docstring updated (the old "token-gated" inference prose removed).
- `tests/test_importer_schema_shapes.py` (not protected) — AC-1: the bare-`python3` assertion now calls the shared `no_bare_python3(prompt, sys.executable)`.
- `CLAUDE.md` (protected, owner's file; mechanical edits, both recorded here): reading-order and schema-load lines now name `factory/data/schema/`; the "conventionally deselected (`--deselect`)" sentence replaced by the `FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1` convention (no deselect needed anywhere).
- `README.md` — Setup names the two visible skip exceptions (live opt-in, live_network job) without claiming "never skipped" absolutely, and names the new CI jobs.
- `REVIEW.md` (protected) — the protected-glob example `prompts/**` → `factory/data/**`. `TASK-CONTRACT.md` schema links → `factory/data/schema/…`. `factory/state.py`/`factory/dashboard.py` comments re-pathed.
- `factory/toolkit.py` (protected) — `re.split(r"[/?:#]", rest, maxsplit=1)` (m-23; the only positional count in the package — approve.py:595 already passed `flags=` by keyword; the AST sweep in test_ac_5 enforces the rest).
- AC-9 type fixes across `factory/` (95 pyright-basic errors → 0): annotations (SeatConfig.permission_mode typed with the SDK's PermissionMode; RunResult‑adjacent casts), narrowing (approve.py's two evidence guards rewritten equivalently; contract.py gates binding; l2.py verdict binding; sentinels.py None guard), typed accessors (state.py `_status_of`), two sandbox exception subclasses declaring `force_closed_pipes` (same exception identity for every existing handler), asserts on impossible states matching the repo's established style (gates.py status row, prbody.py section match, prompts.py standards loop, sandbox.py PIPE pair, session.py final record), and casts with reasons at publish-path invariants. 4 narrow rule-named suppressions (allowlist below). No behaviour change except failure CLASS on already-crashing impossible states (AttributeError → AssertionError/ValueError, same loudness).

**Design notes:**

- **The marker and job split (AC-2).** One registered marker `live_network` + `--strict-markers` (a typo'd marker name errors instead of silently selecting nothing). Main job deselects exactly `live_network` (test_ac_2 pins that nothing else is deselected). Live tests kept: `test_allowlisted_model_endpoint_is_reachable_from_the_sandbox`, `test_direct_socket_to_non_allowlisted_ip_is_blocked` — each touches a public endpoint (asserted by source scan). Mandatory deny tests pinned by name in test_ac_2 (each must exist, unmarked): F-05's `test_ac_3` (proxy deny), `test_git_hook_and_config_planting_is_denied_in_sandbox`, `test_planted_hook_never_fires_for_orchestrator_git`, `test_home_reads_are_denied`, `test_sibling_run_reads_are_denied`, `test_settings_deny_reads_for_holdouts_ssh_gh_and_other_runs`, `test_settings_credential_deny_covers_github_and_keychain_names`, `test_settings_allowlist_is_model_endpoint_plus_profile_lists`, `test_local_binding_session_denied_gate_allowed_real_srt`.
- **Opt-in semantics (AC-3).** Exactly `FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1`. No opt-in → the live halves SKIP with the reason naming the variable (visible in `-rs`); the refusal halves still run everywhere. Opt-in + no credential → `pytest.fail` naming `factory-zai` (loud, never a silent skip). The credential lookup is consulted only AFTER the gate — proven by the create_run tripwire (no session attempted) and the hostile bomb probe. The keychain helper itself is unchanged (only its callers' gating).
- **Pins and the Node floor (AC-4).** `pytest==9.2.0`, `ruff==0.17.4` (the versions the gate passes on today, read from the venv), `pyright==1.1.414`, `build==1.6.1`. CI keeps `node-version: 22`: the alias resolves to the NEWEST 22.x, and srt's engines floor (read from package-lock.json: `>=22.12.0`) is a 22.x release, so the alias can never resolve below the floor while staying inside the floor's major — the rule test_ac_4 implements (a bare major N satisfies iff N ≥ the floor's major; concrete versions compare directly).
- **Warnings (AC-5).** `filterwarnings = ["error::DeprecationWarning"]` in config (not a CI CLI flag) — plain `pytest -q`, `-n auto` and CI all inherit it. Zero narrow ignores needed on 3.11 or 3.13 (the full gates ran clean); the config comment states the narrow-ignore policy if a third-party warning ever appears.
- **Sleep classification (AC-6).** test_f05's textual `time.sleep` occurrences after the change: line ~1192 child script `time.sleep(60)` — SUBJECT (the process the timeout kills); ~1197 the sh loop's `sleep 0.2` — SUBJECT (the heartbeat writer that must die); ~1416 child `time.sleep(30)` (background child, must outlive the leader's return) — SUBJECT; ~1443/~1446 detached `time.sleep(60)` pair — SUBJECT (the escapee that must survive killpg); ~1729/~1732 same pair in the partial-output test — SUBJECT. The two PARENT-side waits (old lines 1199/1201) are the ones converted — the AST-level call count in test_f05 is now zero (asserted).
- **The package-data layout (AC-8).** Move (not data-files mapping): setuptools `data_files` install outside the package and are not importlib.resources-addressable — the opposite of the invariant. One root `factory/data/` (schema, toolkit, prompts) — no name clash with the `factory/toolkit.py`/`factory/prompts.py` modules, one package-data glob root, one protected glob. Every reader goes through `importlib.resources.files("factory")`, which resolves identically for this checkout, an editable install and a wheel. Path references that changed: CLAUDE.md (reading order, schema-validate rule), TASK-CONTRACT.md §header, REVIEW.md glob example, factory/state.py + factory/dashboard.py comments, seven test files' anchors (listed above), `.factory/project.yaml` globs, `pyproject.toml` package-data. BUILD-ORDER's historical narrative references stay as written at their time (F-52's truth pass owns prose beyond this WO's forced renames — classified 3 below).
- **Pyright scope (AC-9).** `include = ["factory"]` only: the contract tests are dynamic by design (tmp_path repos, stub seats, subprocess probes) and typing them adds no gate strength for the churn; the choice is recorded here per the brief. 4 suppressions, each narrow + rule-named + reasoned at site: (1) `l0.py` `parser.optionxform = str` — the documented configparser idiom, stubs type the slot as a method; (2) `session.py` `options.plugins` — the SDK accepts plain local-plugin dicts at runtime, `SdkPluginConfig` doesn't model the shape; (3) `session.py` `options.mcp_servers` — dict|str|Path in the SDK, this branch only ever assigns a mapping; (4) `dashboard.py` `server.dashboard` — http.server stubs don't know the attached attribute (converted from a pre-existing mypy-style `# type: ignore[attr-defined]`, which the new policy forbids).

- **AC-9** RED: `AssertionError: [tool.pyright] is not configured`. **Spec notes (reported, not resolved):**

1. **Should `live-network` be a required check?** The owner's call — and on this repo's free plan, branch protection answers 403 (`branch_protection: unavailable` in project.yaml), so "required" is not settable anyway; the factory self-enforces (F-58 AC-5's red-CI guard is the seam to extend to the new jobs). Recommendation: once F-58 lands, treat `live-network` like the main job in the self-enforced green-CI rule, with an outage policy: a red `live-network` with a green main job reads "endpoint down", not "PR broken" — the split exists precisely so those two never masquerade as each other. A fork PR without the Z.ai network path just fails that named job.
2. **AC-3 replacement text (CLAUDE.md, README)** — applied (both outside `.factory/**`, mechanical): CLAUDE.md "Working here" now reads that the two f06 tests run their live halves only on explicit `FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1`, skip with a named reason otherwise, fail loudly with opt-in + no credential, and "no `--deselect` is needed anywhere"; README Setup names the same convention plus the live_network job split. The old `--deselect` convention text is gone from both.
3. **AC-8 move vs data-files** — moved (justification in design notes); every path reference that changed is enumerated there. One residual checkout-relative lookup remains by design and is allowlisted in the test with its reason: `factory/sandbox.py`'s node_modules/srt resolution (the sandbox runtime is npm-provided by the operated repo, never by the wheel). A wheel-installed factory that also needs to SANDBOX is F-58's host-preflight territory.
4. **AC-9 tests/ scope** — excluded, with the reasoning above; if wanted later it is a one-line include change plus its own fix WO (the suite's dynamic style will generate noise, not gate strength).

**Hostile pass (each attack on the final tree, or classified):**

- **Marker registration removed** (mutation 2 below doubles as this): `--strict-markers` turns the now-unregistered `@pytest.mark.live_network` into a collection error (`'live_network' not found in `markers` config`) — the suite refuses to run rather than silently deselecting. Blocked by config + test_ac_2.
- **A mandatory deny test marked live_network** (`test_home_reads_are_denied` marked in a probe): test_ac_2 fails with `mandatory deny test test_home_reads_are_denied was marked live_network — it must stay in the main job`. Blocked by the pinned name list.
- **`FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=true`, `yes`, ` 1` with a credential present**: subprocess runs of both f06 tests → `2 skipped`, `LIVE SESSION ATTEMPTED` absent from every run. Nothing spends. (Also pinned permanently by test_ac_3 scenario 3.)
- **Credential present, no opt-in — is the lookup called to decide?** Probe plugin replaced `factory.session.seat_token` with a bomb (`AssertionError: credential lookup used to decide`) and `factory.runs.create_run` with the tripwire: both tests SKIP, neither bomb nor tripwire fires — the skip decision reads only the env var. The hermetic refusal half monkeypatches the lookup before use; after `undo()` the gate skips first.
- **A DeprecationWarning raised in factory code**: the empirical leg of test_ac_5 runs a warning-raising test under the repo's own config via `-c pyproject.toml` and asserts the non-zero exit — mutation-verified in the AC-5 mutation below by reverting toolkit.py to the positional maxsplit (a real 3.13 deprecation): test_ac_5 red.
- **A blanket `ignore::DeprecationWarning` added to filterwarnings**: test_ac_5's static leg fails (`blanket-ish warning ignore 'ignore::DeprecationWarning' subsumes error::DeprecationWarning`) and its subprocess leg goes green → red. Blocked by both legs.
- **A `# type: ignore` / file-level pyright disable**: test_ac_9 fails on the first (`bare '# type: ignore'`) and on the second (`file-level pyright directive`); suppression count drift fails the exact allowlist (`4` today). Blocked.
- **Wheel check from INSIDE the checkout** (the shadowing trap): from the repo root, `import factory` resolves to the CHECKOUT (`/Users/anthonyforan/Desktop/sfa-f51/factory/contract.py`), and the CI check's `site-packages` assertion FAILS there — an accidentally-inside run cannot falsely pass; the outside run (proven: `factory from: /private/tmp/wheelenv/lib/python3.11/site-packages/factory/contract.py`) is the real one.
- **One resource file dropped from package-data**: mutation 8 below removed `data/toolkit/**/*` — test_ac_8 red on both the coverage compare and the wheel listing. Blocked by two independent legs.
- **A `wait_until` predicate that raises**: pinned in test_ac_6 — `RuntimeError` propagates (`pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match='predicate failure')`), never swallowed, never out-waited.
- **A cross-module test import re-added inside a function body**: test_ac_7 walks ALL Import/ImportFrom nodes at any depth (function-local included — that is how the original f38/f44/f48/f56 locals were caught red). Mutation 7 re-added one and test_ac_7 went red listing it.
- **The moved protected globs**: `_matches_protected` (the session hook's own matcher) on every moved file — all protected=True; schema files also factory_control=True (as before the move); absolute-path form denied too. A session editing `factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json` trips `protected_path_touched` exactly as `schema/**` did.

The reviewer's container run (ubuntu-24.04 per ci.yml) is the Linux verification; CI's three new jobs (live-network, typecheck, wheel) are also unverified until the PR runs them — locally the workflow is validated by YAML parse + the structural assertions in test_ac_2/test_ac_4/test_ac_8/test_ac_9 only.

**The first 3.13 run caught one real environment gap (class 1):** `test_ac_8`'s `python -m build --wheel --no-isolation` failed on 3.13 — Python ≥3.12 venvs bundle NO setuptools, and `--no-isolation` builds with the running env's. The wheel build is now a declared gate tool: `setuptools==84.2.1` exact-pinned in the dev extra (satisfying [build-system]'s `>=68` floor), so every gate env — 3.11, 3.13, CI, a fresh venv — can build offline. Fixed and re-verified on both interpreters (ac_4+ac_8 green on 3.13), then the full 3.13 suite re-run green.

**Gate (final tree, macOS, after every edit — this is the frozen tail):**
- `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`
- `.venv/bin/pyright factory` → `0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 informations`
- **3.11** (`.venv`, Python 3.11.9): `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto -rs --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **1138 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, exit 0** (418.23 s).
- **3.11 without the deselects**: same command minus both `--deselect` → **1138 passed, 2 skipped, exit 0** (453.15 s). The skip summary, line by line: `SKIPPED [2] tests/_support/__init__.py:50: live session half: opt in with FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1 (a credential alone never turns this on)` — exactly the two f06 live halves; no other skip exists (a new skip would be a weakening; there is none).
- **3.13** (fresh /tmp venv, Python 3.13.5): same gate command → **1138 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, exit 0** (412.04 s) — the DeprecationWarning-as-error filter is live on 3.13 (where the positional-maxsplit deprecation the WO was filed against actually fires) and the suite is green on it.
- **Fresh venv**: `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.11 -m venv` + `pip install -e ".[dev]"` → installs clean (pytest 9.2.0, ruff 0.17.4, pytest-xdist 3.8.0, pyright 1.1.414, build 1.6.1, setuptools 84.2.1, software-factory 0.1.0 editable), `import factory` resolves to this checkout.
- **Wheel**: built with the pinned build `--no-isolation`; installed into a second fresh venv; from OUTSIDE the checkout `/tmp/wheelenv/bin/python` loads the REAL `.factory/tasks.json` (60 tasks) + `project.yaml` + the toolkit index (1 item) + the reviewer prompt, and `factory` resolves from `.../site-packages/factory/contract.py` (the shadowing assert passes only out here — proven).
- **Mutation checks**: all 9 run (snapshot → mutate → red → restore from snapshot → green), every one caught: `ALL MUTATIONS CAUGHT`.

**Round 1 (2026-11-07, reviewer findings C1, M2–M5, m6–m8 — all fixed red-first or mutation-proven):**

- **C1 (critical): the process-group wait was VACUOUS.** The reviewer's repro confirmed: the shipped predicate compared a sample against one taken microseconds earlier, and `wait_until` calls before any sleep — `True after 0.00s while writer STILL ALIVE`. First fix attempt had its own bug (the look-clock updated every 0.5 s poll, so the 1.0 s window never accumulated — caught because the REAL test then timed out with a genuine kill). Final shape: a judgement requires ≥1.0 s since the last JUDGED sample (first look never counts; polling faster never widens the window) — five write periods of the 0.2 s fixture. Green with the real kill (5.6 s); **mutation: killpg → leader-only kill → RED** (`timed out after 30s waiting for … the whole process group died`), restored green. Audit of every other conversion: exactly ONE parent-side wait was converted in round 0 (this one — the sleep classification listed all others as subject); no other wait_until/sleep replacement exists in the tree (test_f51's own helper legs use always/never/exploding predicates, not subject-waits).
- **M2: the CI wheel job was broken twice — and untested.** Both reproduced: (a) `build` never installed → `No module named build`; (b) the quoted heredoc left `"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE"` a literal → ContractError. Fixed: the job installs the exact pins (`build==1.6.1`, `setuptools==84.2.1`) and the python reads `os.environ["GITHUB_WORKSPACE"]`. Root cause closed: `test_review_the_ci_wheel_job_steps_actually_run` EXTRACTS the wheel job's run steps from ci.yml and EXECUTES them (controlled PATH so `python` is this venv, scratch HOME outside the checkout for the job's `cd "$HOME"`, GITHUB_WORKSPACE set, `/tmp/wheelenv` re-pointed into tmp_path — the one mechanical substitution; everything else is the job's literal text, including the heredoc). Mutations: heredoc re-quoted → RED (step 4 fails with the job's own contract refusal); install step dropped → RED (`expected the 4 known run steps, got 3`).
