## F-57 — Importer robustness: per-task tool, repair turn, prose eval

The goal (BUILD-ORDER F-57): importing a real repo whose planning docs are PROSE must reliably end in a valid proposal, cheaply, without ever lowering the bar for what reaches the queue. The 2026-11-05 jobfinderos import broke on every schema shape at once because the only check came after one 47 KB answer; each failure then cost a full re-run; and nothing proved a real model can import prose at all.

**Built (owner-directed; every protected change below was directed in this WO's brief, the F-46…F-51 precedent):**

- `factory/importer.py` (not protected today — protection itself is a (4) proposal below):
  - **The `propose_task` tool (AC-1)**: `ProposeCollector` + `make_propose_tool`/`propose_server` (the vent.py pattern — an in-process SDK MCP tool backed by a collector; server and tool both named `propose_task`, qualified `mcp__propose_task__propose_task`). One call validates ONE task against the STAGED proposal schema (`build_proposal_schema` — the same shapes the session self-checks with) plus the per-task import rules (`_task_violations`: readable id, every FORBIDDEN_FIELDS entry, non-draft status, out_of_scope_match naming a real register record), returning `accepted <id>` or EVERY violation (capped like MAX_SHOWN_VIOLATIONS with a `+N more` tail). Re-proposing an id replaces it; the accepted set keeps first-proposal order. Bounds on model-controlled input: `PROPOSE_MAX_CALLS` 1000 / `PROPOSE_MAX_TASKS` 200 / `PROPOSE_TASK_MAX_CHARS` 200,000 / nesting ≤ MAX_PROPOSAL_DEPTH (checked iteratively, before any serialisation) — each a refusal message to the model plus a fact on `collector.facts()`, never a crash. Tasks are seam-scrubbed (scrub_session_value with the seat token) at ACCEPT time, so validation judges the scrubbed values exactly as the text path's will be judged. The set is persisted on every accept to the session scratch's `$TMPDIR/proposal.json` via `_atomic_write_json` — the same file the self-check prompt names and the F-56 abort salvage copies out, so a crash/timeout/quota mid-session keeps what was proposed (the collector lives in the sandboxed entry; the orchestrator never sees the raw file on success).
  - **Precedence (AC-1)**: `_session_proposal` — zero calls → the text answer, today's path exactly; calls made → the accepted set IS the proposal and wins over any text answer, with the divergence stated (summary note on success, refusal message on failure; `ImportResult.precedence_note`).
  - **The ONE repair (AC-2)**: `_repair_refused_proposal` + `build_repair_prompt` + `repair_budget` + `_repair_workspace`. On a refused real-session proposal (parse or plan — both stages): the original output is saved FIRST (the keep-refused writer); ONE repair session then runs in an EMPTY git repo under the project's own runs dir (no doc re-reading, structural — `md_files == []` captured at call time), under the queue's run lease (the reclaim rule), at a quarter of the import's own budget (`REPAIR_BUDGET_FRACTION` 4, floors `REPAIR_TURN_FLOOR` 8 turns / `REPAIR_TIMEOUT_FLOOR` 5.0 min — always inside validate_budget's ceilings by construction), with the saved output + the narrowed schema staged into its scratch and the FULL violation list + the generated contract lines in its prompt. Its answer goes through the SAME `parse_proposal` → egress scrub → `plan_import` chain the original faced. Outcomes, each an `import_repair` trace event with turns: `repaired` (the plan is used; the summary says the repaired proposal is what is confirmed), `still_refused` (the final refusal names BOTH saved files — original and repair — both on disk, 0600), `quota_stopped` (`_is_quota` reused — the deferred-import seam F-48 also uses; F-54 owns promoting it), `failed`/`record_lost`/`workspace_failed`/`could_not_start` (the last three fall back to today's exact keep-refused path — nothing was spent, so nothing is claimed; when the original was already kept, the refusal names THAT file, never saving a second copy). A quota-stopped IMPORT gets no repair (non-completed records raise at the session seam, before any proposal exists to repair). Never a repair of the repair: one call site, no loop, exact call counts pinned by three tests. An injected session (a stub, `--proposal`) never triggers a repair.
  - **I-a** (`_list_planning_docs`): the traversal is now explicit — `os.walk(followlinks=False)` + a containment resolve (`path.resolve().is_relative_to(repo.resolve())`): directory symlinks never followed (no loop can hang it; no dependence on pathlib glob internals that differ across Python versions), a file symlink whose target escapes the repo EXCLUDED (the listing must not advertise a path the committed tree and F-47's read boundary would deny), a file symlink to a file inside the repo still listed. **Supersedes the F-49 pin** in `tests/test_f49.py::test_review_doc_listing_edges` (the escaping lure was listed there; the policy decision was made in this WO's brief — see the inherited-items section).
- `factory/session.py` (protected): `SessionRecord` gains `proposed_tasks`/`propose_facts` (default empty — every pre-F-57 record reads unchanged); `build_options` gains `propose_collector` (attaches the in-process server + the `allowed_tools` grant + the mcp-guard grant in `_apply_toolkit`, exactly like vent); `run_session` stamps them on the record on every exit path; `run_session_sandboxed`/`_run_prepared` carry a `propose` config into the spec; `_main` builds the collector from the spec (schema, record paths, project, the env's token as its scrub secret, `$TMPDIR` as its persist dir). All importer imports in session.py are call-time (the importer imports this module at load — the cycle is real and documented at each site).
- `factory/trace.py` (protected): `import_repair` added to `EVENT_TYPES` (one line; written orchestrator-side only, never accepted from stdout).
- `factory/eval.py` (protected): `run_importer_eval` — ONE import against the prose fixture, propose-only (no queue write), report = schema validity (judged by the real pipeline, repair included) / task count / whether the oversized WO split (its source doc counted). **Live gate**: refuses unless exactly `FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS=1` (the f06 convention — value semantics are the contract; a credential alone never turns it on); `scripted=True` is the offline test seam (the caller has patched the runner — nothing can spend). The prose fixture stays test data under `tests/fixtures/` (deliberately NOT package data — a wheel ships no tests/ and the eval says so when it is missing); its checkout lookup is the second allowed `__file__`-escape, pinned in test_f51's allowlist beside the sandbox's srt lookup.
- `factory/cli.py`: `factory eval --importer` (dispatch `_run_importer_eval`; refusal exit 2 naming the variable; success prints the rendered report + path).
- `tests/_support/f57.py` (new): the prose fixture copier (copytree + git commit — no test writes into the checkout), proposal builders, `_completed` records, and `ScriptedRunner` (stateful `run_session_sandboxed` fake capturing prompt/run_dir/**md_files at call time** — the run dir is deleted before asserts, so a later rglob would be vacuously empty).
- `tests/fixtures/prose-import/` (new, protected): three PROSE work orders — a plain buildable one, an owner-only one (certificate renewal + provider decision), and an eight-deliverable offline mode too big for size M — plus the minimal profile (name `prose-demo`). No JSON, no contract field names (pinned by test).
- `tests/test_f57.py` (new, protected): `test_ac_1`/`test_ac_2`/`test_ac_3` + 23 edge/hostile tests (including the record-transport leg — a mid-stream tool call rides `proposed_tasks` out on a completed AND a died-before-result session, the F-27 vents precedent's core pin, found missing in the self-review). Red-first on untouched 4ce8514: **19 failed** (each an AssertionError naming its AC's defect — never an import error; legs needing new symbols sit behind `hasattr` guards), 6 passed (behaviour pins: not-on-other-seats, set≡text equivalence, quota-on-import, stub-no-repair, fixture-is-prose, symlink-loop — all trivially true before the feature exists, all load-bearing after).
- Protected tests re-seated (owner-directed, this WO's brief): `tests/test_f43.py` (importer egress allowlist + `_session_proposal` — the ONE precedence decision; the text flows onward only through parse_proposal and the set was seam-scrubbed in-sandbox), `tests/test_f51.py` (`_PACKAGE_ESCAPE_ALLOWLIST` + eval.py's `_IMPORTER_FIXTURE_ROOT`), `tests/test_f49.py` (the lure pin superseded — excluded now).
- `.factory/project.yaml` (protected): `tests/test_f57.py` + `tests/fixtures/**` under `paths.protected`.

**Design decisions (stated, per the brief):**

- **The oversized WO is expected to be SPLIT, not flagged.** The prompt already commands it ("a task too big for size M is split into slices or proposed as separate tasks, never sized above the enum") and the queue's own schema enforces it (`budget.size` enum S/M, "L is not allowed: slice it") — so a flag would be a second, weaker copy of a rule the schema already refuses. The scripted repair and the eval report both demonstrate the split (2 tasks sourced from `docs/offline-mode.md`).
- **The accepted set rides the record, not a file.** The collector's set crosses the sandbox boundary as `record.proposed_tasks` (the vents' channel); `$TMPDIR/proposal.json` is the crash-safe persistence and the abort-salvage path, not the success channel (the scratch is swept with the run on success).
- **The repair is text-answer-only** (no propose_task on the repair seat): its whole input is the saved output + violations + contract; the tool would add a second channel to a session whose job is one bounded fix.
- **On a successful repair the original refused output stays on disk** under `<factory-root>/import/` — the repair's premise is that the expensive answer survives; the owner can diff what the first attempt got wrong. (The repair's own output needs no keeping: it became the plan.)
- **Repair-session record loss** (`SessionRecordError`) falls back to today's refusal with a `record_lost` trace event: the turns were spent but there is no answer to validate — claiming a repair outcome without its output would be the F-56 failure wearing a new hat.

GREEN: the accepted set riding the record out (completed and died-mid-stream, F-27's mid-session pin); accept; every-violation (a 3-violation task returns all three, the task id in the head); replace (first-proposal order kept); forbidden fields (each of the 6 named); status; ghost out_of_scope_match; fallback (zero calls → the text answer, unchanged); precedence both ways (a valid set beats broken text — success with the note; an invalid-as-a-whole set beats valid text — refused with the note); bounds (1,005 calls / 10 MB-class task / depth-1,000 / 201st distinct id / NUL+ANSI+token scrubbed); crash-safe persistence + F-56 salvage keeps the collector's file; options wiring (server + allowed_tools + mcp-guard grant) and NOT on implementer/reviewer/verifier without a collector; the prompt teaches the tool with no bare interpreter mention.

No network, no keychain, no srt (the runner seam is scripted; the conftest guard stands).

**Hostile pass (each attack run or classified):**

- **Over-privileged tasks through the tool**: per call, status/each FORBIDDEN_FIELDS entry/ghost out_of_scope_match refused by name (test_ac_1). At the set: a FORGED record (proposed_tasks smuggling `status: ready` + `attempts` + `links` + `approved_by` straight past the collector — the record file is sandbox-written, untrusted) is refused by plan_import's own checks (probe H1: refused, "a proposal must not carry 'approved_by'"). The accepted set NEVER bypasses plan_import — by construction it is the input to it.
- **Existing live task's risk/gates downgrade**: via the accepted set and via the text answer the outcomes are IDENTICAL (probe H2: both accepted — spec fields are proposable, F-12's design; the owner's control is the line-by-line diff review). Equal bar, no new door either way. (A refusal here would have made the tool path DIVERGE from the text path — the attack the goal names.)
- **depends_on a phantom**: identical outcomes via both paths (probe H3: both accepted — the queue schema allows `repo:id` cross-repo refs, so plan_import cannot refuse a phantom statically today; the tool matches the text answer exactly). Classified (3): if phantom-ref enforcement is wanted, it is a plan_import/schema change owning its own WO (it would change text-answer behaviour too).
- **Duplicate id via replace race**: unreachable — the collector is single-threaded (one handler per session on one asyncio loop) and keyed by id; a re-propose replaces, never appends. The text answer's duplicate refusal (F-12, "appears twice") still guards the text path.
- **5,000 calls / 10 MB task / depth 1,000 / NUL+ANSI**: bounded, recorded, no crash, output kept (test_ac_1_bounds_hostile_input; the 1,005-call loop keeps the set at the accepted cap). Memory bound by construction: ≤200 accepted × ≤200 KB = ≤40 MB of record, under F-56's 64 MiB record ceiling.
- **Text vs set disagreement**: the accepted set wins, stated (summary + refusal), both directions tested (test_ac_1_precedence…).
- **Repair re-reads the docs**: structurally impossible — the workspace is an empty repo (no .md anywhere, captured at call time; the settings are the reviewer's read-only sandbox over that empty tree). **Runs over budget**: `repair_budget` is a fraction with floors, strictly under the import's, pinned. **Loops**: no loop exists; exact call counts (1 or 2) pinned in five tests. **Runs after a quota stop**: unreachable — a quota-stopped import never returns a proposal (raised at the session seam); quota DURING the repair stops and saves (trace `quota_stopped`).
- **Weaker repair validation**: mutation 7 (above) proves the same-function rule has teeth.
- **`factory eval --importer` spend**: the shipped CLI path executed without the opt-in → exit 2, the variable named, no factory root created (zero files, zero spend); every non-"1" value refuses with spend bombs armed (in-suite). The opted-in run itself is the owner's manual tick — deliberately NOT executed here.
- **No test spends**: the conftest `_no_ambient_spend` guard stands (keychain stubbed, ambient tokens stripped); every f57 session is scripted at the `run_session_sandboxed` seam; the only live path refuses before any session is constructed. One process-level lesson recorded below (residual).
- **Docs symlink shapes**: directory symlink not followed, escaping file symlink excluded, inside-repo file symlink listed, loop bounded — identical on 3.11 (the repo venv) and 3.13 (a throwaway venv, /tmp/f57-py313): test_f57 25/25 on both. Note: on the CURRENT patch levels tested (3.11.9, 3.13.12) `rglob("*")` already declined to descend directory symlinks (verified empirically — the inherited item's stated 3.11/3.13 divergence did not reproduce); the explicit walk removes the dependence on glob internals anyway, and the containment rule is the real behaviour change (the lure exclusion).

**Inherited items' outcome:**

- **(I-a) `_list_planning_docs` symlinks (from F-49 3b via F-51)**: BUILT. Policy decided per the brief: never follow directory symlinks; exclude a file symlink whose target escapes the repo; keep one whose target is inside. Interpreter-independent by construction (os.walk, followlinks=False) + containment resolve; pinned identically on 3.11 and 3.13. The F-49 lure pin (listed) is SUPERSEDED — `tests/test_f49.py::test_review_doc_listing_edges` updated in this WO (protected test, owner-directed; the supersession recorded here).

The prompt's new tool section adds no interpreter mention (`no_bare_python3(prompt, sys.executable) is None` asserted in test_ac_1_prompt_teaches_the_tool, and tests/test_importer_schema_shapes' own check still passes); the repair prompt uses the same `shlex.quote(sys.executable)` form.

4. **Proposed, not built**: (a) protect `factory/importer.py` under `paths.protected` (F-49 3d's proposal, restated: the importer now judges what reaches the queue from THREE doors — text, tool, repair — and is factory_control in all but name; an owner PR, one line); (b) `factory/propose.py` split if importer.py's size starts costing reviews (deferred until a second consumer exists — the vent.py precedent waited too); (c) ship the prose fixture as package data IF `factory eval --importer` ever runs from a wheel install (today it is deliberately checkout-only, refusing with the path when absent); (d) a `propose_facts` line in `factory status` (the bound-hit facts ride the record today but nothing renders them — the vent weekly view is the natural home).

**Round 1 (2026-11-07, reviewer verdict NOT CLEAN — all findings fixed):**

- **F1 (major) — the accepted set re-bounded host-side.** The record file is session-writable, so `record.proposed_tasks` is untrusted structure; the sandbox's caps bound only the collector. `_accepted_set_proposal` now renders `{"project", "tasks": accepted}` to JSON and pushes it through `parse_proposal` — literally the same function the text answer faces (JSON shape, tasks-a-list, nesting ≤ 64, all BEFORE any deepcopy/`canonical_json`/scrub could recurse) — and re-applies the task-count and per-task-size caps where the record is read (200/200 KB: advisory in the sandbox, enforced at the host). `run_import` takes the record's list RAW (the F1 deepcopy crash point is gone). Every hostile shape (900-deep nesting, `5`, `"PROSE"`, `{"a":1}`, non-dict items, 3000 tasks) is an ImportRefused through keep + repair, output kept — the reviewer's probe file re-run: 7/7 REFUSED, zero crashes.
- **F2 (major) — RunInProgressError can no longer escape.** Both leases (the import session's and the repair's) are taken through `contextlib.ExitStack.enter_context`, whose enter-time `RunInProgressError` is caught: the repair writes a terminal `lease_busy` trace outcome and takes the could-not-start fallback (the kept original named, keep-shaped message); the import session converts it to an ImportRefused ("refuses to race a live run", holder named) — the CLI's caught class. `tests/test_f12.py::test_sandboxed_import_holds_the_run_lease` re-seated accordingly (protected test, reviewer-directed): the busy-queue leg now expects ImportRefused with the same "run is in progress" match. The repair's lease itself is PINNED (the reviewer's m28 mutation): the busy-queue test's lease-busy outcome fails if the repair takes no lease.
- **F3 (medium) — the settings half of "no doc re-reading".** `build_settings` (protected `factory/sandbox.py`, reviewer-directed) gains `deny_read` (resolved paths joined to denyRead); `run_session_sandboxed`/`_run_prepared` plumb it; the repair passes the resolved source repo and its docs dir. The home-wide deny said nothing about a repo outside $HOME — the deny holds wherever the repo lives. Pinned by a test that drives BOTH sessions with only `factory.sandbox.run_sandboxed` faked (the real session machinery writes the real settings): the repair's settings carry both paths under denyRead and nothing re-allows them; the fixture repo lives under the pytest tmp dir — outside $HOME on macOS, so the leg covers the outside-home case for real.
- **F4 (medium) — contract changes on a live task demote.** Decision: **(a) demote to draft**, in TASK-CONTRACT terms: F-03's one X→draft reason is "the approved contract changed", and risk/gates ARE contract fields (the risk⇒human-merge rule rides them; acceptance rides the recomputed hash) — a proposal that changes `acceptance`, `risk` or `gates` on a non-draft task merges the change AND resets the task to draft with approval fields cleared (attempts and history survive), the summary line says "-> draft (the approved contract changed; re-approve after review)". Demoting beats refusing because refusing would make the importer the arbiter of which contract changes are allowed: the import proposes, the owner disposes — and a demoted task runs only after the owner re-approves the very change the diff shows. Identical via the accepted set and the text answer (both live in `_merge_task`); `TaskChange.demoted` carries the fact.
- **F5 (medium) — phantom local depends_on refused.** `plan_import` refuses a proposed task's LOCAL dependency (no `repo:` prefix) that names no task in the MERGED document (queue-carried ids count; cross-repo `repo:id` is never judged), naming task and dep — identical on both paths by construction. Only PROPOSED tasks' deps are judged (a pre-existing dangling dep is the queue's problem, not this import's claim).
- **G1 — the repair's answer passes the egress seam (now pinned).** The scrub was already there; the pin wasn't. `test_g1…`: the repair's text carries the literal seat token and a control sequence — neither reaches tasks.json or the summary; "[redacted]" and the surviving text do.
- **S1 — precedence follows CALLS, not accepts.** `SessionRecord.propose_calls` (stamped from the collector on every exit path): calls>0 with nothing accepted and an unparsable text answer → the refusal states "N propose_task call(s), all refused — every task it proposed broke a rule"; with a parsable text answer → the text is the proposal, the summary states "none accepted". The prompt's "once you have called the tool at all" and the factory's behaviour now agree.
- **S2 — the AC-4 report says how the plan was produced.** `tool_used`/`propose_calls`/`accepted_tasks` (new `ImportResult.propose_calls`/`accepted_count`) plus `repair` (outcome+turns) and `precedence_note`, in the JSON and the rendered md ("produced by: …; repaired (outcome repaired, 11 turns)"); the refused arm carries the same keys null-shaped.
- **S3 — per-run fixture dir.** `eval/importer-fixture-<run-id>` (run ids unique per mint): two concurrent evals never clobber each other; pinned by two scripted runs sweeping two different dirs.
- **S4 — cli.py's duplicated `factory_root` line removed.**

**Round-1 tests:** +14 (6 F1 shapes + the host-caps leg, F2 lease with a real child-process flock, F3 settings, F4 both paths + acceptance arm, G1, S1 both arms, S3) and 2 rewritten (the set-level equivalence and precedence tests now ride the F5 phantom dep — the acceptance-empty scenario they used DEMOTES under F4 and is now the F4 acceptance-arm test). `tests/test_f12.py` lease leg re-seated (above). 40/40 in tests/test_f57.py.

All caught, all restored.

The reviewer's probe file re-run: 7/7 refused, output kept. **Round 2 (2026-11-07, reviewer verdict NOT CLEAN — all findings fixed):**

- **R2-1 (major) — the demotion now IS the factory's rule, shared.** `factory/state.py` (protected, reviewer-directed) gains `contract_change_action(status)`: `"demote"` for a contract-frozen status whose `(status, draft)` edge is legal (`check_transition` guards it), `"flag"` for `done` (reopening verified work is a human decision — TASK-CONTRACT's acceptance row), `"leave"` for `draft` and `not_buildable` (never executable; the change rides the visible diff and the task is already in human hands — the reviewer's own suggestion, adopted). `TaskQueue.demote_changed_contracts` now routes through the SAME helper (one rule, two callers; the audit's stale-hash trigger stays its own). The importer's `_merge_task` follows it: demote keeps `acceptance_hash`/`approved_at`/`approved_by` as HISTORY (re-approval overwrites them — the round-1 popping was wrong); `done` keeps its status and is flagged in the plan/summary ("contract changed on a DONE task: flagged for your review, not reopened" — `TaskChange.flagged`); `not_buildable` and `draft` are left. Probed per status: ready→draft(hash kept), done→done(flagged), not_buildable→left, draft→plain change. Identical via the accepted set and the text answer (both live in `_merge_task`).
- **R2-2 (major) — no record scalar renders raw.** `propose_calls` is read INSIDE the keep/repair region and only a non-bool int ≥ 0 is a count; anything else (a string carrying the seat token, a dict, a bool, a negative) is a forged record → ImportRefused ("malformed propose_calls field (<type>) — the record is unusable") through keep and repair. Decision ledgered: REFUSE, not coerce-to-zero — a lying structural field means the record is broken, the same stance as the F-56 record checks. A huge valid int renders as digits (nothing to leak; anything past the int/str conversion limit already dies at the record's own JSON parse). The repair record's `num_turns` (rendered into the summary, the trace and the eval report) is sanitized at read (`_record_turns`: non-bool int ≥ 0 or None → "an unknown number of turns") — the repair already ran when its record is read, so a garbage count costs the number, not the session. Audit of every other field the importer renders: `proposed_tasks` (the F1 parse boundary — never rendered raw), the accepted-set counts (ints by construction), `record.result` (scrub+parse), `record.reason/status` (scrubbed). The reviewer's `test_probe2.py`: token-str/dict/bool all refused clean, no TOKEN-LEAK.
- **Surviving mutations, all now caught:** n03 (per-task size cap off) → `test_n03…` (one forged task over 200 000 chars, refused "serialises to"); n09/n10 (CONTRACT_FIELDS minus acceptance / minus gates) → `test_r2_1_each_contract_field_alone_demotes` — three legs that each change EXACTLY ONE contract field (the sibling fields preserved against the seed; the gates leg relaxes DEPLOY, not merge — merge-auto with risk present is schema-invalid and would test the wrong thing); n12 (merged_ids from the proposal only) → `test_n12…` (a dep naming an id that exists ONLY in the queue is accepted — load-bearing); n14 (`_carry` stamps 0) → `test_n14…` (the collector→record→importer channel for calls: 3 calls, 2 accepted, ride the record out).

## F-58 — Host preflight, red-CI guard, host-named CI, host table, GitGuardian green

**Owner scope decision (2026-11-08), quoted from the WO brief:** "The production host is the owner's Mac. The factory runs there, for client work too. Ubuntu 26.04 support is DEFERRED. AC-1, AC-2 and AC-7 are NOT built here. AC-3 is amended: per-host job naming on 24.04 only, no 26.04 job." CI stays on Linux (ubuntu-24.04) so the Linux sandbox keeps passing there; the macOS-only skip on `test_nested_suite_skips_instead_of_failing` stays, still visible. AC-G (GitGuardian green without hiding real secrets) is owner-added; its test is named `test_ac_g`.

**Goal:** on the owner's Mac, `factory run` must never work on a host that cannot sandbox, and never work over red CI or call a PR ready while it is red — every refusal ONE named, loud message, not a cascade and not silence. (2026-10-24: CI red for 11 days while PRs #90–#95 merged, because `branch_protection: unavailable` enforced nothing about green CI.)

**Built:**

- **AC-4 — host preflight** (`factory/sandbox.py` ★): `HostPreflightError(SandboxError)` + `preflight_host()` — runs the PINNED `srt_bin()` (absolute, pin-checked, no env override) on `/bin/echo factory-host-probe-ok` with a settings file from the same `build_settings` (profile `{}`, `model_endpoint=preflight.invalid`) in a fresh temp dir (canonical `runs/probe/host-preflight` layout), 60 s wall clock. Outcomes: rc 0 + sentinel → proceed; the exact PR #97 setgroups text → one refusal naming the missing setting (the AppArmor sysctl, 26.04 NOT supported) and README 'Setup'; `sandbox_apply` → one refusal naming the seatbelt (never legitimately nested → refusal, not skip); anything else — srt missing / pin mismatch (SandboxError from `ensure_srt`, naming `npm ci`), timeout, other rc, rc 0 without the expected output — refuses naming what failed. Unknown is never ok. Wired in `run_once` as the `host_probe` seam, mirroring `sdk_probe`: auto (real probe) when the session runner is the default, None with stub seats; called AFTER admission and BEFORE the queue is read, reclaimed, picked or anything recorded (order proven by an instrumented-TaskQueue test). `HostPreflightError` subclasses `SandboxError`, already in the CLI's `_RUN_ERRORS` → one line, exit 2, no new wiring.
- **AC-5 — self-enforced green CI** (`factory/mergeorder.py` ★, `factory/orchestrator.py` ★, `factory/cli.py`):
  - `CIState` + pure `classify_ci(check_runs, status_doc)` + `gh_ci_state(remote, sha)` — a thin gh wrapper injected like `protection`/`pr_state`. Reads the check-runs API for the EXACT SHA (`filter=latest`, so a re-run that went green is green), paginated `per_page=100` (capped at 30 pages → unresolvable beyond, never a subset), AND the combined commit-status API (a repo can report through either). Classification: success = every check success/neutral/skipped; failed = any failure/cancelled/timed_out/action_required/stale (or a red combined status); pending = in_progress/queued; none = no checks at all; unresolvable = gh error, rc-0 junk (`null`/list/HTML/empty), non-object JSON, unknown conclusion/status (F-45: never guessed, never success). gh missing / unexecutable → `PublishError`.
  - Default branch (`_refuse_over_red_ci`, before the queue is read, only under the declaration): resolves the REMOTE default head (`git ls-remote` via the shared `remote_call`), never the local checkout's HEAD. failed → refuse naming the SHA12 and every failing check; unresolvable → refuse (fail closed); none → refuse naming it as the owner's policy question (spec mismatch, below); pending → proceed (a run merges nothing); success → proceed.
  - Stale-PR sweep (`_pr_ready_or_not` at all four `ready` sites): an open PR is reported ready only on green CI for its head SHA; failed/pending/none/unresolvable land on `RunResult.not_ready_prs` ("not ready: <url>: CI failed (live-network=failed, …)") — visible, never silent. The gate runs every tick (CI state changes without the SHAs moving: re-run-then-green works). A `PublishError` from the seam → "not ready: unresolvable", never a crash and never ready.
  - Only under `repo.branch_protection: unavailable`. With real protection the seam is NEVER called (call-count-proven). ALL checks count — live-network and GitGuardian included (F-51 open question 1, resolved as its ledger recommended: the message names the job, so an endpoint outage reads as `live-network` failing, not a broken PR).
- **AC-3 (amended) — CI names its host** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` ★): all four jobs renamed to carry their host — `test-ubuntu-24.04`, `live-network-ubuntu-24.04`, `typecheck-ubuntu-24.04`, `wheel-ubuntu-24.04`; `runs-on: ubuntu-24.04` stays explicit, the PR #97 comment restated as the standing reason (26.04 known-broken, deferred to F-61, changing the image must be a deliberate edit; the literal floating-alias name is kept OUT of the file so the no-alias test can pin its absence). Every F-51 invariant kept: exactly-`live_network` deselect, zero-selected-fails live job, fail-fast off, matrix 3.11/3.13 unchanged.
- **AC-6 (amended) — the host table matches CI** (`README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` ★): README's table and CLAUDE.md's hosts bullet (prose made an explicit short list — the AC wording says "table" for both, a mismatch reported below) list exactly the verified hosts: macOS (the factory's host, honestly stated as proven by the owner's local gate — CI has no macOS job) and Ubuntu 24.04 (CI); Ubuntu 26.04 NOT supported, with a pointer to F-61. `test_ac_6` checks BOTH directions for Ubuntu in BOTH documents (every supported version has a CI job on that host; every CI job host is documented as supported; "not supported" rows never count).
- **AC-G — GitGuardian green, nothing hidden** (`tests/_support/tokens.py` NEW, six protected test files converted):
  - Root cause confirmed: high-entropy literals (the SEAT-TOKEN-zq91… seat body, the jwt.io example JWT, hex secrets) in test sources — described, never quoted: round 1 (R1-2) caught this ledger quoting the very token the helper removed, and the lint now scans every tracked file — GitGuardian's generic detector flags every PR that adds one.
  - `tests/_support/tokens.py`: `fake_token` (SEAT-TOKEN- + 24-char body, 35 total), `fake_aws_key` (AKIA + EXACTLY 16 upper/digit — the sentinel pattern is `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\b`, a longer body would break detection), `fake_stripe_key`, `fake_jwt` (two eyJ-prefixed base64url segments + signature, unpadded — matched by the sentinel's own pattern), `fake_zai_key` (`<24 hex>.<20 alnum>`, no eyJ, no known prefix). Deterministic per label (hashlib), distinct across labels; alphabets built from `string` so the helper itself holds no literal.
  - Converted (value-shape preserved; each file's tests pass unchanged, and M11 proves the shape-dependence has teeth): test_f25 (the canonical AWS-docs example key, AKIA…EXAMPLE — 7 sites incl. the output-cut regression, now `AWS_KEY[-9:]`), test_f30, test_f33 (the env-exemption fallback value), test_f43 (seat-tok hex → 48-char body, JWT, ZAI key, the jwt2/bearer shape fixtures), test_f56 (tok-secret-), test_f57 (module constant + 8 per-test locals).
  - The lint (mechanical, in CI, part of `test_ac_g`): ast-walks EVERY `tests/**/*.py` — no exclusions, the helper included — for (a) credential-prefixed literals (16+ body), (b) ≥24-char runs with Shannon entropy ≥ 4.1 and ≥2 character classes, (c) the `<hex20+>.<alnum16+>` id.secret shape; fails naming file:line. Calibrated: catches every old literal and a realistic random token (H7a); passes prose, paths, run ids, hex shas and base64-of-text (f33's 22-letter alphabet fixture is below the length threshold and stays, by design). NO `.gitguardian.yaml` (preferred fix needs none; absence pinned by `test_ac_g_no_gitguardian_ignore_file`) — a real secret under `factory/` is ignored by nothing.

- The remote default head moves between the CI lookup and the pick: the guard's answer is a snapshot of exactly the SHA it resolved — a later-moving head cannot make that SHA's answer green (a SHA's checks are immutable); the next run re-resolves and re-asks. Readiness lines name the sha12 they were judged on; the PR gate asks about the PR's own head SHA fetched in the same tick. Never ready on an old SHA's result.

- GitGuardian SUCCESS on this WO's own PR — only provable on the real PR (owner step after review-clean); every line this WO adds was built to carry no high-entropy literal, and the lint keeps it that way.

- **One accidental real session**: while debugging the empty-tasks fixture bug, I ran the Env harness ONCE outside pytest (no `_no_ambient_spend` guard): it completed one real implementer session against the Z.ai seat on the trivial fixture repo (pr_opened against the fixture's fake remote; no real PR, no real push). Cost: one small session. Lesson: Env-style harnesses run under pytest only — the guard is the boundary.

**Round 1 (2026-11-08, reviewer: NOT clean — 3 must-fix + 5 smaller; all fixed red-first, each mutated):**

- **R1-1 (critical, AC-5): green CI read as PENDING on real GitHub.** GitHub's combined-status API answers `{"state": "pending", "statuses": [], "total_count": 0}` for EVERY commit with no legacy statuses — which is every commit in this repo; classify_ci read the rollup, so a fully green main read "pending" and no PR could ever be reported ready (and the "none" refusal was unreachable). The reviewer proved it by running gh_ci_state against real GitHub (main fddca65, all five checks green → "pending"); my canned fixtures used a `state: success` rollup shape GitHub never returns — that is why every test passed while the code was wrong. FIX: the status API is judged by its ENTRIES, never its rollup — zero entries contribute nothing; each entry failure/error → failed, pending → pending, success → green, anything else → unresolvable. The AC-5 fixtures are now trimmed copies of the reviewer's recorded real responses (/tmp/f58gh/): the green pair reads success, the GitGuardian-red pair (fd041c2) reads failed naming "GitGuardian Security Checks". RED first: real-green → "pending" on the pre-fix tree. MUTATION: rollup-as-truth restored → real-green RED, restored. Every canned shape re-audited against the recorded documents (total_count, check_runs entries, status entries).
- **R1-4: `waiting`, `requested`, `pending` check-run statuses (valid GitHub values — e.g. a required environment approval) were unresolvable**, refusing the run over a healthy awaiting check. Now PENDING; each status tested. Mutation (dropped from the set) → RED.
- **R1-5: malformed status entries were silently skipped** (non-dict entry, missing context, missing state, non-list statuses). Unknown is never ok: all are UNRESOLVABLE now, each tested. Mutation (silent `continue` restored) → RED.
- **R1-6: the CI guard ran before the peak-window return** — an in-window tick made gh lookups (ls-remote + 2+ gh calls) and could raise over a red main, against F-26 round-1 finding 5 (no gate work, no gh lookups, no escalations inside the window). DECISION: the guard moved to AFTER the peak-window return and BEFORE the sweep — the same invariant, and a red main is enforced at the first off-peak tick; off-peak it still precedes every side effect (sweep, concurrency lookups, recording). The host preflight stays before the queue read (local, no network — a broken host refuses even in-window). RED first: in-window + red main raised instead of returning peak_window. Mutation (guard moved back up) → RED. Pinned by test_r1_ci_guard_respects_the_peak_window.
- **R1-2 (AC-G on this very PR): the §F-58 ledger re-quoted the literal** (`SEAT-TOKEN-zq91…` whole, and the AWS example key) — GitGuardian scans every added line of every file, and the lint scanned only tests/*.py. FIX: the literals are described, never quoted; the lint now scans EVERY git-tracked text file (.py through the ast AND a raw-line pass over every file, prefix+entropy+id.secret per line) with TWO named exclusions, each with its stated reason — `corpus/**` (the F-25/F-33 attack corpus is credential-shaped BY DESIGN — it is the detector's test data) and `package-lock.json` (npm's machine-generated integrity hashes; their unbroken base64 pieces are shape-indistinguishable from tokens — the exclusion is the named FILE, never a value rule, and factory/** is never excluded, pinned by test). The extended lint immediately caught TWO MORE real literals my old rule could not see: sequential-digit `ghp_` fakes in tests/test_f27.py (entropy too low for the entropy rule, invisible to a line-start prefix check) — converted to a runtime-built `ghp_`-shaped token, the scrub assertions unchanged and still passing. MUTATIONS: a SEAT-token planted mid-line in README → RED; a bare random token in a doc line → RED; both restored green.
- **R1-3: the lint had no teeth per rule** — each branch disabled singly survived (M10 only proved the combination). FIX: test_r1_lint_rules_each_have_teeth feeds _token_like per-rule positives assembled from fragments (a prefixed token, a 32-char random mixed run, a hex.alnum id.secret) and documented negatives (40-hex sha, the factory run id, paths, a UUID). Calibrations the test forced, each principled: (a) a run carrying the factory's timestamp signature (`\d{8}T\d{6}Z`) is a generated run id — its measured entropy (4.24) is INDISTINGUISHABLE from the old token body's (4.24), so the shape is the only honest tell; (b) runs containing `_` or `-` are NAMES (test_review2_…), not secrets — secret bodies are unbroken blobs; (c) the prefix rule SEARCHES rather than startswith (a token quoted mid-line — exactly R1-2 — must flag). MUTATIONS: prefix branch disabled → RED; entropy threshold 99 → RED; id.secret rule disabled → RED; separator exemption disabled → the repo scan RED (every test name flags).
- **R1-7: tests/test_f05.py's macOS-only skip reason still said "Linux nesting detection is F-58"** — now names the proposed F-61 and the owner decision (owner-directed reason-string edit to a protected file; nothing else in test_f05 changed).
- **R1-8 (honesty):** recorded here, not hidden — gh_ci_state was WRONG against live GitHub, and the canned tests were green over the wrongness; it was caught by the reviewer's live probe, not by my tests. Lesson added to the NOT-verified list: canned fixtures for an external API must be TRIMMED COPIES OF RECORDED REAL RESPONSES, never shapes imagined from docs. The earlier claim "every line this WO adds was built to carry no high-entropy literal" was false as stated (the ledger itself quoted one) — the extended lint plus this round's conversions are what make it true now, re-proven by the diff-wide scan in the round-1 report.

**Round 2 (2026-11-08, reviewer final sweep: NOT clean — 2 items, both test/ledger-only, no production change; the reviewer live-verified the R1-1 fix against real GitHub — fddca65 → success, fd041c2 → failed naming GitGuardian — and independently re-scanned all 796 added lines (0 hits, its own wider detector agreeing with ours plus the documented run-id exemption)):**

- **R2-1: classify_ci's failed-beats-pending priority was UNPINNED** — the reviewer's mutation (pending returned first) survived 39/39. Goal-level hole: GitHub's own rollup calls a failure+pending commit "failure"; a flipped order would read a red main with one concurrently re-running check as "pending", the run-start guard would PROCEED, and `factory run` would start over red CI — the incident class this WO exists to close (the sweep side is fail-safe; the guard is the hole). FIXED: test_r2_failed_beats_pending pins BOTH surfaces — a failed check-run + an in_progress check-run on one SHA → failed, and a failure status entry + a pending entry → failed. MUTATION (the reviewer's flip, re-applied) → RED, restored. 40/40.
- **R2-2: the red fixture was not the recording whole** — the ledger claimed the fixtures were "trimmed copies of the recorded real responses, re-audited", but _R1_GG_RED_CHECKS dropped the typecheck entry (5 checks/total 5; the recording has 6/6). Behaviorally irrelevant (live-verified), dishonest as documented. FIXED the fixture side: the exact recorded document — wheel, test (3.11), typecheck, test (3.13), live-network, GitGuardian=failure, total_count 6 — with `len(state.checks) == 6` pinning the shape. MUTATION (typecheck dropped again) → RED, restored.
- Residual recorded from the reviewer, agreed: the lint is deliberately narrower than GitGuardian (separator-carrying runs are exempt — a hyphenated sk-…-style key would pass the lint); the real PR's GitGuardian run is the final arbiter, and that stays on the owner's step.

**Round 2 verdict (2026-11-08): CODE CLEAN** — the reviewer re-verified everything itself (its priority-flip mutation RED in 0.56s on the new pin; the red fixture matching the /tmp/f58gh recording exactly; production files byte-identical to round 1; its own independent 802-line scan, 0 hits; gates 1261/2/exit 0 on both interpreters). Tree review-clean, uncommitted; commit/PR/merge is the owner's call.

**Harness note (reviewer's process finding, adopted — it manufactured a false gate failure on this very machine today): length-preserving mutations restored within the same wall-clock second POISON `__pycache__`.** A mutate→pytest→restore cycle whose swapped text is exactly the same length, with the restore landing in the same second as the compile, leaves a .pyc built from the MUTATED source whose recorded mtime(seconds)+size match the restored file — Python then executes mutated bytecode against correct source indefinitely (observed: classify_ci answering "pending" while the disk read failed-first; the reviewer's first 3.11 gate failed 1 test on a clean tree until the cache was purged). Our gates ran before any such compile, so our counts stand — but the harness carries the same trap: it can manufacture false REDs (stale pyc "catches" a mutation) or false GREENs (restored tree executing mutated bytecode). RULE for every future mutation leg and every post-restore gate: purge `__pycache__` (or run with PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1) after the restore, before the proof run.

**Round 3 (2026-11-08, caught ON THE REAL PR — neither local tests nor either reviewer saw it): GitHub REJECTED ci.yml; zero jobs ran on 0eb733c.** The run's name was the bare file path (GitHub could not read the workflow); only GitGuardian reported — and it PASSED, so AC-G's proof landed anyway. CAUSE: the AC-3 job ids contained dots (`test-ubuntu-24.04`…); GitHub job ids must match `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_-]*`. The YAML parses locally, so every shape test — mine and the reviewer's — passed: the file was checked against OUR rules, never against GitHub's ACCEPTANCE rules. FIX (follow-up commit on the branch, no amend/force-push): ids dot-free (`test-ubuntu-2404`, `live-network-ubuntu-2404`, `typecheck-ubuntu-2404`, `wheel-ubuntu-2404`), each job carrying a display `name:` with the exact host (`test (ubuntu-24.04)` — matrix legs report as `test (ubuntu-24.04) (3.11)`); AC-3 now asserts the DISPLAY name contains the runs-on host; test_f51 re-seated (six refs; f24 was already content-based); README/CLAUDE.md reference the display name. NEW test (would have caught this): test_r3_github_accepts_the_workflow — every job id fully matches `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_-]*`, every job has runs-on, steps a non-empty list, each step EXACTLY one of run/uses (the offline-checkable GitHub rules). RED first (the dotted ids failed it); MUTATIONS: a dot back in one id → RED; a display name dropped → AC-3 RED; a step with both run and uses → RED. LESSON (the third of the WO, each costlier than the last): a workflow edit needs a GitHub-ACCEPTANCE check, not only shape tests — the same class as R1-1 (canned shapes vs recorded reality): our validators judge what WE wrote down, and GitHub is the only oracle for its own rules. SCOPE (4) PROPOSAL, not built here: run actionlint in CI (pinned) so every future workflow edit is checked against the real workflow grammar at review time, before a push can kill every job.

## F-62 — Host-boundary escapes (trusted srt launcher, push-content secret fence, project-slug rule)

**Source:** security audit run-2 (2026-11-09, at 0f08664; evidence owner-held, outside the repo). Three of its four confirmed highs share one shape — a lower-trust file or string reaches a trusted host action: a gate command swaps the binary that launches the sandbox (`factory/sandbox.py:run-sandboxed:srt-launcher-PATH-from-run-writable-venv`), the seat token rides the pushed commit to the client's remote (`factory/publish.py:check_pr_text:push-tree-never-scanned-for-literal-seat-token`), and the queue's `project` string chooses where under the factory root — or outside it — the factory writes and reads (`factory/importer.py:run-import-queue-project-unvalidated-path-joins`).

**Goal:** nothing a sandboxed command writes can change what executes outside the sandbox; no egress secret the orchestrator holds leaves in pushed objects; no factory-root path component built from a project name is anything but `[a-z0-9-]+`.

**Built:**

- **AC-1 — launcher from trusted paths** (`factory/sandbox.py` ★):
  - `_resolved_on_path(name)` (lru_cache, the srt-pin-cache pattern): resolved ONCE per process from the orchestrator's own `os.environ["PATH"]` — never a child env, never a venv. `trusted_binary(name, run_dir, required)`: refuses (named SandboxError, naming which boundary) any realpath inside the run dir or inside the factory root (derived exactly as `build_settings` derives it: parent of the `runs/` dir); `required=False` returns None (uninstalled bwrap/socat/rg stay srt's own error, today's behaviour). `srt_launch_argv(run_dir, srt_bin_path)`: THE one argv prefix `[node, realpath(cli.js)]` — the launch (`… --settings … -c …`) and the pin check (`_verified_srt` now execs exactly `[trusted node, realpath(cli), --version]`) share it; a `srt_bin_path` realpadding into the run dir is refused like the node.
  - `run_sandboxed`: trust check BEFORE the pin check execs anything; srt exec'd as `[*launch_argv, "--settings", …, "-c", command]` with a LAUNCHER env — today's child env with PATH stripped of every run-dir entry (`_path_without_run_dir`, realpath-compared, order kept) and VIRTUAL_ENV popped. The child's venv reaches the confined command only through the `-c` string, as the shell assignment prefix TMPDIR already was: `TMPDIR=… PATH=<venv>/bin:$PATH VIRTUAL_ENV=<venv> <cmd>`. HOME/CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR/GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR (run-dir scratch the command needs) and the seat token stay in env — env is not an exec-resolution vector and the token must never move to argv (visible in ps). `.venv/bin` is NOT denyWritten (gate `pip install` writes console scripts there; the fix is that nothing in `.venv` is ever looked up by the launcher).
  - Linux settings pins (`build_settings`, `sys.platform == "linux"`): `bwrapPath`/`socatPath` (absolute, resolved by the same trusted rule — srt 0.0.79 `whichSync`es them otherwise) and `ripgrep.command` (srt spawns `rg` by name for its mandatory-deny scan). Verified against the pinned srt source: the keys exist in 0.0.79's `SandboxRuntimeConfigSchema`; `seccomp.applyPath`/`javaAgentJarPath` default to the package's own vendored paths (absolute, not PATH-resolved) — left unpinned by design. macOS: NO settings keys exist for `bash` (whichSync), `env`, `which`, `log` — the stripped launcher PATH is the control (comment in code); `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` is invoked absolutely by srt.
  - Test stand-ins are JavaScript now (the launcher exec's `[node, cli.js]`): shared `tests/_support/srt.py` writer with the REAL cli.js shebang on line 1 — a PATH-resolved launch (the pre-fix shape, and the mutation check) execs it exactly the way the real one was. Ports: test_f05 (`_fake_srt`, the caching fake), test_f30 (both line-emitting fakes, incl. the async chunk-split), test_f47 (`_srt` shim), test_f56 (shim), test_f58 (`_fake_srt` battery: ok/setgroups/seatbelt/silent/weird/hang/shim).
- **AC-2 — push content scan** (`factory/publish.py` ★, `factory/orchestrator.py` ★): `check_push_content(root, base_sha, head_sha, *, secrets)` called in the orchestrator directly after `check_pr_text` and before `push_branch`. Reads with hardened git (`GIT_HARDENING`): `git diff --text --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-renames -U0 base..head` — the WHOLE output (headers and file paths included, not just `+` lines; `--text` is the load-bearing visibility flag: without it a binary payload prints "Binary files differ" and the content is invisible — probed) — plus `git log --format=%H%x1f%B base..head`. Forms per secret (short/empty skipped via `is_scrubbable_secret`, MIN_SECRET_LEN=8 — the ONE rule shared with check_pr_text/egress): literal; hex lower+upper; URL-percent-encoded; base64 and base64url at all 3 byte alignments — the "core" chars whose 6-bit windows lie inside the secret's bit range (start ⌈8a/6⌉, end ⌊8(a+n)/6⌋), so `ab+<token>` mid-stream is caught (edge chars that depend on neighbouring bytes are trimmed). Refusal is `PushRefused` (the same type → the existing `publish_refused` escalation path), names the file or commit SHA12 and the encoding, and echoes no secret form — the file NAME itself is needle-redacted when the token is IN the path. `--no-textconv`/`--no-ext-diff` refuse nothing on their own but are mandatory against a repo `.gitattributes` textconv or a global diff driver running commands at diff time (both tested with active hiding configs).
- **AC-3 — project slug everywhere** (`factory/runs.py`, routed through every builder):
  - `project_component(name)` in runs.py: `re.fullmatch(r"[a-z0-9-]+", name, re.ASCII)`, raises `ProjectSlugError(RunError)`, never sanitises. `sandbox_settings_path(root, project, run_id)` NEW — the `<root>/sandbox/<project>-<run_id>.json` join now built in ONE place (create_run, the importer repair path, session's `_derive_settings_path`).
  - Routed builders: `create_run` (runs/ join + settings), `sandbox_settings_path` (3 callers), `trace_path`, `prune_traces`, `ProgressLog.for_task` (project only — task ids keep `safe_name`; they are not project names), `vent.weeks_vents`, `orchestrator.reclaim_runs` (validated value reused for the cleanup settings name), orchestrator reports dirs ×2, `importer._save_refused_output` (import/), `importer._repair_workspace` (runs/), `status._week_escapees` (trace/ read), dashboard overview trace dir + `stream_events` run dir, eval fixture project (literal, routed anyway), `cli._run_trace` run dir. NOT routed (constants/no untrusted input): `session.py` sdk-probe `runs/probe/sdk-import` literal; eval's stamp-named settings/report files; prompt/heading strings that interpolate the project into TEXT (importer prompt builders, status heading).
  - `tasks.schema.json`: `project` gets the profile's `^[a-z0-9-]+$` + minLength 1. The schema ALONE still admits `"abc\n"` (Python re `$` matches before a trailing newline) — `validate_queue` adds the `project_component` fullmatch (load-bearing, proven by test), and `_check_profile_semantics` the same for the profile `name`. Pair checks (queue `project` ≠ profile `name`, both named): `run_once` (RunError, before reclaim/pick/record), importer `run_import`'s profile-load point (ImportRefused, before the session starts), dashboard `_queue` (ContractError → 503 error page).
- `.factory/project.yaml` ★: `tests/test_f62.py` added to `paths.protected` beside `tests/test_f58.py`.

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief): factory/sandbox.py ★, factory/orchestrator.py ★, factory/publish.py ★, factory/trace.py ★, factory/session.py ★, factory/contract.py ★, factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★, .factory/project.yaml ★, tests/test_f47.py ★ + tests/test_f56.py ★ + tests/test_f58.py ★ (stand-in ports to node-run JS — the exec-model change forced them), factory/runs.py, factory/cli.py, factory/dashboard.py, factory/progress.py, factory/status.py, factory/vent.py, factory/eval.py, factory/importer.py, tests/test_f05.py + tests/test_f30.py (same port), tests/_support/srt.py (NEW), tests/test_f62.py (NEW, protected).

**Red first** (tests/test_f62.py alone on untouched af67f18; 8 failed, 1 passed, 4 errors in 10.57s — the 4 errors are the fixture's dependency on the not-yet-existing `_resolved_on_path` cache; the passing one is the argv/token invariant pin, green by construction):

- AC-2: `ImportError: cannot import name 'check_push_content'` (no fence existed); e2e `assert result.outcome == "needs_human"` — the token-carrying branch PUBLISHED (`pr_opened`) with every existing fence green.
- AC-3: `ImportError: cannot import name 'ProjectSlugError'`; `factory status` accepted `../../outside` (exit 0, rendered); the pair mismatch ran a full session instead of refusing.

**Green:** tests/test_f62.py 13 passed. Gates (worktree .venv): `ruff check .` clean; `pyright --warnings` 0 errors/0 warnings; `pytest -q -n auto` **1275 passed, 2 skipped** (the two live-only f06 halves; no FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS set, no credential, no GitHub/Z.ai contact).

**Mutation table** (each restored, `__pycache__` purged, re-run green):

| AC | Mutation | Result |
|----|----------|--------|
| 1 | PATH-resolved launch restored (`[binary, …]`, `env=child_env`) | RED — test_ac_1 (planted node ran) |
| 1 | `env=child_env` only (argv kept) | RED — launcher-env test (run-dir PATH + VIRTUAL_ENV visible to srt) |
| 2 | `--no-textconv` dropped | ABSORBED (green) — `--text` independently forces raw content; not a hole, a second layer (probes below) |
| 2 | `--text` dropped (the load-bearing flag) | RED — binary-payload case: "Binary files differ" hides the token, DID NOT RAISE |
| 2 | hex-upper form dropped | RED — the hex-upper case |
| 3 | `fullmatch` → `match`+`$` | RED — `"abc\n"` admitted |
| 3 | `trace_path` routing skipped | RED — the builder case |

Textconv probes (why the absorbed mutation is a layer, not a hole): with `--text`, raw content shows even with an active hiding textconv; without `--text`, a BINARY file's content is invisible regardless of textconv — `--text` carries visibility, `--no-textconv` prevents driver-configured commands/noise at diff time. Both flags kept, each pinned (the binary case pins `--text`; the textconv/ext-diff cases pin that a configured hider changes nothing).

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; scope class per item):
- Planted `<run>/.venv/bin/node`+`bwrap`+`socat` via gate command 1, command 2 next → marker never appears, stand-in received the trusted node (AC-1 test itself). Class: fixed here.
- Planted `.venv/bin/env` and `bash` (srt's macOS wrapped command resolves both via which()) + real srt → command works, marker absent (real-srt test). Class: fixed here.
- PATH entry pointing into the run dir → named refusal (run dir / factory root / missing-node variants each tested); symlinked node (target inside run dir) refused, symlink to the REAL node allowed (it executes the real node); symlinked `srt_bin_path` whose realpath lands inside the run dir refused. TMPDIR is popped from the launcher env (pre-existing); HOME points at run-dir scratch but nothing in the launch path resolves executables through HOME — no consumer to probe. Class: fixed here.
- Launcher env leaking run-dir PATH to srt's own children → the stand-in (running as srt does) reports its own env: no run-dir entry, no VIRTUAL_ENV; the confined command still gets the venv via the `-c` prefix (`command -v python` resolves into the venv). Class: fixed here.
- Token split across two diff hunks / two commits → KNOWN LIMIT (class 2, AC note): a split leak defeats any needle scan; the literal+simple encodings are the boundary the audit's own remediation draws ("shape scanning is inherently bypassable by encoding").
- gzip / reversed encodings → KNOWN LIMIT (class 2, AC note): same boundary argument; not matched, documented.
- Huge diff → measured: ~50MB diff scans in 0.95s at ~285MB peak RSS (~6× the diff text; `remote_call` captures the whole output, then per-line scanning — NOT streamed). Same posture as every other git call in publish.py; a streaming scan is a class-4 proposal if the owner wants it.
- Project `"-"` / leading dash → `create_run("-")` lands at `<root>/runs/-/<id>`, settings `<root>/sandbox/--<id>.json` — inside the root, on absolute paths, never a bare argument (probed on a real repo). `"-"` matches the profile's own `^[a-z0-9-]+$`; refusing it would TIGHTEN past the AC — left allowed, noted (class 2).
- Every builder NOT routed → listed in the AC-3 bullet above (constants and text interpolations only). Class: none (inventoried).

**Spec mismatches reported (not resolved here):**
1. srt 0.0.79 binary keys: `bwrapPath`/`socatPath`/`ripgrep.command` exist and are pinned (Linux); `seccomp.applyPath`/`javaAgentJarPath` exist but default to vendored package paths — nothing resolved through PATH, left unpinned. macOS has NO settings keys for `bash`/`env`/`which`/`log` — cannot pin; the stripped launcher PATH is the control.
2. "No existing minimum secret length" was a candidate — one EXISTS: `sentinels.MIN_SECRET_LEN = 8` via `is_scrubbable_secret`, already the one rule shared by check_pr_text and the egress seam; reused, no new rule added.
3. The brief lists `approve` among queue+profile pair sites: `approve.py` loads NO profile (it receives `TaskQueue` instances — slug-checked at construction); there is no pair to compare there. Same for the `factory status` CLI (queue only; its hostile-queue protection is the loader refusal, tested).
4. The brief's AC-2 mutation "drop `--no-textconv`" is absorbed by `--text` (see probes) — the red-bearing mutation is dropping `--text`, which is pinned by the binary-payload case.

**What could NOT be verified here:** real srt on Linux (bwrap/socat/rg pins exercised only through `sys.platform` monkeypatching — no Linux host in this session; CI's ubuntu-24.04 job runs the suite where the branch keys land in real settings); the real-srt test runs on this Mac's seatbelt stack only. The token-split and gzip/reversed limits are inherent to the needle-scan boundary, not untested code paths.

**Reconcile markers (added 2026-11-10; evidence: each contract test green on main 8dedaaa and on the merge PR's CI, merged #115 (230c797)):**

- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f62.py::test_ac_1`
- **AC-2** proven: `tests/test_f62.py::test_ac_2`
- **AC-3** proven: `tests/test_f62.py::test_ac_3`

**Status to set at reconcile:** `done` — F-62 `done` (3 ACs, `tests/test_f62.py::test_ac_1/2/3`, all green; evidence above), `depends_on` F-05/F-43/F-49 unchanged.

**Round 1 (2026-11-09, reviewer: NOT clean — 2 holes, both confirmed on this tree by reproduction before fixing):**

- **R1-H1 (AC-2, high): the scan read the NET DIFF, but the push sends every OBJECT.** `git diff base..head` shows the tree delta; `git push` transfers every object reachable from head that the remote lacks — intermediate commits' trees and blobs, and every parent of a merge. The reviewer's two probes both passed the round-0 fence with the token in the published objects: add-then-delete (commit 1 adds `leak.txt`, commit 2 deletes it — net diff clean) and an orphan branch merged `--allow-unrelated-histories -s ours` (net diff empty; `merge-base --is-ancestor` still holds, so `_commit_work` accepts the head). RED first: both cases (plus token-only-in-an-intermediate-tree-entry-name and token-in-an-intermediate-commit's-AUTHOR-identity, which the old `git log --format=%B` never covered either) recorded RED against the round-0 implementation, then fixed. **FIX (class 1): the scan is now over the OBJECT SET** — `git rev-list --objects base..head`, every object streamed through ONE hardened `git cat-file --batch`: blobs raw, trees raw (an entry NAME carrying the secret is caught in the tree's bytes), commits raw (message AND author/committer). `cat-file` serves object bytes — it never applies a textconv or diff driver, so the round-0 `--no-textconv`/`--no-ext-diff` reasoning (and its mutation row) is moot by construction; the textconv/ext-diff cases stay as pins that no repo diff config changes what the scan sees. Objects are scanned STREAMED in 1 MiB chunks (`PUSH_SCAN_CHUNK`) with a longest-needle − 1 byte overlap (a needle cannot straddle a chunk boundary — pinned by a blob with the token exactly across the boundary, red under the no-overlap mutation). Fail closed on rev-list failure, a missing object, an unreadable header, EOF mid-object, a stall past the remote-call timeout, or a non-zero cat-file exit. The refusal names the object kind, its path (needle-redacted when the path itself carries the form) or commit SHA12, and the encoding. Measured (round 0 → round 1): ~50MB publish 0.95s/285MB RSS (whole-diff-in-memory) → **0.02s/37MB baseline** (streamed; nothing buffered whole) — the round-0 "not streamed" known limit is gone.
- **R1-H2 (AC-1): relative and empty PATH entries survived the strip.** `_path_without_run_dir` resolved each entry against the ORCHESTRATOR's cwd, but srt runs with `cwd=run_dir` — a relative entry (`.`/`bin`/`node_modules/.bin`, common on a developer's PATH) searches the RUN DIR at srt's own lookup time, so a planted `<run>/bash` would stand in for the shell srt wraps commands with (probe on round-0 code: `_path_without_run_dir("/usr/bin:.:bin:<run>/bin", run)` → `'/usr/bin:.:bin'`). Same root cause in `_resolved_on_path`: `shutil.which` on a relative entry resolves against the orchestrator's cwd — the client checkout — so a client `node_modules/.bin/node` would become the trusted launcher node. RED first: both new tests recorded RED (relative entries reached the launcher PATH; the relative node EXECUTED as the pin-check interpreter, marker written). **FIX (class 1): `_absolute_path_entries`** — only absolute, non-empty entries are searched, in BOTH `_resolved_on_path` (the which-PATH is built from absolute entries only) and `_path_without_run_dir`. A node findable only through a relative entry is NOT FOUND (named refusal); the launcher PATH never contains a relative or empty entry.

**Round-1 red** (new cases against the round-0 tree, before fixing): `test_ac_2` DID NOT RAISE on add-then-delete (and orphan-merge/tree-entry-name/author-identity); `test_relative_path_entries…` relative entries in the launcher PATH; `test_node_from_a_relative_path_entry…` the relative node executed (wrong SandboxError shape + marker present). The chunk-straddle case is green against round 0 by construction (whole-string scan, no boundary) — its teeth are the no-overlap mutation, which is red.

**Round-1 green:** tests/test_f62.py **15 passed** (13 + 2). Mutation table after round 1 (the `--text`/`--no-textconv` rows are N/A — the diff scan no longer exists):

| AC | Mutation | Result |
|----|----------|--------|
| 1 | PATH-resolved launch restored (`[binary, …]`, `env=child_env`) | RED — test_ac_1 |
| 1 | `env=child_env` only (argv kept) | RED — launcher-env test |
| 1 | relative entries kept in `_path_without_run_dir` | RED — relative-PATH test |
| 1 | relative which-lookup in `_resolved_on_path` | RED — relative-node test |
| 2 | scan only the last commit's objects (`head~1..head`, the net-diff hole) | RED — add-then-delete |
| 2 | no chunk overlap (`carry = b""`) | RED — straddle case |
| 2 | hex-upper form dropped | RED — hex-upper case |
| 3 | `fullmatch` → `match`+`$` | RED — `"abc\n"` admitted |
| 3 | `trace_path` routing skipped | RED — builder case |

All nine re-run in the round-1 final sweep (every mutation, not just the new ones — the final-round rule), each restored with `__pycache__` purged and the full file re-green after.

**Round-1 hostile re-run:** the reviewer's two probes now refused (they are the test cases); huge publish re-measured 0.02s/37MB (above); the round-0 hostile items (symlinks, planted env/bash with real srt, dash-named projects, TMPDIR/HOME vectors, split-token/gzip known limits) unchanged — split-across-OBJECTS joins split-across-hunks as an accepted class-2 limit (a token split between a tree name and a blob body, or across two objects, defeats any needle scan; the literal+simple encodings remain the boundary).

**Post-review nit (folded in, per the reviewer's option 2):** the round-1 `header.split()[0:1] == [b"missing"]` guard was dead code — cat-file's missing-response is `<sha> missing`, so token 0 is the sha, never `missing`; the `len < 3` check was what caught it. Folded into one `parts` parse (`len(parts) < 3 or parts[1] == b"missing"`) so the guard names what it guards; test_f62.py re-run green (15 passed) after the change, ruff/pyright clean.

**Post-review CI fix (caught on the PR, not locally — the platform gap this entry flagged):** `test_linux_settings_pin_bwrap_socat_and_ripgrep` failed BOTH Linux legs (3.11/3.13): on a real Linux host `_make_run`'s own `build_settings` resolves the SYSTEM bwrap/socat/rg into the per-process cache (the designed one-resolution-per-process behaviour — the platform branch needs no monkeypatch there), so the fixture's setup-time clear was too early and the asserted build saw the cached `/usr/bin/bwrap`. macOS never populates the cache (no linux branch), which is why every local run passed. FIX: clear the cache AFTER the toolbin PATH is set, immediately before the asserted build — the pins under test then resolve against the toolbin PATH on every platform. Lesson (same class as F-58 R1-8): a platform-conditional test must account for the platform being REAL on CI — the monkeypatch is a no-op there and setup-order assumptions flip.

## F-63 — Owner-held approval of project config and contracts (task/profile ledger, exact-host egress, structural .factory protection)

**Source:** security audit run-2 remediation WO 3 of 3 (drafted in #114; audit evidence owner-held, outside the repo). Three findings share one shape — the decisive facts of what the factory will do live in the client repo it works on: task approval is an unsigned hash stored next to the contract (`factory/contract.py:acceptance_hash:unsigned-approval-record`, medium), the egress allowlist of the token-bearing session comes verbatim from `project.yaml` (`factory/sandbox.py:build_settings:client-controlled-allowed-domains-for-token-bearing-session`, high), and protecting `.factory/**` is a per-profile opt-in (`factory/contract.py:apply_profile_defaults:security-config-protection-is-profile-opt-in-not-default`, medium). F-63 moves the decisive facts into an owner-held ledger under the factory root (the ledger approach, owner-chosen at draft), pins egress entries to exact hosts the owner saw, and makes the control surface structurally protected.

**Built:**

- **AC-1 — task approvals in an owner ledger** (`factory/approvals.py` NEW, `factory/contract.py` ★, `factory/state.py` ★, `factory/orchestrator.py` ★, `factory/approve.py` ★, `factory/cli.py`, `factory/data/schema/approvals.schema.json` ★ NEW):
  - `ApprovalLedger` (subclass of `ProgressLog` — the SAME chaining/append mechanism, no second one): `<factory_root>/approvals/<project_component>.jsonl`, numbered `n`/`prev`-chained entries, flock+fsync appends; adds per-line schema validation (approvals.schema.json, every line on load, the line named) and a project binding (every entry carries `project`; a ledger copied from another project's file refuses to load). `entries()` reads under a SHARED lock so a concurrent owner approve is never observed half-written (probed: 142 clean concurrent reads, 0 torn).
  - `contract_hash(task)` in contract.py beside `acceptance_hash`: SHA-256 over canonical JSON (sorted keys, fixed separators, `ensure_ascii=False`) of the task minus exactly `CONTRACT_HASH_EXCLUDED_FIELDS` (status, acceptance_hash, approved_by, approved_at, attempts, attempt_resets, links) and each owner step's `OWNER_STEP_HASH_EXCLUDED_FIELDS` (done, done_at, done_by, evidence). Both constants pinned by test, including "an unknown future field changes the hash". **AC amendment (the AC's own tick test forces it):** the AC's parenthetical names `done_at/done_by/evidence`, but `tick_owner_steps` writes `done: true` in the same update — `done` joins the exclusion or the "ticking does not demote" test cannot pass.
  - Writers: `approve_task` and `reconcile_bootstrap` append a `kind: task` entry (id, contract_hash, approved_by, approved_at) BEFORE the status move (an entry for a task that never left draft is harmless history; an approved task the ledger doesn't cover would demote straight back). `factory approve` takes the same `--factory-root` as `run` (default `~/factory`).
  - Enforcement — every path that acts on a task automatically because of its status, each an independent `has_contract` check on the task's CURRENT hash: `pick`/`pick_next` (ledger param, unledgered frozen tasks pre-filtered), `begin_run` (ledger param, refuses before the lease), `demote_changed_contracts(ledger)` (EXTENDED, no parallel pass: ready+unledgered → draft; every unledgered frozen task named in `audit.unledgered`), `_sweep_stale_prs(ledger)` (recovery edges, in_review re-validation and merged-close stamps all filtered), `_close_merged_tasks(ledger)` (both call sites), `run_loop` via run_once per iteration. `reclaim_abandoned`/`sync_readiness` only move tasks TO ready, where pick/demote gate them (documented). Publish/push/re-validate all happen inside `begin_run`'s lease or the sweep — no ungated actor remains. `RunResult.unledgered` + CLI `unledgered (not acted on): <id>` lines make every refusal visible.
  - Adoption: `factory approve --adopt` — each unledgered task in a frozen status (except done) rendered with `render_contract`, recorded on interactive confirm; always interactive (no `--yes` path exists for adopt, so risk-flagged tasks are covered by construction).
  - The factory's own queue today: 35 draft + 28 done ⇒ no `ready` tasks; done needs no entry (the demote audit skips done in `unledgered` reporting).
- **AC-2 — owner-approved profile security keys** (`factory/approvals.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py` ★, `factory/importer.py`, `factory/cli.py`):
  - `PROFILE_SECURITY_GROUPS` — the 12 groups (commands, sentinels, network_allowlist, docs_allowlist, sandbox, repo.remote, repo.default_branch, repo.branch_protection, paths.protected, paths.risk, live_checks, toolkit), each hashed separately (sha-256 of canonical JSON with `ensure_ascii=False`; a MISSING key hashes as null, so removal is a change). F-59's provider/data_owner groups join this constant when they exist — an approval predating a group never matches it (fail closed).
  - `factory approve --profile [PATH]` renders the groups (network entries one per line, absent groups as `(absent)`), interactive confirm only (no `--yes` in this mode), appends `kind: profile` with the per-group hashes. `require_profile_approval` runs at the TOP of `run_once` (before admission's gh call, before the host probe, before the queue is read) and in `run_import`'s real-session branch (covering the import session and the repair seat) — hashing the SAME in-memory loaded-and-defaulted dict everything downstream uses; the file is never re-read (TOCTOU probed).
  - Eval/rehearsal (factory-owned fixture profiles — source-defined, admitted by the same reviewed-PR chain as the schema consts) record their own approval into their OWN factory root via `ensure_fixture_profile_approval` (run_on_demand_eval, run_importer_eval, run_rehearsal). No production path calls it; the choice is reported, not silent.
- **AC-3 — exact hosts for token-bearing sessions** (`factory/contract.py` ★, `tests/test_f63.py`):
  - `_check_allowlist_hosts` at load (`_check_profile_semantics`): lowercase ASCII DNS names, labels `[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?`, ≥1 dot, final label not all-numeric; `fullmatch` (the F-62 `$`-newline lesson). Refused with the entry named: wildcard, scheme, port, path, IPv4/bracketed-IPv6/integer-IP, uppercase, non-ASCII, trailing dot, empty, padding, trailing newline. All three current profiles (jobfinderos, soloadventurer-web, the factory's own) still load.
  - **srt finding, from the pinned 0.0.79 source:** `matchesDomainPattern` is EXACT (case-insensitive) for a bare pattern — `h === pattern.toLowerCase()`; only `*.base` widens and only `*` matches everything. A bare `example.com` does NOT match `sub.example.com`. No mismatch: the exact-host policy and srt's bare-entry semantics agree. Pinned by a source-pin test so an srt upgrade that changes this fails the suite.
  - Seat settings test: implementer, reviewer (read-only), verifier, importer (reviewer shape), importer repair (deny_read), fan-out (own scratch) — each seat's generated settings carry EXACTLY `[model host, *docs_allowlist, *network_allowlist]`, `deniedDomains == []`, and never `allowLocalBinding` (gate-only knob).
- **AC-4 — structural `.factory/**` protection** (`factory/data/schema/project.schema.json` ★, `factory/contract.py` ★, `factory/l0.py` ★):
  - `$defs.defaultProtectedControl.const = [".factory/**"]` beside the runner-config default; `default_protected_control()` reads it (refuses to guess if the `$defs` entry is missing — judge state); unioned in BOTH seams (`apply_profile_defaults` and `check_diff_policy`'s independent re-union). A profile (or a hand-built dict) can only widen.
- **Test-fixture rule (AC-1 item 6):** ONE helper, `tests/_support/f63.py::ledger_approve(root, queue_path, *, tasks=True, profile=True)` (+ `importer_fixture_approve` for real-import fixtures), called from the existing fixtures — no test builds ledger files by hand.
- **Suite hygiene:** `tests/conftest.py` ★ gains `_pin_factory_root_default` (autouse): the approve/approvals/importer `FACTORY_ROOT_DEFAULT` bindings pin to a per-test tmp dir, so no test can ever append to the owner's REAL ledger (an f63 bug class caught in this build — see the incident below).

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; scope class per item):
- Can any seat read/write `<root>/approvals/`? Built settings for all 7 seat shapes (implementer, reviewer, verifier, importer, importer repair, fan-out, gate): no allowRead or allowWrite entry covers approvals/, and the factory root is denyRead in every shape — OK (class: verified, no change needed).
- Client-minted `ready` with forged `approved_by` + correct `acceptance_hash`: never picked, demoted, named — the AC-1 test itself (class: fixed here).

- Canonicalisation collisions: NFC/NFD distinct, `1.0` vs `1` distinct, key order irrelevant (sorted keys), acceptance-list order semantic (tested) (class: pinned).
- Concurrent approve during a run: shared/exclusive flock on the ledger — 142 interleaved reads, 0 torn, 0 refusals (probed) (class: verified).
- Profile TOCTOU: settings built from the loaded dict after the file was rewritten carry the LOADED content (probed) (class: fixed here).

- docs/network entries reaching the tokenless gate seat: the gate settings carry the same allowlist (inert there — its env holds no provider credential; the entries' only risk is egress WITH a credential) (class: stated, no change).
- Known limits (class 2, stated in code and tests): the hash chain detects a rewrite only via the follower — a schema-valid rewrite of the LAST line is chain-undetectable (progress.py shares this property); tail truncation only REMOVES approvals → demote/refuse, never widens (tested). The chain is integrity-of-record, not a signature; the boundary is the file's location outside every sandbox.

**Incidents:**
1. **The F-56 mutation-harness trap, repeated and caught:** the first mutation battery restored mutated files with `git checkout --`, which on tracked files carrying UNCOMMITTED F-63 work reverted contract.py/orchestrator.py/l0.py to HEAD outright, and could not restore the untracked approvals.py at all (its mutations persisted). Recovered by re-applying every edit from the session record (all edits verified by re-running the gates and the full suite); all later mutations used `cp`-backup/restore. Lesson: on an uncommitted worktree, mutation restores must copy from a backup, never `git checkout`.
2. **Real-root ledger pollution (pre-pin):** before the conftest pin existed, test_f04's approve tests and the importer fixtures' auto-approval wrote into the REAL `~/factory/approvals/` (10 files: fixture/jobfinderos/proj/prose-demo/software-factory `.jsonl` + locks, all `approved_by` "Tester"/"factory-fixture", all this session's timestamps). The pin (`tests/conftest.py::_pin_factory_root_default`) stops every write; the leaked files remain in place for the owner to delete (`rm -rf ~/factory/approvals` — no real approvals were there before this session; deletion left to the owner by the session's permission boundary).

A future WO that makes the orchestrator write slice statuses must extend the exclusion list or every sliced task demotes at slice merge.
