**Round 1 (2026-11-09, reviewer: two fixes — both confirmed real before fixing):**

- **R1-1 (AC-2 hole, class 2 amend + class 1 fix): `repo.path` was unapproved and decides which code is cloned.** After approving client A's profile, A could change `repo.path` to client B's checkout — no re-approval — and the next run cloned B's code, ran sessions over it, and pushed B's history to A's approved remote: a cross-client code leak, the exact boundary this WO exists for. RED first recorded on the round-0 tree (the new `repo.path` mutator case FAILED with the run sailing past the check to `create_run`'s "not a git repository" refusal — the hole, observed), then fixed: `repo.path` joins `PROFILE_SECURITY_GROUPS`, hashed (and rendered at `approve --profile`) on the EXPANDED+RESOLVED absolute path (`_PATH_GROUPS`), so `~/x` and `/Users/<u>/x` are one approval and no spelling change dodges re-approval (normalization pinned by test). Goal-level probe on the fixed tree: approve → swap `repo.path` to another repo → run refuses with `repo.path` named, zero sessions started.
  - **The key sweep the reviewer asked for** (which other profile keys select which code/repo/path the factory reads or clones): `repo.remote` (covered), `repo.path` (NOW covered). Repo-CONTAINED relative keys — `standards` (prompts.py refuses absolute/outside-repo entries by its own PromptError check), `baselines` (read from the git tree at base_ref, l0.py:753), `paths.setup_outputs`, `dependency_manifests` — select files INSIDE the already-approved clone; they stay uncovered per the AC's own test. NOT CONSUMED TODAY (future-WO constraints, same class as the slices note): `cross_repo[*].path` (no factory consumer yet — the WO that lands one must add the group or it is the repo.path hole again) and `holdouts.dir` (denied-path derivation uses `factory_root/holdouts`, never the profile key; F-15 must add the group when it consumes the key).

**Round 2 (2026-11-09, new reviewer after round 1's ended — three findings, all confirmed and fixed red-first):**

- **R2-1 (HIGH, goal-level): the owner approval prompts could be spoofed with terminal control sequences.** A profile whose real `repo.remote` was an attacker URL, plus one `paths.protected` glob carrying cursor-up/erase-line sequences, rewrote the already-printed remote line: the owner saw the factory's real remote, the attacker URL nowhere on screen (the reviewer's VT-emulator repro, re-run RED on this tree before fixing: `attacker remote visible on screen? False`). Both renders printed raw and `load_profile` accepted C0/ESC in strings. FIXED three ways, red-first:
  - **Escape, never strip, in approval output** (`factory/egress.py` ★ — extended, not forked: `escape_approval_text(text, escape_non_ascii=)` beside `strip_control_sequences`, sharing the module's one notion of "control"): every C0 (\n, \r, \t included), DEL, C1 and bidi/isolate control (U+202A–202E, U+2066–2069, U+200E/200F, U+061C) renders as a visible `\xNN`/`\uNNNN` escape; `repo.remote`/`repo.path` escape ALL non-ASCII (a homoglyph remote reads as codepoints). Wired into `render_contract`, `render_profile_security` and reconcile's marker echo (`factory/approve.py` ★) — everything approve/reconcile/adopt echo.
  - **Load-time refusal, key path named** (`factory/contract.py` ★): no profile string may carry any of the classes (\n and \t included — no free-text exemptions on the profile side); task strings refuse the same EXCEPT \n/\t in the free-text fields (`note`/`evidence`/`text` — the real queue's `attempts[*].note` is multi-line, the only such field today). The F-62 name-slug check runs FIRST so a bad `name` gets the value-naming slug refusal, not the key-path control one.
  - **Proofs:** the reviewer's emulator, turned into `test_approval_renders_escape_terminal_controls` for BOTH renders (attacker value on screen / real risk flag on screen; ESC bytes visible as `\x1b`), per-class load refusals for profile and task strings, and `test_real_queues_and_profiles_still_load` (both real queues — none holds a control character today — and all three profiles). Post-fix the reviewer's script prints `attacker remote visible on screen? True` with the payload visible as inert escaped text.
- **R2-2 (test gaps: seven mutations had survived on AC-named seams).** All seven seams now have direct tests, and each listed mutation goes RED: `pick(ledger)`/`begin_run(ledger)` tested at the STATE layer (unledgered ready with a valid acceptance_hash → pick None; begin_run raises StateError with no transition and no lease left — run_once's demote no longer masks them); approve/reconcile/adopt each proven to write a ledger entry carrying the CURRENT contract hash; `approve_profile` proven to print network entries one per line and absent groups as `(absent)`, with a decline recording nothing; the CLI's `--yes` refused for `--profile`, `--adopt` and `--reconcile-bootstrap` (exit 2 each); the importer's real-session path proven to refuse without a profile approval (zero sessions started); `_close_merged_tasks` proven to skip an unledgered merged task and close an approved one. The round-0 BOOTSTRAP line "function-level tested with injected confirm/echo" was NOT true for these seams at the time (the reviewer was right); it is true now, with the tests listed above.
- **R2-3 (fixture approvals could land in the production ledger directory).** `factory eval`'s root defaults to ~/factory, so fixture approvals (approved_by "factory-fixture") appended to e.g. `~/factory/approvals/soloadventurer.jsonl` — a future client named like a fixture would get its latest owner approval superseded by a fixture entry. FIXED: `ApprovalLedger._append` refuses a factory-fixture entry into any ledger holding a non-fixture entry (named error suggesting `--factory-root`); the eval/rehearsal/approvals comments and this ledger's round-0 "their OWN factory root" wording corrected to "the root the eval/rehearsal was given — DEFAULTS to ~/factory, and the mixing guard keeps owner ledgers owner-only". Test: `test_fixture_approval_never_mixes_into_an_owner_ledger` (owner entry → ensure/fixture-record refuse; fresh ledger → fixture approval lands). The test helper `_support/f63.py` now records OWNER approvals (`record_profile_for(approved_by="owner")`), not fixture ones — tests simulate the owner, and the fixture path is eval/rehearsal production code only.
- **Residual recorded (no change, per the reviewer):** `gates.matches` is case-sensitive — a diff path `.Factory/x` does not match the `.factory/**` glob; on macOS an APFS clone of a case-variant dir folds such writes into the existing `.factory` dir, so the diff path reads `.factory/…` and the protection holds there in practice. Known limit, recorded here.

## F-64 — Parent-side budget accounting (fixed per-seat turn caps, review wall reserve, bounded free quota requeues, budget visibility)

**Source:** security audit run-2 remediation WO 4 (third family, drafted in #114; audit evidence owner-held, outside the repo). The turn-budget and quota arithmetic read `num_turns` and `reason` from the session record, which the session writes: a forged `num_turns: 0` funded later seats past `budget.max_turns`, and a forged "429" reason granted unlimited free requeues on the owner's shared Z.ai token. The parent knows what it granted — F-64 accounts from that.

**AC-1 AMENDMENT (owner decision 2026-11-09, option D — fixed per-seat caps; recorded per the brief):** the literal AC ("the sum of granted caps never exceeds `budget.max_turns`", with the implementer granted the whole budget) would leave 0 turns for review, and the pre-split alternative would take ~50 turns from an implementer whose real p90 is 109 of 120 (JobFinderOS traces). The owner chose fixed per-seat caps: the sum of granted caps EXCEEDS `max_turns` by design, and no cap depends on any session's record. This supersedes F-38 review finding 3's remainder sharing and the turn half of F-41 AC-4 (both documented at the constants and in the rewritten tests); the tasks.json AC text is updated at reconcile — never edited here.

**Built:**

- **AC-1 — turns accounted from granted caps** (`factory/orchestrator.py` ★):
  - Constants beside `_REVIEW_TURN_FLOOR`: `REVIEWER_TURN_CAP = 20`, `VERIFIER_TURN_CAP = 10` (every reviewer seat — round review, final review, each re-run — gets the reviewer cap; the verifier its own), with module-level asserts that both sit at or above the floor (a cap below it would reinstate the F-38 starvation as a constant). The floor's runtime refusals are gone; it remains the documented minimum + the caps' lower bound. PROPOSED values — trace evidence re-checked read-only (`~/factory/trace/**`, session_end events): implementer n=24 median 46 / p90 109 / max 139 turns; reviewer n=8 median 10 / max 15; verifier n=1: 5. 20/10 give headroom over the observed maxima; samples thin — revisit after F-13.
  - `_turns_spent` DELETED (no code use remains; grep-proven). The attempt record's `"turns"` sum goes through `_session_turns` (the importer `_record_turns` rule: non-bool int >= 0 counts, anything else a forged record carries renders as nothing, never raw) — telemetry, labelled session-reported. `factory/trace.py` ★ `session_end` applies the same validation (garbage renders as None, "turns unknown").
  - Wall clock is the parent-measured total bound: `REVIEW_WALL_RESERVE_MIN = 8.0` (PROPOSED — observed review-pass max reviewer 4.4 + verifier 1.0 ≈ 5.4 min; a reviewer spending its FULL 20-turn cap at the observed ~18 s/turn needs ≈ 5.9 + verifier ≈ 6.9 → 8.0) and `_MIN_IMPLEMENTER_WALL_MIN = 1.0`. The implementer's per-round timeout is `remaining − reserve`; the round-start check escalates `budget_exhausted` (numbers named) when only the reserve remains — a round whose review the wall cannot fund never runs its implementer. A static pre-seat refusal in `_run_task` (before `create_run`, the `_sweep_escalation` session-less attempt shape) refuses budgets below reserve + minimal window before ANY seat starts. All four turn-based `budget_exhausted` refusals (round fan-out, round verifier, final fan-out, final verifier) are REMOVED; the four wall-clock ones stay as backstops. `_retry_budget(budget, session_seconds)` — fixed cap, per-re-run wall recomputation from parent-measured time kept (PR #90's invariant), signature loses `records`/`reviewer_share` (it no longer reads any record).
- **AC-2 — free quota requeues bounded** (`factory/state.py` ★): `FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_PER_DAY = 2` (PROPOSED, named constant); the ONE counting function `attempt_budget_used` now bounds quota-classified aborts (outcome `aborted`, escalation `quota_exhausted`): the first 2 per task per UTC calendar day are free, the third and later count toward `max_attempts`; the day is parsed from the attempt's PARENT-written `started` stamp (`_quota_utc_day`, fail-closed: a missing/unparseable stamp counts), never a session field; non-quota aborts (peak window, crash) stay free and never consume a day's allowance; slice scoping and the `attempt_resets` floor are unchanged and verified. No second counter exists — run numbering, the "attempt N of M" prompt line and the PR body attempt line still flow from this one filter. `factory loop`'s `quota_exhausted` stop unchanged (pinned).
- **AC-3 — budgets visible and bounded at approval** (`factory/approve.py` ★, `factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json` ★): `render_contract`'s budget line shows `max_turns`, `wall_clock_min` and `max_attempts` beside size, through the existing F-63 escaping; the schema gains `maximum: 200` (max_turns) and `maximum: 150` (wall_clock_min) — today's queue maxima (`.factory/tasks.json` 200/150/3; `projects/jobfinderos.tasks.json` 120/90/3), so nothing existing fails to load (proven in-test). `contract_hash` untouched (render is display-only; proven with a ledger-approved fixture task, hashed before and after the render, still picked, not demoted).
- `.factory/project.yaml` ★: `tests/test_f64.py` added to `paths.protected` (the F-63 precedent).
- Pinned tests updated to the amended invariants: `tests/test_f38.py` ★ (`test_ac_5` caps → fixed constants; `test_review3_turn_budget_is_aggregate` → `test_review3_turn_caps_are_fixed_not_remainder`: the spent-budget and verifier-floor refusals are GONE, the fan-out runs at full caps), `tests/test_f41.py` ★ (`test_ac_4` turn half flipped: the final review now RUNS at full caps on a spent turn budget — the amendment's guarantee; wall half unchanged), `tests/test_l2_retry_budgets.py` (the `_retry_budget` unit tests to the fixed-cap signature; `test_turn_refused_retry_never_runs_a_third_seat` → `test_turn_spent_budget_still_runs_one_rerun_per_seat` (one re-run per seat still, at the cap); `test_a_retry_never_starves_the_verifier` asserts both caps).

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief): factory/orchestrator.py ★, factory/state.py ★, factory/approve.py ★, factory/trace.py ★, factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★, .factory/project.yaml ★, tests/test_f38.py ★, tests/test_f41.py ★, tests/test_f64.py (NEW, protected), tests/test_l2_retry_budgets.py. `.factory/tasks.json` untouched.

**Red first** (tests/test_f64.py on the untouched tree cd67f45):
- AC-1: `ImportError: cannot import name 'REVIEW_WALL_RESERVE_MIN'` — plus the behavioural red captured by stubbing ONLY the constants in (no logic): with a planted `num_turns: 0` on the implementer record the reviewer seats were CALLED at `{10}` (the funded remainder `(20-0)//2`), not `{20}` — the audit's attack, observed live.
- AC-2: `AssertionError: ['quota_exhausted' × 10] … assert 'attempts_exhausted' == 'quota_exhausted'` — the planted 429s requeued free forever.
- AC-3: `AssertionError: 37 missing from the contract` (the render showed attempts only); the 201-turn queue loaded (schema had no maximum — DID NOT RAISE on the later pytest.raises). The F-63-interaction part (ledger hash/pick) was green before and after by construction — the render never fed the hash.

**Green:** tests/test_f64.py 3 passed. Gates (worktree .venv): `ruff check .` clean; `pyright --warnings factory` 0 errors/0 warnings; `pytest -q -n auto -p no:cacheprovider` **1328 passed, 2 skipped** (the two live-only f06 halves; no FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS, no credential, no GitHub/Z.ai contact); `__pycache__` purged before; `find ~/factory -newer <marker>` after the suite: empty.

The static check stays: it refuses earlier (no run dir, no trace) and is the AC's named refusal. |

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; class per item — all class 1, fixed/verified here):
- Planted `num_turns` 0 / 10**9 on the implementer record, then on a reviewer record: every later seat CALLED at its fixed cap; telemetry renders the raw count (valid int) and funds nothing. A verifier-seat plant cannot change any later cap by construction (all four cap sites assign constants; mutation 3 pins the guard).
- Planted `num_turns` −5 / `true` / `"999"` / 1.5 / bytes: `_session_turns` → 0, `trace.session_end` → None ("turns unknown"), and the F-57 record wall (`record_field_error`) refuses the record raw at `_read_record`/`_read_record_quietly` — a garbage-count record never crosses the file boundary at all. Missing (`None`) renders unknown, absent is fine (pre-F-06 records).
- A record planted only for the timeout path (real `TimeoutExpired` arm, `run_sandboxed` monkeypatched to plant + hang — the f57 `_planted_timeout_run` pattern): the record is USED, named in the trace (`record_used_after_timeout`), and no later seat's cap moves; a forged-FIELD record at timeout reads as absent (f57 T1, unchanged).
- A reviewer claiming quota on every seat AND re-run: 5 quota attempts (2 free + 3 counting) then `attempts_exhausted` — the daily cap bounds re-run-sourced quota aborts through the same `_quota_exhausted` path.
- A verifier quota mid-fan-out: `aborted`/`quota_exhausted`, task requeued `ready`, free under the same daily cap.
- 3+ quota aborts across a UTC midnight boundary (both sides, injected clock on the PARENT-written `started` stamps): day 2's allowance restored; the run numbering moves only on counting aborts.
- `attempt_resets` combined with quota aborts: the floor holds (pre-reset attempts invisible), post-reset quota frees start fresh; slices never share an allowance (per-slice per-day); a quota attempt with a missing, unparseable or offset-less (naive) `started` counts (fail closed — a naive stamp has no defined UTC day and is never local-time-interpreted).
- `max_turns = 1`: implementer granted 1, both reviewers 20, verifier 10 — PR opened. `wall_clock_min` too small for the reserve: named refusal naming the numbers, zero seats started.
- Ledger-approved fixture task after the schema change: still picked, not demoted, hash matching (F-63 interaction, in-test).

**num_turns/_turns_spent inventory** (every remaining use in factory/): `orchestrator._session_turns` (telemetry, validated — this WO), `trace.session_end` (telemetry, validated — this WO), `importer._record_turns` + its docstring (telemetry, validated — R2-2), `session.SessionRecord` field + `record_field_error` validation + `_record_from` construction (the transport field, validated at the ONE entry point — F-57 R3-1), `rehearsal.py` stub records (fixture data). `_turns_spent`: zero code uses (one historical comment). Budget arithmetic reads none of them.

**Spec mismatches reported (not resolved here):**
1. AC-1 is AMENDED by the owner's option-D decision (text and reasoning above); the tasks.json AC text is updated at reconcile.
2. PROPOSED values for owner confirmation at spec approval: `REVIEWER_TURN_CAP = 20`, `VERIFIER_TURN_CAP = 10`, `REVIEW_WALL_RESERVE_MIN = 8.0`, `_MIN_IMPLEMENTER_WALL_MIN = 1.0`, `FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_PER_DAY = 2` (state.py), schema maxima `max_turns ≤ 200` / `wall_clock_min ≤ 150`.
3. F-38 finding 3's remainder rule and F-41 AC-4's turn half are superseded (BUILD-ORDER text unchanged — flagged here; the tasks.json/build-order text updates ride the reconcile).
4. Routed elsewhere, named not fixed: F-59 (per-project provider/credentials, quota accounting per provider — the daily cap is per task, not per token), F-14 (live checks), F-60 (memory), and the audit's other open items (gate-command output buffering, abort-salvage size bound, regex CPU findings).

**What could NOT be verified here:** no real (paid) session ran against the new caps — every seat is scripted; the caps'/reserve's adequacy for real reviews rests on the trace evidence above (thin samples; revisit after F-13's first JobFinderOS runs). The reserve holds a full-cap review pass at observed per-turn rates (~6.9 min < 8.0) but not an arbitrarily slow one — the wall backstops escalate `budget_exhausted` there rather than starving review silently, which is the amendment's rule for the wall's own exhaustion. Multi-round wall accounting is per-run (pre-existing shape): session_seconds resets each run, so a fix round days later starts a fresh wall budget — unchanged by this WO, noted.

**Round 1 (2026-11-10, reviewer: NOT clean — one goal-level gap, five surviving mutations, one wording; all confirmed red-first before fixing):**

- **R1-1 (class 2 — AC amendment, goal-level): the free quota allowance was per UTC day ONLY, so across days it was unbounded.** A task run about once a day whose session claims 429 every time requeued free forever — up to 2 full implementer sessions a day indefinitely on the shared token: still "a record's reason grants more", merely rate-limited. **AC-2 AMENDED:** a quota abort is free only while BOTH the per-day allowance (2) and a LIFETIME ceiling hold — `FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_TOTAL = 6` (PROPOSED; owner confirms at spec approval), counted since the current attempt-budget floor in the same `attempt_budget_used` loop (no second counter; an owner reset restores the lifetime allowance with the budget, and slices keep their own, as before). Red: `0 == 2` with the ceiling removed. Test: 2 quota aborts a day over 4 days → the 7th+ count (`used == len(attempts) − FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_TOTAL`); a reset restores the total.
- **R1-2 (class 1 — five test gaps, the reviewer's mutations X4/X7/X11/X14/X15 survived round 0; each now has a direct test, red shown with the reviewer's own mutation applied, cp-restores, `__pycache__` purged):**
  - **X4 — the final-review VERIFIER's cap was unpinned.** test_f41 test_ac_4's final verdict now carries one minor finding (declared pass), so the final verifier runs; both verifier passes (round 1's and the final's) are pinned at `VERIFIER_TURN_CAP`. Red: `{10, 13} == {10}`. Lesson recorded: the env's first fix used `max_turns: 10` — equal to the verifier cap — so the mutation was INVISIBLE (caps collide with the budget); the env now uses `max_turns: 13`, deliberately equal to neither cap. A cap-pin test needs a budget distinct from every cap it pins.
  - **X7 — the per-round reserve refusal was only covered at round 1 (the static pre-check).** test_ac_1 adds a LATER-round case: round 1's fan-out "takes" 38 of 45 wall minutes (review FAIL), round 2 arrives with 7 remaining — under the 8-min reserve — and must refuse THERE (named note, "review reserve" in it) with NO implementer for round 2. Red: the weakened check (`remaining_min <= 0`) ran round 2's implementer and published.
  - **X11 — the naive/missing/unparseable stamp fail-closed claim was untested.** test_ac_2 pins all three: 2 valid frees + naive ("2026-11-15", no offset) + missing (None) + garbage ("not a timestamp") → `used == 3`. Red with naive-made-free: `2 == 3`. (Naive stamps are never local-time-interpreted — a hand-shifted naive stamp cannot mint fresh days.)
  - **X14 — `_session_turns` accepting negatives was unpinned.** test_ac_1 now asserts the helper directly on −5/True/"999"/1.5 (→ 0) and 7 (→ 7), plus an exact end-to-end sum: −5 on reviewer 1, True on reviewer 2, 1.5 on the verifier → `attempt["turns"] == 3` (the implementer's valid count only). Red: `−5 == 0`.
  - **X15 — trace.py rendering num_turns raw was unpinned** (the hostile pass claimed it, no test held it). New focused test `test_trace_turns_render_unknown_for_forged_counts`: session_end renders None for −5/True/"999"/1.5 and 7 for 7. Red: `[−5, True, '999', 1.5, 7] != [None×4, 7]`.
- **R1-3 (wording):** the schema maxima descriptions said "owner-confirmed at spec approval" — they are not confirmed; now "PROPOSED maximum … the owner confirms at spec approval." (Re-applying this after a json.dump reformat casualty: the round-0 schema change was re-applied as a two-line edit so the diff stays minimal — never rewrite a schema file through a JSON round-trip when a text edit will do.)

### F-64 amendment — owner-confirmed budget values (2026-11-10)

**Owner decision (2026-11-10), after F-64 merged (#117) with PROPOSED values:** `REVIEW_WALL_RESERVE_MIN` 8.0 → **15.0** (it is a floor: review also gets every minute the implementer leaves; 15 gives headroom for large diffs at ~5% of a 150-min budget). `FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_PER_DAY` 2 → **3**, `FREE_QUOTA_REQUEUES_TOTAL` 6 → **9** (a GLM quota window is ~5 h; past either allowance a quota abort spends a normal attempt, so this only decides when a genuinely rate-limited task parks for an owner reset). Confirmed unchanged: `REVIEWER_TURN_CAP` 20, `VERIFIER_TURN_CAP` 10, `_MIN_IMPLEMENTER_WALL_MIN` 1.0, schema maxima `max_turns` ≤ 200 / `wall_clock_min` ≤ 150 (work needing more is sliced, not extended).

**Changes:** the constants and their comments (`factory/orchestrator.py` ★, `factory/state.py` ★); the schema descriptions drop "PROPOSED" (`factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json` ★); the F-64 spec text in `.factory/tasks.json` and `BUILD-ORDER.md` now states the as-built AC-1 (option D fixed caps + wall reserve — the old text still described the superseded shared-budget rule) and AC-2 (per-day + lifetime). `tests/test_f64.py`: AC-1/AC-2 now derive every number from the constants (no literal 2/6/8 left), plus `test_owner_confirmed_budget_values`, which pins the confirmed numbers so any change to them is a visible, reviewed test edit. A 15-min reserve + 1-min floor means a task needs `wall_clock_min` ≥ 16 to run: the shared test fixtures' 10-min budgets became 30 (`tests/_support/f23.py`, `f30.py`, `f38.py` and the per-file task helpers in test_f10/f22/f25/f26/f27/f41/f42/f43/f45/f58/f62/f63); the three timing assertions tied to a 10-min window moved with it (test_f38 reviewer timeout bound, test_f63 hash canonicalisation pair, test_l2_retry_budgets re-run grants 30/30/26/22). Real queues are unaffected: `.factory/tasks.json` and `projects/jobfinderos.tasks.json` use 60/90/150 only.

**Evidence:** macOS (fresh venv, `pip install -e ".[dev]"`): `ruff check .` clean, `pyright --warnings factory` 0/0, `pytest -q -n auto -p no:cacheprovider` **1330 passed, 2 skipped**, 0 writes under `~/factory`. Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 container, same tree): 1326 passed / 3 skipped (`not live_network`), `live_network` 3 passed, pyright 0/0, ruff clean, `preflight_host` OK.

**Mutation battery (on a snapshot of this tree, fresh venv, `__pycache__`-free copy per mutant):** all **23 RED** — F-64's 17 (seat caps at every grant site, implementer wall window, per-round reserve refusal, same-day/off-by-one/non-quota/naive-stamp/lifetime quota counting, render, schema maximum, telemetry validation, trace turns) still caught with the derived tests, plus 6 new: reserve back to 8.0, per-day back to 2, total back to 6 (each caught by `test_owner_confirmed_budget_values`), the day check reading the lifetime constant, the lifetime check reading the per-day constant, and the implementer window subtracting half the reserve (each caught by the behavioural AC-1/AC-2 tests).


## F-65 — Declared file scope (scope.paths contract field, L0 out_of_scope gate, request_scope transport, generated-file profile group)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-65 (owner decision 2026-11-10, spec amended in #120, approved same day). The owner's rule "the task and nothing more" was judged only by the L2 reviewer against free text; F-65 makes the file half a deterministic gate: each contract declares the paths it may change, the owner approves them with the contract (inside `contract_hash`, so the F-63 ledger covers them), L0 fails anything outside them, and a session that needs more asks — a request can only escalate, never widen.

**Built:**

- **AC-1 — contracts declare their file scope:**
  - `factory/gates.py` ★: `scope_matches` — the ONE scope matcher, beside (never replacing) `matches`. Semantics: `*`/`?` never cross `/` (segment-wise), `**` as a whole segment matches zero or more directories, case-sensitive on the exact path string; character classes unsupported (refused at validation). `matches` keeps fnmatch semantics on purpose — protected/risk globs should over-match (fail-safe), scope globs are allowlists and must under-match. Memoised on segment tuples (an owner pattern stacking `**` segments would otherwise revisit suffix pairs exponentially).
  - `factory/contract.py` ★: `scope_pattern_error` — the ONE validator every scope-shaped pattern sees (task `scope.paths` via `_check_task_scope` in `_check_semantics`, profile `paths.generated` in `_check_profile_semantics`, the importer's per-task violations, the parent-side scope-request validation). Refusals: empty/non-string; NUL/control, backslash, `[`/`]`; absolute or leading `/`; any `..` segment; `.`/empty segments (`src//x`, `src/`, `./src/**` — they match no real git path while reading like a scope); structurally catch-all (every segment `*`/`**`: `**`, `*`, `**/*`, `*/**`, `**/**`, `*/*` and equivalents — a structural rule, not a spelling deny-list). The F-63 control walk fires first for control bytes in a stored pattern (proven: the walk's message names `scope.paths[i]`); the validator's own control check covers the non-stored callers (request paths, importer).
  - `factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json` ★: task `scope` (`{paths: [string, ...]}`, minItems 1, optional so pre-F-65 entries load) + `$defs/escalation` gains `scope_missing`, `scope_change_needed`.
  - Scope is inside `contract_hash` (NOT in `CONTRACT_HASH_EXCLUDED_FIELDS` — asserted in-test), so the F-63 ledger covers it and any widening is a new contract the owner must re-approve.
  - Checked in TWO places: `factory/approve.py` ★ `_check_approvable` refuses a build/docs contract with no scope (approval, single and reconcile); `factory/orchestrator.py` ★ `_run_task` refuses at attempt start, BEFORE any seat starts — the session-less `scope_missing` escalation (needs_human, task named), whether or not the ledger holds the pre-F-65 hash. `owner_only`/`decision`/`research` are exempt (they run no session). `attempt_budget_used` (`factory/state.py` ★) skips `scope_missing` attempts: no session ran, and a queue-wide fail-closed period must not burn every task's budget.
  - `render_contract` shows the paths through the existing escaping (the approval decision sees the write surface).
  - Importer (`factory/importer.py` ★): `scope` joins `SPEC_FIELDS` (proposal schema, keep-set, prompt contract lines auto-generated) with explicit prompt rules (repo-relative, no catch-all/absolute/`..`/`[`), and `_task_violations` refuses a malformed pattern naming the task. `CONTRACT_FIELDS` gains `scope` — an imported proposal that widens a frozen task's scope demotes it like any contract change (the AC gap this completes: scope IS contract now).
- **AC-2 — L0 fails changes outside the declared scope** (`factory/l0.py` ★): a new `out_of_scope` FAIL finding (not an escalation — the fix round can revert it) in `check_diff_policy`, judged through `scope_matches` for every `events()` path (added, modified, deleted, type-change `T`, mode-only `M`, copy destination `C`/`A`, submodule gitlink, BOTH sides of a rename) on the exact path string git reports (`core.quotePath` decoding as `_name_status_rows` already does). Allowed set: the task's `scope.paths` + the profile's `paths.generated` + `contract_test_files(task)` — nothing else implicit. Scope never overrides protection (both findings fire when both apply; a protected in-scope path fails exactly as protected). Boundary stated in the docstring: this gates WHICH files change, not what changes inside them (in-file drift stays L2's). A task with no scope is not judged here at all — requiring the scope is approval's and the run's job; the replay corpus and pre-F-65 revalidations call this policy with scope-less tasks.
- **AC-3 — the implementer asks instead of drifting:**
  - Prompt (`factory/data/prompts/implementer.md` ★ + `_implementer_prompt`): the system prompt gains the declared-file-scope rule (request_scope is a REQUEST, never an edit; out-of-scope edits FAIL L0; the same path twice parks the task) — hash re-pinned in `tests/test_f09.py` ★ (c23bc53b…); the per-run user prompt carries the task's `scope.paths` in the contract JSON plus the request rule. `prompt_versions` changes with it.
  - Transport: `factory/session.py` ★ `SessionRecord.scope_requests` (`{path, reason}` list; type-checked in `record_field_error`), carried by the F-56 record file; the in-sandbox tool `factory/scoperequest.py` (NEW, the vent pattern): `request_scope(path, reason)` MCP tool + `ScopeCollector`, attached for the implementer seat only (`run_session` builds it by seat; `build_options` wires the server + `allowed_tools` grant; the toolkit mcp-guard grants it like vent/propose). Collector bounds (≤20, path cap, reason seam-scrubbed + capped) are the first line; the parent re-validates.
  - Parent validation: `parse_scope_requests` (`factory/session.py`) — strict (count ≤ 20, path ≤ 1024 through `scope_pattern_error`, reason a non-empty string, seam-scrubbed + capped); an invalid request is treated as no request with what was dropped reported (`[scope request ignored]` extra finding / refusal notes in the escalation note), never a crash.
  - Escalation on a request: the parent escalates `scope_change_needed` (needs_human) AT ONCE after the implementer returns — no gates, no review, no round spent reverting. Every path/reason in the note goes through `escape_approval_text` BEFORE it lands in the attempt note (the queue's F-63 control refusal would otherwise trip on the next load). Quota aborts keep precedence (a quota-aborted session re-queues free; its requests ride the re-run).
  - Unrequested out-of-scope edits: the AC-2 finding's wording ("revert it, or request the wider scope") reaches the fix round via `_fix_findings` unchanged; the SAME path flagged in two rounds of one run — counted from the PARENT's own L0 findings (`scope_repeats`, never a session field) — escalates `scope_change_needed` before more rounds (and before `review_not_converging`, which an L0-blocked round can never reach).
  - **Owner path back (class-2 amendment, see mismatches):** `factory/state.py` ★ `TaskQueue.recover_scope` + `scope_recovery_eligible` + `factory/approve.py` ★ `_recover_scope_task` (the `factory recover` branch): interactive y/N, no `--yes`, `--evidence` required, ProgressLog audit entry (outcome `recovered`) like the credential exit — and the load-bearing gate: the CURRENT `contract_hash` (the widened scope included) must have an owner entry in the F-63 ledger, demanded at the state layer, so a widened scope that is not re-approved does not move. `factory/cli.py` help texts updated.
  - Nothing inside a run can widen scope: `.factory/**` stays protected structurally (F-63), the request path only reads the record, and the stored scope is unchanged after an escalation (asserted in-test).
- **AC-4 — generated files only by owner-approved profile list:** `factory/data/schema/project.schema.json` ★ `paths.generated` (optional glob list, refused at profile load by the same `scope_pattern_error`, pattern named); `factory/approvals.py` ★ `PROFILE_SECURITY_GROUPS` gains `paths.generated` — the F-63 group mechanism, not a new one, so widening it without re-approval refuses the run through `require_profile_approval` naming the group (rendered by `render_profile_security` like every group); `check_diff_policy` unions it into the allowed set.

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief): factory/gates.py ★, factory/contract.py ★, factory/state.py ★, factory/approve.py ★, factory/approvals.py ★, factory/l0.py ★, factory/orchestrator.py ★, factory/session.py ★, factory/importer.py ★, factory/cli.py, factory/rehearsal.py, factory/scoperequest.py (NEW), factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★, factory/data/schema/project.schema.json ★, factory/data/prompts/implementer.md ★, TASK-CONTRACT.md ★, VALIDATOR-SPEC.md ★, .factory/project.yaml ★ (tests/test_f65.py added to paths.protected), tests/test_f09.py ★ (prompt pin), tests/test_f65.py (NEW, protected), and the fixture scopes: tests/_support/f23.py ★, tests/_support/f30.py ★, tests/_support/f38.py ★, tests/test_f10.py ★, tests/test_f25.py ★, tests/test_f26.py ★, tests/test_f27.py ★, tests/test_f41.py ★, tests/test_f42.py ★, tests/test_f43.py ★, tests/test_f45.py ★, tests/test_f62.py ★, tests/test_f63.py ★ (task helpers; scopes honest per harness — f23/f30/f38/f26/f27/f42/f45/f63: `impl.py`, `tests/test_feature.py`; f41 adds `other.py` — its collateral-damage fixture file; f62 adds `notes/**`, `docs/**` — its egress-leak files, the test's subject; f10 keeps the default with one per-test override (`test_ac_8`: `feature.py`, its new module); f25's `_e2e_task` default). `.factory/tasks.json` and `projects/*.tasks.json` untouched.

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; class per item): - **Git tricks** (real git, temp repos — class 1, all covered by test_ac_2 or probed): rename in→out and out→in (both sides judged); `SRC/x.py` added against `src/**` — on this case-insensitive host the variant survives as its own path only when no canonical dir exists (FS-absorption probed and documented, class 1 by absorption); a case-only `git mv` (`R100 docs/Readme.md → docs/readme.md`, the old side judged); an NFD path planted in the tree the real way (`update-index --cacheinfo`, `core.precomposeunicode=false` — the bytes a Linux-built branch carries) judged out of scope against the NFC pattern; symlinks both directions (own path, never the target); chmod-only (M, judged); a submodule add (gitlink + `.gitmodules`, both judged); a name with a quote and newline (quotePath-decoded, judged).
- **Glob tricks** (class 1): `src/../factory/**` (the `..` refusal), `./src/**`, `src//x`, `src/`, `**/` alone (empty-segment refusals), `[ab]` classes (unsupported → refused) — all in test_ac_1's refused list; decision documented at `scope_pattern_error`.
- **Request tricks** (class 1, probed on `parse_scope_requests` + the orchestrator): 10 000 paths → 20 survive, 9 980 named drops; a 1 MB reason → capped at 2 000 (seam-scrubbed first); ESC `]0;` / CSI clear-line stripped by the F-43 seam in-sandbox, U+202D survives the seam and renders as the visible `\u202d` escape in the note (the queue still loads — the F-63 control refusal never trips); a homoglyph reason rides as plain letters, exactly like every other note surface (notes are not approval prompts; class 4, documented); a request naming `.factory/project.yaml` is a valid request that only escalates (nothing writes the queue; `.factory/**` protected since F-63); a request plus an out-of-scope edit in the same round escalates at once and nothing publishes (in-test); malformed entries (non-object, missing path/reason, absolute, catch-all) each dropped with the reason named.
- **Lifecycle** (class 1, in-test): a ledger-approved pre-F-65 task (no scope) refuses at run with zero seats started, parks `needs_human`, spends no budget, and the next run idles (no spin — test_ac_1); a widened scope without re-approval leaves the ledger hash stale — `recover` refuses naming the missing approval, and `begin_run`'s ledger check would refuse a run under it; `factory loop` on an all-scope_missing queue stops cleanly (the second run is `idle`).
- **Importer** (class 1, probed): a proposal carrying `**` or `/etc/passwd` in `scope.paths` refuses with the task and pattern named (`_task_violations`), and the same shapes refuse the queue at load (`_check_task_scope`) — the landed file can never be unloadable.

**Spec mismatches / amendments (recorded, not silently resolved):**
1. **AC-3's "the existing approval path" does not exist** — built the minimal one (class-2 amendment): `approve_task` refuses non-draft tasks, `recover_task` handled only credential/attempts/stale/closed, and the contract audit never demoted a `needs_human` task whose scope changed (acceptance-text changes and unledgered-`ready` demote; a parked task with an edited scope is merely reported `unledgered`). `TaskQueue.recover_scope` + the `factory recover` branch are the new path, gated on the re-approved hash at the state layer.
2. **AC-1's "a scope-less `owner_only` task still approves" is impossible today** (F-04: approving a never-executed kind has no meaning — `_check_approvable` refuses every `owner_only`). Tested as the exemption it means: the refusal names the kind, never a scope.
3. **macOS collapses case/NFD variants written onto existing paths** (probed): `SRC/x.py` added where `src/` exists records as `src/x.py` (the FS resolves the variant to the real dir); an NFD write over an NFC file records the NFC path; `core.precomposeunicode=true` re-composes even plumbing argv. The surviving real shapes — a variant add where no canonical dir exists (`A SRC/x.py`), a case-only `git mv` (`R100 docs/Readme.md → docs/readme.md`), and a tree genuinely holding NFD bytes (planted via `update-index --cacheinfo` with `precomposeunicode=false`, the bytes a Linux-built branch carries) — are the tested cases; the FS-absorbed shapes are safe by absorption (documented, probed).
4. **`--name-status -M` with `-C` off** (probed): a copy reports as `A` at the destination (judged); mode-only is `M`; a submodule pointer bump is `M`/`A` on the gitlink path plus `.gitmodules`. No path-bearing change class escapes the judgement; the one class the format cannot express is a change with NO paths (an empty commit), which gates nothing.
5. **Importer `CONTRACT_FIELDS` gains `scope`** (class 1, an AC gap): scope joined `contract_hash`, so an import that widens a frozen task's scope must demote it exactly like an acceptance/risk/gates change — the AC text names the hash, not the importer's contract-field set.

**Consequences (reported, not fixed):** every `build`/`docs` task in the real queues is scope-less until its owner adds a scope at approval — `.factory/tasks.json`: **60 of 60** eligible (26 `done`, 34 `draft`; none `ready` today, so nothing refuses to run before the next approval); `projects/jobfinderos.tasks.json`: **2 of 2** (both `draft`). Fail-closed is intended. No scopes were added to the real queues.

**What could NOT be verified:** no real (paid) implementer session was run — the request_scope tool's in-sandbox plumbing (server attach, allowed_tools grant, toolkit mcp-guard grant) is exercised only by the option-shape/seat-config tests and the parent-side transport by scripted records; the F-51 wheel data path (factory/scoperequest.py ships in the package by construction — it is a factory/ module — but no wheel was built); Linux git behaviour (the NFD-in-tree case) was probed via `precomposeunicode=false` on macOS, not on a Linux host.

### F-65 round 1 — review findings (2026-11-10)

Four findings and nine surviving mutations from the owner's review session (byte snapshot of the worktree, its own 22-mutation battery); all fixed, each with a test that catches the mutation it survived.

- **R1-1 (security, class 1): the catch-all refusal was a spelling check.** `?*/**`, `**/?*`, `**/*?*`, `?*`, `*?` each match every path (`?` is a wildcard; `?*` matches every non-empty name exactly as `*` does) and none was refused — the rule keyed on segments being literally `*`/`**`. Fix: the structural rule is now "no literal character anywhere" — `_WILDONLY_RE = [*?]+` per segment, every segment wildcard-only ⇒ refused (contract.py). test_ac_1 refuses every spelling above plus `**/`, and positively LOADS `src/**` + `a?c/**` (a single literal is enough).
- **R1-2 (security, F-43 class, class 1): the parent never scrubbed request text with the run's secrets.** `parse_scope_requests` called `clean_request_text(reason)` with no secrets; a session-written record could carry the seat token through parsing into the attempt note, progress log and findings (the in-sandbox collector's scrub cannot be assumed to have run — the record is attacker-written). Fix: `parse_scope_requests(record, secrets)`; the orchestrator passes `egress_secrets`; surviving PATHS and REASONS and every refusal line (they echo the path) scrub with them. test_ac_3 plants a fake seat token via `orch.seat_token`, files it in a reason and a refused path, and asserts the token appears in neither the note, the progress log nor the findings.
- **R1-3 (correctness, owner path, class 1): three messages told the owner to `factory approve` a non-draft task — which approve refuses.** The working route is: widen `scope.paths` in the queue → `factory adopt` (shows the contract, records the current hash for a needs_human task) → `factory recover --evidence`. Fixed in all four places: the recover prompt (approve.py), the `recover_scope` TransitionError (state.py), both orchestrator escalation notes, the scope_missing note, and the TASK-CONTRACT paragraph. test_ac_3 now drives the widen through `adopt_queue(...)` with a confirm callable — the owner's real path, not the internal ledger API.
- **R1-4 (test vacuousness, class 1): the "widened but not re-approved does not move" case ran on an already-recovered (ready) task**, so the refusal came from eligibility and the ledger gate was never exercised (the reviewer's Y7 mutation — ledger check `if False` — stayed green). Fix: the case now parks a FRESH task on `scope_change_needed`, widens its scope without adopting, and asserts the refusal names adopt, the status stays `needs_human`, and the progress log gained nothing. Y7 now goes RED.

**Surviving mutations, each with its new catcher (all RED now):** Y1 — the L0 contract-test-file exemption removed; catcher: the contract-file allowance case now uses scope `["src/**"]` ONLY (the file is allowed by the exemption, not by the scope). Y6 — scope_missing counted by `attempt_budget_used`; catcher: three planted scope_missing attempts leave the budget at 0. Y7 — the ledger check in recover_scope (R1-4). Y8 — `scope_recovery_eligible` accepting any escalation; catcher: a task parked on `review_not_converging`, adopted, still refuses the scope recover. Y15 — the parent 20-request bound removed; catcher: 25 valid requests parse to 20 kept + 5 named drops. Y17 — scope dropped from importer `CONTRACT_FIELDS`; catcher: an import widening a ready task's scope demotes it (and an unchanged scope does not). Y18 — the `_task_violations` scope check off; catcher: a proposal with `**` and `/etc/passwd` scopes reports the violation naming the task, through the REAL `build_proposal_schema`/`_load_tasks_schema`. Y20 — the SEATS grant removed survived because `build_options` also appended it; fix: ONE source (the seat config, the vent-tool shape — a grant without a server is inert), the build_options append deleted; catcher: the implementer's built options carry `mcp__request_scope__request_scope`, reviewer/verifier options do not. Y21 — the parent shape check in parse off; catcher: `../x`, `/etc/passwd`, `**` requests are dropped with named refusals, the round does NOT escalate (an in-scope diff opens the PR, three "scope request ignored" lines in the log).

**Minor:** `_scope_glob_match`'s memo bounded at `maxsize=4096` (unbounded growth in a long-lived loop/dashboard process otherwise).

**Mutation battery (re-run on the round-1 tree, cp-restored, `__pycache__` purged, tree verified byte-identical after): all 32 RED** — the original 23 plus Y1, Y6, Y7, Y8, Y15, Y17, Y18, Y20, Y21 (M2a/M2b re-anchored to the `_WILDONLY_RE` rule; M2b now lets one NEW spelling through to prove the structural rule, not the spelling, is load-bearing).

**Round-1 addendum (self-found, R1-2's class):** sweeping my own new note paths after the fixes, the 2-round `scope_change_needed` note embedded the out-of-scope git PATH — a git path the implementer NAMES, so a drift file called after the seat token would land raw in the durable attempt note (the request-note fix scrubbed request text; this sibling scrubbed nothing). Fixed: the repeat note's paths pass `scrub_session_text(path, egress_secrets)` before the display escape; test_ac_3 drifts with a file named `evil-{seat-token}.py` and asserts the token never reaches the note, with `[redacted]` visible. Battery re-run on the fixed tree: **33/33 RED** (32 above + M17b "repeat-note path unscrubbed").

### F-65 amendment — reconcile records built work without a scope (2026-11-10)

Found by the reconcile dry run that followed F-65's merge: `_check_approvable` (shared by `factory approve` and `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap`) refused every already-built build task for lacking `scope.paths` — work merged long before F-65 that no session will run again. Class 2 amendment, owner-directed: the scope is a run-time boundary, so it is demanded when an approval makes a task runnable (`factory approve`; a reconcile to `ready`/`approved`/`blocked`) and NOT when a reconcile records work already built and validated (`in_review` and beyond — `_PROVEN_REQUIRED_TARGETS`). A finished task that is ever re-opened still meets F-65's run-time `scope_missing` refusal before any session starts. `factory/approve.py` ★ (`_check_approvable(..., require_scope=)`), `tests/test_reconcile_ledger.py` (new, protected): built work reconciles scope-less to `done`/`in_review`; a reconcile to `ready`/`approved` still refuses; a scoped one passes. Mutations RED: reconcile always/never demanding scope, `factory approve`'s scope check off, the keyword default flipped.

Same PR, the ledger grammar: every entry since F-45 had drifted (status written as "F-45 → `done`" / "F-63 `done`", no per-criterion markers) and F-48/F-49/F-51/F-57/F-58 had no status line at all, so the parser dropped them as prose. Markers and status lines were added (each `proven` marker names the contract test, every one re-run green on main 8dedaaa), with three honest exceptions held by the guard test's `OWNER_PENDING`: F-48 (AC-5 manual baseline re-run), F-57 (AC-4 paid import run), F-58 (contract text predates the 2026-11-08 rescope). The guard (`test_the_repo_ledger_reconciles_cleanly`, `test_every_task_heading_is_a_ledger_entry`) reconciles the real ledger against a copy of the real queue on every suite run, so the next drift fails CI.

## F-59 — Per-project model provider and provider record (data_owner/provider profile blocks, per-kind credentials, provider-bound egress, provider record + token totals, seat overrides, no fallback)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-59 (drafted in PR #114's audit-remediation batch; owner-approved spec, built 2026-11-11 in `feat/f59-per-project-provider`). Every seat reached one provider: `session_env` hard-coded the Z.ai base URL and token, so client source would have gone to Z.ai — no DPA, processing outside the EU, a consumer plan. F-59 makes the provider a per-project, owner-approved choice, records it per run, and refuses a client repo that has not made the choice. The goal-level core: the OWNER approves the provider — `data_owner`, `provider`, `providers_allowed` and `seat_providers` are F-63 profile security groups, so a client repo cannot flip itself to `self`, change its provider or widen its allowed list without re-approval; and no path — default, fallback, quota, missing credential, import or eval — reaches Z.ai from a `client` project.

**Built per AC:**

- **AC-1 — project.yaml gains `data_owner` and `provider`:** `factory/data/schema/project.schema.json` ★ (`data_owner` enum self|client, absent = self — stated in the schema description; `provider` {kind (zai|anthropic|bedrock|vertex|anthropic_compatible), models per seat role, region?, base_url?}; `providers_allowed` (unique kind list); `seat_providers` (seat role → override block)) + `factory/contract.py` ★ `_check_provider_semantics` (called first from `_check_profile_semantics`): region required for bedrock/vertex and refused for the others (per-kind pattern — a region is never free text interpolated into a host); `base_url` required for and allowed only with `anthropic_compatible`, https only, no userinfo/query/fragment, exact-host rule (`_ALLOWLIST_HOST_RE`), no IP literal, no localhost, no trailing dot; a `client` project with no provider block or with `kind: zai` refuses at load, the reason named; `providers_allowed` may not name zai for a client; every `seat_providers` kind must come from the list. `factory/approvals.py` ★: `PROFILE_SECURITY_GROUPS` gains `data_owner`, `provider`, `providers_allowed`, `seat_providers` — the F-63 group mechanism, not a new one (a flip/change/widen refuses the run through `require_profile_approval` naming the group; YAML anchors and merge keys cannot dodge it — the hash covers the RESOLVED content, hostile-probed).
- **AC-2 — credentials are per provider:** `factory/provider.py` (NEW, protected): `keychain_service(kind)` = `factory-<kind>` (`factory-zai` unchanged), `provider_token(kind, base_url=...)` — the keychain item first, then a per-kind ambient fallback that refuses a token exported for ANOTHER provider (the F-06 ambient-URL rule generalised: zai/anthropic/anthropic_compatible read `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` only when the ambient `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` names THAT kind's endpoint — no URL means first-party, accepted only for `anthropic`; bedrock reads only `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, a name that can only mean Bedrock). The literal env builders: `anthropic_family_env` (the old `session_env`, unchanged trio) and `bedrock_env` (`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK=1` + the scoped bearer token + `AWS_REGION` + `API_TIMEOUT_MS`). No ambient credentials: never `~/.aws` files, never `AWS_PROFILE`, never gcloud ADC files, never IMDS — with the bearer token set, the pinned CLI's AWS credential chain never runs (verified in the binary: `if(!r&&!a.AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK){…credential chain…}`). `factory/sandbox.py` ★: `SESSION_ENV_ALLOWLIST` gains the three bedrock names (a deliberate, reviewable act); `sandbox_env` strips every ambient `ANTHROPIC_*`/`AWS_*`/`GOOGLE_*`/`CLOUDSDK_*`/`CLAUDE_CODE_*` from the AMBIENT half BEFORE the resolver's literal extras are laid on (never on the merged env — that would eat the seat's own `AWS_REGION`). `run_secrets(profile)` (provider.py) — the ONE source for every `egress_secrets`/`extra_secrets` tuple: every token the profile's seats resolve to (project kind + seat-override kinds), so a Bedrock token is scrubbed exactly as the Z.ai token is today. Vertex has no literal-credential path (the pinned CLI authenticates it through ADC — a credentials file or ambient lookup): it refuses at RESOLVE time with the reason named rather than weakening the rule (spec mismatch 1, below).
- **AC-3 — session egress follows the provider:** `build_settings`'s `model_endpoint` takes the resolver's `egress_hosts` (a bare string stays the one-host shape every existing caller uses); hosts derived per kind — `bedrock-runtime.<region>.amazonaws.com`, `api.anthropic.com`, the compatible `base_url`'s host, `api.z.ai` — all verified against the pinned CLI's own URL builders. "No other model endpoint": `factory/contract.py` ★ refuses any `network_allowlist`/`docs_allowlist` entry that is a known model-provider host and not EXACTLY one of the project's own inference hosts (a same-kind different-region entry refuses too — a data-residency hole, not a convenience); the known-host matcher (`model_host_owner`) recognises exact hosts plus the regional bedrock/aiplatform families, label-matched so a subdomain cannot smuggle a suffix.
- **AC-4 — provider record and token totals:** `factory/session.py` ★ `SessionRecord.provider` (stamped by the PARENT resolver's spec via `_carry`/the spec's `provider` block — never a credential) and `SessionRecord.usage` (`{input, cache_read, cache_write, output}` from the SDK usage dict, each a validated non-negative int or unknown — `_usage_counts`; `record_field_error` enforces the F-64 telemetry rule). The attempt record (`factory/orchestrator.py` ★ `attempt_record`) carries `provider` from the parent's resolver for the implementer seat (resolved once BEFORE any run dir exists — a profile whose provider cannot run here refuses as an environment failure that spends no attempt) and `token_usage` summed per attempt (any unknown count stays unknown, never 0). `factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json` ★: attempt `provider` (required kind; model/region/endpoint_host) + `token_usage` (integer|null per field) — both optional, so pre-F-59 attempts load. `factory/status.py` ★: a `## Providers` section — per kind+model token totals and an estimated cost from the owner-maintained price table (`factory/config.py` ★ `prices`: kind → model → {input, cache_read, cache_write, output} USD per million, validated, malformed = ConfigError); a missing price renders `n/a`, never `$0.00`; an invalid usage count renders `unknown`, never 0; pre-F-59 attempts render as one legacy line, never folded into a provider row. `render_status(config=…)` accepts a loaded config; None loads `<factory_root>/config.yaml`.
- **AC-5 — seat overrides and no fallback:** `factory/provider.py` `resolve_provider(profile, seat)`: the seat's model from `provider.models[role]`; a `seat_providers` override resolves its own kind/credential/host (its host joins only that seat's settings — each `_run_prepared` builds its own — never the implementer's). No fallback, anywhere: a missing credential for the seat's OWN provider is a named refusal in `_run_prepared`/`run_session_sandboxed` (kind + keychain item + the env name, "never falls back to another provider"); errors and quota requeue re-enter the same profile → the same resolver → the same provider (there is no alternate-provider code path to fall into — the requeue is F-64's, unchanged); `_QUOTA` gains `throttl` (Bedrock's ThrottlingException shape) so the quota requeue still works on the SAME provider. `role=` on `run_session_sandboxed` lets the importer's reviewer-CONFIG seat run the importer-ROLE provider/model.
- **AC-6 — one resolution for run, import and eval:** every session construction goes through the resolver: `_run_prepared` (the ONE per-session prepare/run/parse step — single-session, fan-out, import, eval), `_gate_settings` and `probe_session_interpreter` (seat=None → the project's own provider), the importer (`sdk_import_session` + the repair session, `role="importer"`). The spec file carries the provider as NAMES (`env_keys`, `credential_key`) and the record block — never the credential value (the file is session-readable); the in-sandbox entry (`_main`) rebuilds the literal env from exactly those keys and refuses without the credential. No call site keeps a `DEFAULT_BASE_URL`/`seat_token()` path of its own: `session.py` keeps only zai-kind delegates (`seat_token` → `provider_token("zai")`, `session_env` → `anthropic_family_env`, `KEYCHAIN_SERVICE = keychain_service("zai")`) for the F-06 contract tests; `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` itself is gone from session.py — `ZAI_BASE_URL` lives in the resolver alone.
- **AC-7 — the owner's paid run:** runbook below; `pending` (owner).

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief): factory/provider.py (NEW, protected), factory/session.py ★, factory/sandbox.py ★, factory/orchestrator.py ★, factory/importer.py ★, factory/contract.py ★, factory/approvals.py ★, factory/cli.py, factory/config.py, factory/status.py, factory/publish.py (docstring contract), factory/data/schema/project.schema.json ★, factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★, tests/conftest.py ★ (the no-spend guard stubs the resolver's keychain seam and strips `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` with the ANTHROPIC_* names), .factory/project.yaml ★ (`factory/provider.py` + `tests/test_f59.py` added to paths.protected), tests/test_f59.py (NEW, protected), and the seam repairs the WO's moves forced in other WOs' protected test files (each ★, each a mechanical seam migration, listed with its reason): tests/test_f06.py ★ (import ZAI_BASE_URL; refusal-half and keychain patches → the provider seam; the entry-spec test writes the F-59 spec shape), tests/test_f24.py ★ (import), tests/test_f12.py ★, tests/test_f43.py ★ (the AST secrets-shape lint accepts `run_secrets(...)` — the resolver's normaliser, tuple by construction, one deliberate widening; the mutation fixtures rewritten for the new source lines; patch seams), tests/test_f49.py ★, tests/test_f51.py ★ (the f06-plugin mechanism patches the resolver seam), tests/test_f56.py ★ + tests/test_importer_schema_shapes.py ★ (entry-spec shape), tests/test_f57.py ★, tests/test_f62.py ★, tests/test_f65.py ★, tests/test_importer_limits.py ★, and the pairless in-process `run_session(` calls that gained `base_url=` explicitly (tests/test_f56.py ★; the rest already passed it). `.factory/tasks.json` and `projects/*.tasks.json` untouched; `~/factory` untouched (marker check below).

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; class per item):
- **Client-written profile** (class 1, all refused): `data_owner: self` slipped in after approval → group hash changes, run refuses naming `data_owner`; `provider.kind` changed → `provider`; `region: "eu-north-1.evil.com"` → the region pattern refuses at load (never reaches a host); `base_url` with userinfo / non-https / IP / localhost / trailing dot → each refused, the reason named; `network_allowlist: [api.z.ai]` on a client bedrock project → the model-host refusal (also probed: `bedrock-runtime.us-east-1.amazonaws.com` on an eu-north-1 project — another REGION refuses); `providers_allowed` widened → group hash; YAML anchors/merge keys → the hash covers the RESOLVED content (an anchored block hashes identical to its literal, and a merge-key region change is detected — probed, no dodge).
- **Ambient environment** (class 1): the resolver run with every `ANTHROPIC_*`, `AWS_*`, `GOOGLE_*`, `CLOUDSDK_*` and `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*` variable planted — NONE reaches a session env or a settings file (probed over `sandbox_env` and `build_settings` output: the only provider names in the child env are the resolver's own, with the resolver's values); the resolver's own values are not overwritten by same-named ambient variables.
- **Wrong token** (class 1): a `factory-anthropic` token present but the project names bedrock → the anthropic token is never read for bedrock (different name) and never sent; the ambient `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` under a foreign `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is refused for every anthropic-family kind (probed).
- **Session-side** (class 1): a scripted record claiming `provider.kind: zai` on a client bedrock run — the attempt's provider is the parent resolver's (probed through a real Env run in test_ac_4(e)); a forged usage of −1/"9"/1e30/`true` → `record_field_error` refuses the record field (`usage.input: a int, not a non-negative integer`); a token echoed in result text → seam-scrubbed (probed).
- **Fallback** (class 1): missing credential → the named refusal (`no bedrock credential available … factory-bedrock`), no Z.ai env or host anywhere; quota → the same provider requeues (F-64's path, `ThrottlingException` classified); unknown error → the attempt records on the same provider.
- **Self project** (class 1): the factory's own profile resolves zai with exactly today's three env names and `api.z.ai` (probed); the F-64 quota requeue tests pass unchanged.

**Spec mismatches / amendments (report, not resolved):**
1. **Vertex refuses at resolve time** (AC-2's anticipated mismatch): the pinned CLI authenticates Vertex through google-auth-library ADC — `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` or `~/.config/gcloud/application_default_credentials.json` (both verified in the binary) — and exposes no literal token env for it. Rather than weaken the no-ambient-credentials rule, `resolve_provider` refuses `vertex` with the reason named. The kind still loads (AC-1's schema admits it) so the owner can approve the shape before a literal path exists.
2. **UNVERIFIED names/hosts** (verified against the pinned CLI binary `claude-agent-sdk 2.1.301` `_bundled/claude` — names, URL templates and the bearer-token short-circuit; the WIRE behavior is the owner's AC-7 run to confirm): (a) `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` accepted by the EU endpoint and the model IDs available in-region; (b) whether a Bedrock session also needs `ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL` pinned (the CLI maps small/fast calls per provider — if the run's stderr names a model miss, add it to the profile's models map in a follow-up); (c) the exact reason string a Bedrock throttle produces (the `throttl` classification covers ThrottlingException and 429; AC-7's record confirms).
3. **`data_owner` defaulting to `self` when absent is the spec's reading** — stated in the schema description. Audited the repo's own and the projects/ drafts: none names `data_owner` today and all are owner repos, so the default is safe for every profile that exists; the first client repo MUST set it (and its provider) at onboarding — the load refusal enforces the provider half.
4. **AC amendment (class 2):** `seat_providers` (the seat→override map AC-5 needs) joins the security groups beside `providers_allowed` — a client re-pointing a seat to another provider is exactly the flip the WO exists to refuse; the AC text named only `providers_allowed`, the group covers the map that consumes it.
5. **Design note (class 2):** Bedrock v1 supports the scoped bearer token only. "Short-lived credentials passed literally" (AC-2's alternative) has no hermetic source under the one-keychain-item-per-kind rule — an owner-directed follow-up can add a structured keychain item if provisioning ever yields session credentials instead of a bearer token.

**AC-7 runbook (owner; paid):**
1. Create the keychain item: `security add-generic-password -s factory-bedrock -w '<the scoped bearer token>'`.
2. The profile: the rehearsal fixture (F-24, `factory/rehearsal.py`'s `RehearsalFixture` repo), with `.factory/project.yaml` gaining `data_owner: client` and `provider: {kind: bedrock, region: eu-north-1, models: {implementer: anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5, reviewer: anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5, verifier: anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5, importer: anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5}}` — then `factory approve --profile` for it (the F-63 ledger; the new groups are printed).
3. The exact command: `factory run --queue <fixture>/.factory/tasks.json --factory-root <a fresh root>` (rehearsal fixture's one-criterion task; F-24's script).
4. What the record must show: the attempt's `provider` = `{kind: bedrock, model: anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5, region: eu-north-1}` and `token_usage` with non-null counts; NO credential anywhere in the queue, the trace or the PR body (grep the token literal).
5. How to read the egress log for "no other model host": the run's srt settings (`<root>/sandbox/*.json`) `network.allowedDomains` must be exactly `bedrock-runtime.eu-north-1.amazonaws.com` plus the fixture's own allowlist entries — no `api.z.ai`, no `api.anthropic.com`; the session record's `provider.kind` is `bedrock`.
6. Checklist of the UNVERIFIED items (mismatch 2): bearer token accepted in-region; small/fast model mapping; the throttle reason string lands as a quota classification (a free requeue, not an attempt).

## F-67 — Importer v0 gates, session record and shipped-work flag (gate normalisation, import session records, possibly_shipped, dropped-criteria accounting)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-67 (drafted in #126, owner-approved by merging it 2026-11-11). The F-13 pilot's fresh 34-task import showed the importer — the only writer that turns a project's planning docs into contracts — could not yet be trusted: it proposed `deploy: auto` on 15 risk-flagged build tasks and `spec_approval: false` on 23; left no record of its own sessions (cost and model unknown afterwards); re-proposed work already on main as open build tasks; and silently dropped criteria the docs still carry, including WO-19's URL-exfiltration guard. F-67 closes all four PARENT-side: the session only ever proposes, the owner decides. Built 2026-11-11 in `feat/f67-importer-v0-gates`.

**Built per AC:**

- **AC-1 — imported gates are human in v0:** `factory/importer.py` ★ `_normalise_gates` — the ONE normalisation step inside `plan_import`, applied to EVERY task the importer writes (new and merged) right after `_task_from_proposal`, BEFORE the proposal-schema check and the merge: `merge`/`deploy` forced `"human"` and `spec_approval` forced `True` for `ELIGIBLE_KINDS` kinds, whatever the proposal says (`"auto"`, `"Human"`, a bool, a missing key — all forced, all recorded). Other kinds keep the proposal's value (the pilot's owner_only tasks carry `spec_approval: false` by design — probed). Every change lands on the plan as a `GateOverride` (task, field, proposed, stored, new/existing) and `render_summary` lists them under their own heading BEFORE the diff. F-57's contract-change demote rule stays (a normalised proposal now changes gates only against a stored non-human gate — the demote rides real contract changes); F-12 AC-3's omitted-field rule stays (a merged task whose proposal omits `gates` keeps its stored gates; a stored non-human gate is caught by the approve check). Prompts: `build_import_prompt` and `build_repair_prompt` both state all three rules next to the merge line. `factory/approve.py` ★ `check_v0_gates` (called from `_check_approvable` before the F-65 scope check, single AND reconcile approvals) refuses `merge`/`deploy` ≠ `"human"` naming task and field — the hand-edited/pre-F-67 queue backstop; `spec_approval` is never judged there.
- **AC-2 — every import session is on record:** `factory/importer.py` ★ `_write_session_record` — one JSON record per session under `<factory-root>/import/` as `<project>-<run id>.record.json` (the refused-output writer's naming/dir, mode 0600, O_EXCL; telemetry, never a gate — a failed write never masks the outcome it records). Contents: the PARENT's `resolve_provider(profile, "importer").provider_record()` (never the session-written `record.provider` — F-59 R2-1 re-applied here), the session-reported `model` (or the string `unknown`, never null), F-64-validated usage (`_validated_usage`), turns (`_record_turns`), outcome, run id, project, and the kept proposal file's name when one exists; the whole payload passes `scrub_session_value` with the run's `egress_secrets` (a token echoed anywhere in the record's text never reaches the file). Written for the main session on every exit — accepted (after the summary, before the confirm), refused (after the repair, naming the kept original), aborted (`run_import` wraps `sdk_import_session`, whose new `run_sink` fires exactly once per run attempt with the record in hand, or a best-effort file read on record-loss/timeout; could-not-start refusals fire nothing — nothing was spent) — and for each repair session inside `_repair_refused_proposal` (repaired/refused/failed/quota_stopped/record_lost/record_unusable; could-not-start paths write nothing). `_abort_refusal` now returns the salvage's file name with the message; `keep_refused_import_output` attaches `.saved_path` to the kept refusal. `factory/status.py` ★ `_import_session_rows`/`_render_import_sessions`: one row per record under the Providers section (run+outcome, provider kind, model, the four usage columns, est. cost under the same price table), unknown counts rendered `unknown` through `_column`/`_fmt_millions`, malformed files degraded to a note. The stub path writes nothing (`keep` gate) — no test touches the owner's root (marker check below).
- **AC-3 — possibly shipped work is flagged, never decided:** proposal schema (`build_proposal_schema`, both sides) gains the bounded top-level `possibly_shipped` (≤200 entries of `{task_id, evidence, reason}`, string caps); `_validate_possibly_shipped` re-checks parent-side (count ≤ the proposal's task count, every entry names a proposed task, evidence non-empty and repo-relative — `..`/absolute/NUL refused) and checks path-shaped evidence against the repo (`evidence not found` flagged, never refused). `render_summary` shows the list FIRST (before the overrides and the diff), every string through `escape_approval_text`. Nothing changes any task because of the list (mutation 14 proves it). The propose_task path carries the field via the final text answer (`_carry_proposal_toplevel`: the two whitelisted lists ride a parsing JSON text answer onto the accepted-set proposal — the collector is per-task). The prompt teaches the HEAD check (files, functions, migrations, a status line in the repo's own records) with `path:line`/commit-sha evidence.
- **AC-4 — criteria the docs still carry are never silently dropped:** `read_reference_queue` — the repo's own `.factory/tasks.json` read as PLAIN JSON (never schema-validated; it may predate F-64 — the pilot's landed queue fails today's schema 22 times), bounded (20MB stat cap, `MAX_PROPOSAL_DEPTH` nesting, JSON-shape refusal — clean refusals, never a crash, never a write) and used EVEN when `--queue` points at a fresh file; no file → the summary says the check had nothing to account for. `read_kept_doc_texts` — the kept docs' texts, bounded per file (10MB), for the doc-line checks. The proposal schema gains task `predecessors` (≤50), acceptance-item `from` (`<ref task>/<ref AC id>`) and the bounded top-level `dropped_criteria` (`{ref_task, ref_ac?, doc, line, reason}`); `_task_from_proposal` extracts and strips them (they never reach tasks.json — asserted). `_account_reference` — the deterministic accounting: every reference task accounted (same id / some predecessor / dropped whole) and every criterion mapped (`from`, or the same-id carry: the re-proposed task holds the same AC id), dropped, or REFUSED by name; every `from` target verified against the reference; every drop's `doc:line` verified against the kept docs' real line counts (a doc outside the kept set, or a line past EOF, refuses). `still_in_docs` — the reference criterion's text, whitespace-collapsed and casefolded, found in any kept doc, EXCEPT when the drop's own cited line carries a done-marker (`[x]`, ✓, done/shipped/superseded/executed/completed/landed) and quotes the criterion. `render_summary` renders the drops (after possibly_shipped) with the flag marked; `apply_import`'s new `confirm_drop` refuses the write per UNACCEPTED flagged drop (no callable = no acceptance — there is deliberately no blanket yes), the CLI asks per drop by name, `run_import` threads the callable. The accounting runs inside `plan_import` (both the proposal's and the repair's) via the new `reference`/`doc_texts`/`repo` kwargs — optional with None defaults so direct callers keep today's shape; `run_import` always passes the real ones.
