Both the strengthened test and the retargeted mutation are part of this WO's red/green.

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; class per item — class 1 = refused/contained by design, verified by probe):

- **Gate tricks** (class 1): gates `"Human"` / `" human"` / merge `42` → all forced to `human` with the override listed (recorded, visible — the AC's "whatever the proposal says"); an extra key in `gates` → refused by the proposal schema (`additionalProperties`); `gates` absent on a new task → the all-human defaults, zero overrides; `gates` omitted on a MERGED task → the stored gates survive verbatim, and a stored `deploy: auto` (planted the pre-F-67 way) is refused by `check_v0_gates` at approval — probed end to end through `approve_task`.
- **Record tricks** (class 1): a forged record (negative/10³⁰/bool/string usage, a claimed provider, negative turns) → REFUSED at the sdk session's own `record_field_error` boundary (defence in depth firing before the writer); driven straight at `_write_session_record` (the abort-salvation path's shape), the F-64 rule renders every forged count `unknown`, model `unknown`, never raw; the token echoed into the session's reported model/result → scrubbed from every record file (literal scrub with the run's `egress_secrets`); a record path collision (all run ids forced equal) → O_EXCL refuses the overwrite, the run completes, telemetry for the loser is lost, nothing else.
- **Shipped-work tricks** (class 1): evidence `../outside.py:1` and `/etc/passwd` → refused naming the entry; ESC bytes in evidence → carried (flagged `evidence not found`) and escaped at render — control bytes cannot survive the egress scrub on the run path (test_ac_3 e); a 1MB reason → refused by the 500-char schema cap; 10 000 entries → refused (schema maxItems 200 AND the parent's count-vs-tasks rule); an entry for a task the proposal omits → refused naming it.
- **Accounting tricks** (class 1): a `from` naming `pilot-19/AC-9` (nonexistent) → refused by name; two criteria claiming the same `from` while another goes unaccounted → the unaccounted one refuses; `dropped_criteria` line 9999 (past EOF) → refused naming doc and count; a drop citing `CLAUDE.md` (outside the kept planning docs) → refused; a 21MB / not-JSON / 200-deep reference queue → each a clean `ImportRefused` (never a crash, never a write — the reference file verified byte-identical after all probes).
- **Real shape** (the pilot's own artefacts, read-only: `/tmp/jfo-f13-import/tasks.json` as the proposal, `origin/main:.factory/tasks.json` (32 tasks) as the reference, the repo's 54 kept docs): the accounting REFUSES the fresh proposal wholesale — 31 unaccounted items, `wo19-honest-gates-a-c (the whole task)` among them: the WO-19 URL criterion (`AC-2`, "Prompts never fetch or output URLs found inside posting bodies…") shows up as UNACCOUNTED, exactly the hole F-67 exists to close. The second half, probed honestly: with the drop the session SHOULD have proposed (citing `WO-19-honest-gates.md:141`), `still_in_docs` is FALSE — the doc line paraphrases the criterion ("never fetch URLs that appear inside the posting body"), and the mechanical rule is exact-text. That false negative is the rule's stated failure mode (below); the drop still RENDERS in every summary, only the blocking flag is missed.

**Spec mismatches / amendments (report, not resolved):**

1. **"Still in docs" is exact-text and the pilot itself paraphrases.** The chosen rule (the reference AC text, whitespace-collapsed and casefolded, found in any kept doc; a done-marked cited line exempts) misses a PARAPHRASED criterion — proven on the real artefact above (WO-19's doc line vs its queued AC text). Failure modes, both directions: paraphrase → false negative (the owner still sees the drop listed; only the per-drop acceptance gate is skipped); a done-marked quote exempts the drop even if another unmarked copy exists elsewhere (rare; needs both the marker and the criterion text on the cited line). A semantic matcher is F-60/F-66 territory, not a v0 heuristic.
2. **The importer prompt carries no version/hash to bump.** `prompt_versions` (F-10 AC-6) covers implementer/reviewer/verifier only; `build_import_prompt`/`build_repair_prompt` are unversioned. The three gate rules and the accounting teaching are covered by tests on the prompt text instead (`test_prompt_carries_the_hard_rules` and test_f67's prompt arms).
3. **The propose_task path cannot carry the accounting fields per-task** (spec mismatch 3 anticipated): the collector is per-task, so `possibly_shipped`/`dropped_criteria` travel in the final text answer (`_carry_proposal_toplevel`) — a session that uses the tool and answers a bare one-line confirmation files no shipped-work flags. The bridge is deliberate and minimal; a structured channel would be new tool surface (not this WO's).
4. **AC-4 class-2 amendment (the owner-added-task friction):** unaccounted means REFUSED, full stop — including a reference task the OWNER added by hand after an import (no doc line can back its drop; the session never saw it). The honest exits today: re-propose it unchanged, or drop it citing a doc line the owner accepts. A first-class "owner task" accounting class is an F-66-era queue-migration question, named here so it is not rediscovered as a bug.
5. **M11's original form was vacuous** (per-row rendering holds one value per column — no partial sum exists); retargeted to the real seam with the strengthened partial-usage test. Recorded above.
6. **R1-1 (HIGH, review round 1) — class-2 amendment of AC-4's blocking rule and review surface.** The reviewer's probes, reproduced on the round-0 tree: (a) a DECLARED drop citing a doc that PARAPHRASES the criterion applied with zero prompts (the exact-text `still_in_docs` heuristic decided blocking, and paraphrase defeats it — the pilot's own WO-19 doc words the URL rule differently from the queued criterion); (b) a "mapping" of pilot-19/AC-2 onto `Fix the footer typo` applied with NO trace of the criterion's text or its reference key anywhere in the summary — the accounting proved the claim, the review showed nothing of what the claim did to the meaning. Amended: drops need per-drop acceptance BY DEFAULT (`needs_acceptance`); the sole exemption is a cited line carrying a done-marker IN the reference task's own `source` doc (the "and quotes the criterion" condition removed — paraphrase defeats it both ways); `still_in_docs` is advisory rendering only; and a new "Rewritten criteria" section (after the drops, before the diff) shows every mapping whose proposed text, normalised, differs from the reference text — the same-id carry included, escaped, capped at 20 with the overflow count stated. Round-0 tests (a)/(c) re-seated to the amended semantics; arms (f) paraphrase-blocks, (g) cross-doc-done-line-does-not-exempt, (h) the rewritten section (bogus mapping + same-id carry) added red-first.
7. **R1-2 (MEDIUM) — the reconcile guard narrowed back.** The round-0 widening (any "dependency not met" accepted for every entry) was a protected guard made weaker for everyone. Now `_owner_held`: a dependency wait is owner-held only when EVERY unmet dependency it names is itself in OWNER_PENDING; a live negative test reconciles an entry whose dep is not owner-pending and asserts it stays a failure (mutation 25 red).
8. **R1-3 (LOW) — the pyright suppression removed.** `ImportRefused.saved_path: str | None = None` is a declared attribute now; `PYRIGHT_IGNORE_ALLOWLIST` back to 4.
9. **R2-1 (HIGH, review round 2) — class-2 amendment of AC-4's exemption.** The reviewer's probe, reproduced on the round-1 tree: a line about ONE criterion ("- AC-1 trust boundary block: [x] done, shipped") added to pilot-19's own source doc, then cited to drop pilot-19/AC-2 (a DIFFERENT criterion) — APPLIED with zero prompts. The R1 exemption (done-marker + own source doc) still let the session choose which line to cite; the reviewer notes the hole was their instruction's (nothing replaced the removed quotes-condition). Amended: the exemption is REMOVED ENTIRELY — every drop needs the owner's per-drop acceptance. The evidence survives as advisory labels in the Dropped criteria section ("cited line marks done", "cited doc is the task's own source", "still in docs") so the owner decides with it in view. AC text's "a drop backed by a doc line that marks the work done applies" now means "applies on the owner's per-drop yes". Owner cost: one y per drop (the pilot's accounting carried ~6).
10. **R2-2 (MEDIUM) — the Rewritten section also shows CHECK changes.** A mapping that keeps a criterion's text but turns its test check manual (`{"type": "manual", "expect": "looks fine"}`) removes the enforcement while keeping the words — invisible under the text-only comparison. `_rewritten_criteria` now compares the check too (type, or a test check's ref — manual-expectation rewording is a note, not lost enforcement), the fixture reference's criteria are all TEST-backed so the arm exercises a real test→manual downgrade, and the row names what changed (`[text]`, `[check]`, `[text+check]`) with both values shown, escaped.

**What could NOT be verified:** the REAL session path end to end (a paid import through srt with a live credential — the record contents beyond the ScriptedRunner seam, the run id's uniqueness under real `create_run` minting, the abort-salvage record on a real wall-clock kill); the repair session's behaviour on a real quota/timeout record (scripted shapes only); `factory status`'s import rows against the owner's real `~/factory` (rendered over tmp roots); and the second JobFinderOS import itself (F-13 step 3 — the owner runs it on this code after merge; the real-shape probe above is the accounting's verdict on the artefacts it will see, read-only).

## F-71 — Pre-spend contract checks and dependency policy (scope risk coverage, runnable refs, budget floor, declared dependencies)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-71 (drafted in #129, owner-approved by merging it 2026-11-11). The F-13 pilot's first two paid JobFinderOS runs failed on mistakes the factory could have caught for free: run 1 starved (30 turns, died twice before any validation layer), run 2 escalated `risk_path_touched` (scope reached two `external_service` paths its risk did not declare) and `new_dependency` (L0 escalated EVERY manifest change, so a contract whose own criterion was "pin pypdf" could never pass). A sweep found 13 of 22 build tasks with undeclared risk paths, 5 with ≤ 40 turns, and an earlier import with 0 of 65 refs in exact `file::test` form. F-71 moves each check to import and approval — where it costs nothing — and gives dependencies a policy. Built 2026-11-11 in `feat/f71-pre-spend-checks`.

**Built per AC:**

- **AC-1 — risk flags cover the scope:** `factory/gates.py` ★ `scope_risk_flags(scope_paths, risk_globs, tracked_files)` — the ONE shared implementation. Candidates: every DEFAULT-BRANCH tracked file (`git ls-tree -r --name-only <default ref>`, never the working tree) matching any scope glob (`scope_matches`), plus every literal (glob-free) scope path (files the task will create). Each candidate runs through the SAME risk matcher L0 uses (`matches` via `matched_risk_flags`'s rule — fnmatch, `*` crossing `/`), never a second matcher. **Approval:** `factory/approve.py` ★ `_prespend_checks` loads the profile from the `approve --profile` default (next to `--queue`; a missing/unloadable profile REFUSES a runnable kind, the path named — fail closed — and so does a profile naming another project than the queue: the F-62 pair rule, self-review hardening found during the build) and the repo from `repo.path`; a `risk` lacking a computed flag refuses, each flag named with up to 3 reaching paths. Reconcile takes the same checks on the runnable boundary (`_reconcile_entry`: `_check_approvable` first — the F-65 scope refusal still leads — then the pre-spend checks, refusals as failed RESULTS); adopt_queue deliberately does NOT (it records existing approvals, approves nothing — spec mismatch 1). **Importer:** `_normalise_task` (the F-67 normalisation step, extended in place — not forked) ADDS missing flags to `risk` (a union, never a removal) over the EFFECTIVE scope/risk (proposed if carried, else stored), using the default branch's tracked files computed once per plan (a repo whose default branch cannot be listed refuses the import — fail closed); each addition is a `RiskAddition` rendered in the Gate overrides section before the diff. A task that gains a flag is risk-flagged, so `--yes` stays refused as today.
- **AC-2 — check refs are runnable before approval:** `factory/approve.py` ★ `_ref_runnable_error` (refusing: no `::`, empty/absolute/`..` path, empty or padded names, a bare `.py` path; empty/whitespace command refs) applied on the runnable boundary in `_check_approvable` — single approvals AND runnable reconcile targets; the refusal names the criterion and the exact ref. `protected_ref_warnings` computes the WARNING set (a ref whose file matches `paths.protected` while `scope_matches` says the scope cannot touch it); the WARNING block renders INSIDE `render_contract` beside the criteria it names, the owner answers the same y/N prompt, and `--yes` is refused for a task carrying it (a refusal of `--yes`, not of the approval). The importer refuses an inexact ref in a proposal the same way (`_normalise_task`, judged on what the proposal carries — a merged task whose proposal omits acceptance keeps its stored criteria for approval to judge), and both prompts (`build_import_prompt`, `build_repair_prompt`) teach the exact-ref rule with the new-file convention `tests/test_<task>.py::test_ac_N`.
- **AC-3 — a budget floor the owner sets once:** the profile schema ★ gains optional `budget_floor {max_turns, wall_clock_min}`; `factory/contract.py` ★ `budget_floor()` resolves the declared floor over the named-constant defaults (`BUDGET_FLOOR_DEFAULT_TURNS=60`, `BUDGET_FLOOR_DEFAULT_WALL_MIN=30` — the owner's to confirm at review), and `budget_caps()` reads F-64's maxima FROM the tasks schema so the load-time refusal (a floor above a cap, the field named) can never drift from the cap it bounds. Approval refuses a runnable task below the floor, the field and both values named; the importer clamps UP only (never down) and lists each clamp with the overrides. `factory/approvals.py` ★ gains the F-63 group `budget_floor` — changing it invalidates the profile approval. F-64's caps and the fixed per-seat caps untouched.
- **AC-4 — declared dependencies pass without a human:** the task schema ★ gains optional `dependencies: [{ecosystem: pypi|npm, name}]` (inside `contract_hash` — never added to `CONTRACT_HASH_EXCLUDED_FIELDS`; importer `SPEC_FIELDS` extended so proposals may carry it; `render_contract` shows the list; `--yes` refused for a contract that declares any). **Parsing** (`factory/deps.py`, NEW): deterministic base-vs-head blobs at the refs L0 already has — `requirements*.txt` (name + `==`-pin, `-r`/`-c` includes recursed with depth/cycle bounds; URLs, VCS refs, `-e`, index options and environment markers REFUSED — they are not deterministically parseable), `pyproject.toml` (the F-31 surface comparison moved here verbatim; `[project] dependencies`/`optional-dependencies` extracted; a change in any dependency-bearing surface OUTSIDE those two tables — poetry/uv/dependency-groups/build-system/dynamic — escalates), `package.json` (the four dependency maps), `requirements*.lock` and `package-lock.json` v2/v3 `packages` (a v1 lock refuses). "Added" = absent at base or a different pinned version; a REMOVED direct dependency still escalates (the contract's `dependencies` declare additions, never removals — the F-31 rule kept). **Rule:** every added direct package must be declared (normalised: PEP 503 / lowercase); every added package, direct or transitive, must pass the registry checks; all pass → no escalation; anything else escalates with the failing package and check in the report. **Registry checks** (`factory/registry.py`, NEW, parent-side): exists (the registry answered for THAT name), first release ≥ 30 days old by the parent's UTC clock (`FIRST_RELEASE_MIN_AGE_DAYS`, future timestamps fail), and not within Damerau-Levenshtein-1 of a shipped top-package name it is not itself (a name that IS on the list skips the scan — its siblings are the lists' own false-positive class). The lists ship as package data (`factory/data/top_packages/{pypi,npm}.txt`, 500 names each, generated once by `scripts/generate_top_packages.py`, source and date in the headers; `pyproject.toml` package-data extended — the F-51 wheel glob covers them). One injectable client seam (`RegistryClient(get_json=…)` → `_http_get_json`: timeout 10s, 1 MiB cap, JSON parse); timeout, non-200, malformed JSON, an over-size body or a registry answering for another name fails the package — fail closed. Results land on the L0 report as `dependency_checks` (`l0.py` ★: `run_l0(registry_client=…)` builds a `DependencyAnalyser` and passes it to `check_diff_policy`, which keeps today's blanket escalation for manifest touches the analyser does not prove safe; direct callers without an analyser keep the pre-F-71 rule).

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief or repo-protected): factory/approve.py ★, factory/importer.py ★, factory/l0.py ★, factory/contract.py ★, factory/gates.py ★, factory/approvals.py ★, factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★, factory/data/schema/project.schema.json ★, .factory/project.yaml ★ (`tests/test_f71.py`, `factory/deps.py`, `factory/registry.py`, `scripts/generate_top_packages.py` added to `paths.protected` — the dependency policy and its list generator are judge surface), factory/cli.py (approve `--yes` help), pyproject.toml (package-data), factory/deps.py (NEW ★), factory/registry.py (NEW ★), scripts/generate_top_packages.py (NEW ★), factory/data/top_packages/{pypi,npm}.txt (NEW, under the protected `factory/data/**`), tests/conftest.py ★ (suite-wide registry-network guard — the seam fails closed unless stubbed, the `_no_ambient_spend` posture), tests/test_f71.py (NEW ★), tests/_support/f71.py (NEW, the fixture harness — no JobFinderOS content, shapes only), and the honest fixture re-seats this WO's semantics forced in other WOs' protected test files (each ★, each mechanical): tests/test_f04.py ★ (`_queue_file` gains the profile+repo an approval now reads), tests/test_f63.py ★ (fixture budget 20→60 turns, above the default floor; the hash-canonicalisation arms follow), tests/test_reconcile_ledger.py ★ (`_reconcile` gains the profile+repo; fixture budget above the floor — a runnable reconcile target takes the floor check), tests/test_importer_schema_shapes.py ★ (fixture refs gain `::test_ac_1` — the F-71 exact-ref rule refuses the bare-path shape those fixtures used to carry). TASK-CONTRACT.md ★ and VALIDATOR-SPEC.md ★ swept for the new rules (budget row + dependencies row + budget_floor row + scope note; L0's new_dependency paragraph rewritten). `.factory/tasks.json` untouched; `~/factory`, `~/Desktop/JobFinderOS` and every jfo-* worktree untouched (marker checks below; the pilot artefacts were read from origin/main only).

**Red first** (tests/test_f71.py on the untouched tree 9c55d63): `test_ac_1`/`test_ac_2`/`test_ac_3` RED as `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ApprovalError` — the undeclared risk flag, the prose ref and the 30-turn budget all approved; `test_ac_4` RED as `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'factory.registry'` (the F-59/F-65 ImportError precedent: the feature's absence is the red).

**Green:** tests/test_f71.py 4 passed. **Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code; class per item — class 1 = refused/contained by design, verified by probe):

- **Risk** (class 1): a scope glob case-variant from the risk file (`SVC/**` vs `svc/pdf_service.py`) reaches nothing (case-sensitive, the L0 scope-matcher semantics); a `*`-crossing risk glob (`billing/*`) reaches `billing/deep/invoice.py` where a segment-wise matcher would not — pinned by test arm (b2); a symlink is tracked with git mode 120000 and judged by its OWN path (a risk glob naming the link reaches; the target is irrelevant — L0's rule); a renamed risk file reaches nothing under its old name (the default branch carries the new name only — a renamed risk file needs its profile glob updated, a profile-content question, same as L0); a scope whose only risk match was DELETED on the default branch reaches nothing through the glob, while a literal scope path still counts (the task may create it — and L0's risk matcher catches the created file at run time).
- **Refs** (class 1): `tests/test_x.py::` / `::test_a` / `tests/../factory/x.py::t` each refuse, the shape named; ESC/bidi bytes in a ref refuse at QUEUE LOAD (the F-63 control-string walk names `U+001B`), and `render_contract` escapes every value as the second layer; 10 000 criteria render in 0.04s, linear, no crash.
- **Dependencies** (class 1): `Py_PDF`/`py.pdf` normalise to `py-pdf` — a DIFFERENT family from `pypdf` (PYPDF), exactly PEP 503; a package added to `requirements.txt` and pinned only in the lock is one package with one check (the run-2 shape, below); a dependency smuggled via `setup.cfg`/`setup.py`/`Pipfile` is judged ONLY if the profile lists the file in `dependency_manifests` — neither shipped profile does (the factory's lists `pyproject.toml`; JobFinderOS lists its five) — a profile-content hole, not a code hole; `package-lock.json` with 50 000 entries parses in 0.04s; a 1 GB response refuses at the real seam's 1 MiB cap (probed against the real `_http_get_json`); a registry answering for another name fails; npm scoped names normalise and are asked lowercased (`@Scope/Util` → `@scope/util`); a manifest that changes line endings only parses to the identical set (no additions, no escalation); `-r` include cycles refuse at the depth bound; markers, URLs, `-e` and `--index-url` lines refuse.
- **Registry lists** (class 1, measured): the shipped lists' DL-1 self-collision rate is 4.0% (pypi, 10 pairs of 500) and 5.2% (npm, 13 pairs) — all sibling pairs of real packages (`psycopg`~`psycopg2`, `httpx`~`httpx2`, `aiofile`~`aiofiles`, `@tsconfig/node10`~`node12`…). The check skips the scan for a name that is itself on the list (it has proven popularity), which removes the whole class for list members; a NEW package within DL-1 of a list name still flags — the defense working as specified.
- **Real shape** (read-only): JobFinderOS's landed queue + profile from origin/main — **18 of 23 build/docs tasks would refuse: 14 on risk coverage** (`wo31-phase-a`, `wo13`, `wo16`, `wo19`, `wo21`, `wo22`, `wo15`, `wo24`, `wo26`, `wo29`, `beta-review-carried-fixes`, `launch-gate-signup-cap-and-metrics`, `publish-billing-investigation`, `i18n-phase3` — dominated by `auth` via `frontend/src/lib/supabase.ts` and `frontend/src/app/login/**` under `frontend/src/**` scopes) **and 4 more on the floor** (`wo31-phase-b` 30 turns, `wo20` 30, `wo23` 15, `i18n-plan-closeout` 15); against the pilot sweep's "13 of 22 undeclared, 5 with ≤ 40 turns": the floor set matches exactly (the sweep's ≤40 measure equals the 60-floor refusal set on this queue); the risk count differs 14-vs-13 over 23-vs-22 tasks because the landed queue gained `send-from-own-gmail-dispatch` after the sweep (it passes) and the sweep counted one task this computation does not refuse — the mechanical rule (fnmatch over default-branch tracked files + literals) is stricter than the sweep's hand count where a scope reaches a risk path only through `*` crossing `/` or a literal future file. That is the point: this is what the owner will see at the next approvals. **AC-4's parser over the run-2 shape** (pypdf added to `backend/requirements.txt` + both locks, on the real origin/main blobs): finds exactly `pypdf` — one direct declared entry, one registry check, verdict pass, no escalation — the wo21 scenario F-71 exists to unblock.

**Spec mismatches / amendments (report, not resolved):**

1. **adopt_queue does not run the pre-spend checks; reconcile does, on the runnable boundary.** adopt_queue records approvals that already exist (tasks in frozen statuses) — it approves nothing, so AC-1/AC-3 have no decision to gate; a stored-bad contract it adopts is still caught by `check_v0_gates`/approval when anything re-approves it. Reconcile entries to `ready`/`blocked`/`needs_human` (runnable targets) take the full pre-spend battery; entries to proven-criteria targets (`in_review` and beyond — built work) take none, exactly the F-65 scope boundary.
2. **The near-miss rule reads "a name on a top-package list it isn't itself"** — implemented as: a name that IS on the list skips the scan entirely (sibling-popular packages are not typosquats), and a name within DL-1 of any OTHER list entry fails. False-positive rate on the shipped lists: 4.0% (pypi) / 5.2% (npm) of list members sit DL-1 from another member — all removed by the list-member skip; the residual class is a genuinely new name DL-1 from a popular one (e.g. a hypothetical `httpx3`), which is the defense working.
3. **Grammar choices (refused rather than parsed):** requirements environment markers (`; python_version …` — not deterministically evaluable parent-side), direct URL/VCS/editable requirements, every pip option besides `-r`/`-c`, `package-lock.json` v1 (no `packages` map), pyproject dependency surfaces outside `[project] dependencies`/`optional-dependencies` (poetry/uv/dependency-groups/build-system/dynamic — escalated, never parsed), and a REMOVED direct dependency (escalated; the contract declares additions, never removals — the F-31 rule kept). Lock-file removals are NOT escalated (locks prune on every regen).
4. **The importer's risk/floor halves skip when no profile was loaded** (a queue beside no profile file, or a direct `plan_import` call): the real session path always loads the profile strictly, and approval's fail-closed checks stand behind the skip. Recorded as a design choice, not a hole: the importer cannot invent the project's risk globs.
5. **AC-2's exact-form grammar allows any number of `::` name segments; `resolve_test_ref` (the F-49 queue audit) refuses more than `path::Class::name`**, and refuses parametrised ids (`name[param]`) where AC-2's syntactic check does not. No divergence reaches approval: the audit runs in CI over the repo's queue, and a 4-segment or parametrised ref that somehow lands is still refused by AC-2's own path/emptiness rules or caught by the audit — but the two rules are not one rule. Unifying them is F-66-era queue-migration territory.
6. **The defaults 60/30 are the owner's to confirm** (kept as `BUDGET_FLOOR_DEFAULT_*` named constants); the profile can override per project.

**What could NOT be verified:** the registry checks against the REAL registries (every test stubs the one HTTP seam; the shipped lists were generated from the real sources — `curl` against hugovk.dev and registry.npmjs.org — but no test proves a live package answers today); a real JobFinderOS import/approval cycle on this code (the owner runs it after merge — the real-shape probes above are this code's verdict on the artefacts it will see, read-only); the F-72 consequences (JobFinderOS's queue will refuse 18 of 23 runnable drafts at approval until their contracts are amended — deliberate, and the pilot's next step); and the npm list's long-term stability (the search-API union ranking is a snapshot; regeneration is the script, staleness weakens nothing).

- **AC-2** proven: `tests/test_f71.py::test_ac_2`
- **AC-3** proven: `tests/test_f71.py::test_ac_3`
- **AC-4** proven: `tests/test_f71.py::test_ac_4`

**Round 1 (review findings R1-1..R1-8, fixed 2026-11-12, red-first per arm; the 30-mutation battery re-run RED on the round-1 tree — M22's driver string re-anchored after the R1-7 edit, verified RED standalone, restore byte-identical — and the reviewer's own battery (/tmp/f71rev/battery.py) re-run against a fresh snapshot of the fixed tree: R0-baseline GREEN (369 passed), R1..R4 and R6..R18 all RED (caught), R5 SETUP-FAIL 0x — the `"scalar"` additions call it targets became the path-keyed `"lock"` mode, covered by M14/M15 and the (q4) nested-bump arm; R13 GREEN-SURVIVED is the equivalent mutant the reviewer themselves classed (the empty-test-name check still refuses it; the real attack shape — a bare .py path — is pinned red by my retargeted M8)):**

- **R1-1 (HIGH) — the policy now judges WHERE THE CODE COMES FROM, not only the name.** `factory/deps.py`: `_npm_registry_specifier` refuses every package.json value that is not a registry range (any `: / @ \` — kills npm: aliases, git+/github, file:, link:, workspace:, portal:, user/repo shorthand, local tarball paths in one deterministic rule; semver ranges and dist-tags carry none of those characters); `parse_requirement` refuses PEP 508 direct references (`name @ …` — rest starting `@` or carrying `: / @ \`); `parse_package_lock` refuses `link: true` entries and any `resolved` outside `https://registry.npmjs.org/`, keys every pin by its FULL `node_modules` path (nested duplicates are distinct pins — the old name-keyed map let a nested bump overwrite the root pin last-wins), and carries `(name, version, resolved, integrity)` so a rotated `resolved`/`integrity` at the same version is an added pin that gets registry-checked. Arms (p) 7 specifier shapes × refusal with the reason, (q1) link, (q2) foreign resolved, (q3) rotated integrity at same version, (q4) nested-only bump, (r1/r2) the pypi direct reference in the direct file and the lock. Mutations M24/M25 RED.
- **R1-2 (HIGH) — the body cap is measured, streamed, and the call count is bounded.** `REGISTRY_MAX_BYTES` 1 MiB → **40 MiB** (measured 2026-11-11 with curl: npm next 31.5 MB, typescript 15.9, react 7.1; pypi numpy 3.7 — 40 MiB covers the largest with ~27% headroom); `_http_get_json` reads in 1 MiB chunks so an over-size body aborts AT the cap, never buffered whole. The wall-time question, answered on the real shape: JobFinderOS's locks carry 70 + 74 pypi pins and ~454 package-lock node_modules entries — a full-regen diff where every pin counts as added would issue ~500 sequential calls, worst case 10 s each ≈ **82 minutes inside L0** (happy path 0.1–2.7 s each ≈ 8–20 min — still a gate no owner would wait on). Fix: `MAX_REGISTRY_CALLS = 30` per L0 run (`factory/deps.py`) — the first 30 added packages are checked, the overflow fails closed with the count and cap named; worst case 30 × 10 s = 5 min. A cross-run disk cache would fix the happy path but not the timeout tail and adds invalidation surface — PROPOSED (class 4), not built. Arms (s1/s2) exercise the REAL read path (`_http_get_json_real`, urlopen faked, no network): a 5 MiB document passes (above the old 1 MiB, real npm docs are 31.5), a 41 MiB document fails; (y) the cap: 31 declared aged packages → exactly 30 passes, the 31st fails with the cap reason. Mutations M26/M30 RED.
- **R1-3 (MED) — the risk reach is computed on the branch the RUN builds on.** `factory/gates.py` `default_branch_files(repo, default_branch)`: when the repo has an `origin` remote it FETCHES (forced refspec `+refs/heads/<default>:refs/remotes/origin/<default>`, hardened git, 60 s timeout, prompt-disabled) and reads the tree from the tracking ref; a repo with no origin (a fixture, a local-only repo) is its own truth and reads its local default. A failed fetch refuses (approval: ApprovalError; the importer: ImportRefused) — the remote default is what the run will build on, and the pilot's checkout sat 11 commits behind origin on another branch. Both callers (`approve._tracked_files`, `plan_import`) switched. Arm (i): origin/main carries `billing/late_invoice.py` that local main lacks → approval refuses naming the flag and the file; declared → approves. Mutation M21 (fetch skipped, local ref used) RED.
- **R1-4 (MED) — the three surviving mutants each gained an arm, then both batteries re-ran.** (t) echoed-name: the registry answering for another package fails the check, the reason naming it (their R4 now RED). (u) removal: a direct dependency removed from requirements.txt escalates with "removed" in the reason (their R7 now RED). (k) the F-62 pair rule: a profile naming another project than the queue refuses, both names in the message (their R17 now RED). Their battery on a fresh snapshot: R0-baseline green, R1–R4, R6–R18 RED.
- **R1-5 (LOW) — the top-package list fails closed.** `load_top_packages` raises `RegistryError` for a missing/unreadable file AND for an empty list; `RegistryClient.check` catches it and fails the package with the reason (never a silently-disabled near-miss rule). Arm (v): the real loader against a scratch dir raises for both shapes (asserted directly), and the raise lands in a check verdict fail naming the list. Mutation M27 RED.
- **R1-6 (LOW) — an added manifest now parses, exactly as the docstring claimed.** `_blob` proves presence/absence from the TREE (`ls-tree` with a literal pathspec — no lazy-blob-fetch failure can masquerade as either), reads absence as the empty side, and refuses on every other error. A manifest file ADDED by the diff therefore parses against an empty base — every entry judged as an addition; a DELETED one keeps its base side for the removal rule (which escalates). Arm (w): a new `requirements-extra.txt` (profile glob `requirements*.txt`) adding declared, aged pypdf passes with no escalation. Mutation M28 (absent→refuse again) RED. Spec mismatch recorded: pre-round-1, no task could ADD a dependency-manifest file and pass; now one can, with every entry judged.
- **R1-7 (LOW) — the candidates include L0's implicit allowances.** `scope_risk_flags` gains `generated_globs` (tracked files matching the profile's `paths.generated`) and `extra_files` (the task's own contract test files); both approval and the importer pass them, so a risk glob covering what the run may legitimately touch demands its flag at approval exactly as L0's matcher will at run time. Arms (j1) a contract-test ref file under a risk glob, (j2) a generated path under a risk glob — each refuses naming the file. Mutations M22/M23 RED.
- **R1-8 (LOW) — malformed registry documents fail the check, never crash L0.** The echoed-name extraction types every field (`info` must be a dict before `.get`); a wrong shape lands in the echoed-name refusal. Arm (x): `info` as a list → verdict fail, no exception. Mutation M29 RED.

The spec sweep the reviewer kept: TASK-CONTRACT §dependencies row and VALIDATOR-SPEC L0's dependency paragraph now state the non-registry-specifier refusals, the measured 40 MiB cap with streamed reads, the 30-call run cap, the lock pin keying (path-keyed, resolved/integrity-sensitive) and the added-manifest rule.

**Round 2 (review findings R2-1..R2-4, fixed 2026-11-12, red-first per arm; the 36-mutation battery re-run RED on the round-2 tree and the reviewer's p_r2.py probes re-run: A/B/C refuse, D's `.npmrc` is judged):**

- **R2-1 (HIGH) — `resolved` must be the entry's OWN canonical tarball.** `factory/deps.py` `canonical_npm_tarball(name, version)` builds the registry's canonical URL (`…/<name>/-/<unscoped>-<version>.tgz`; scoped `…/@scope/name/-/name-<version>.tgz`); `parse_package_lock` refuses any `resolved` that is not EXACTLY it (a same-host URL for another package's tarball is npm's own alias shape — the check would run on the name while the install fetches the other package) and refuses an entry `name` field that differs from its `node_modules` path's package name. Arms (r2a) cross-package tarball, (r2b) a version mismatch between the URL and `version`, (r2d) an alias `name` field on an otherwise-canonical URL; (r2c) a scoped package in the right shape passes, checked by name. Mutations M25 (canonical check removed) and M31 (alias field check removed) RED.
- **R2-2 (HIGH) — a changed or added non-link entry without `resolved` refuses.** Judged at addition time in `DependencyAnalyser.allows` (an unchanged entry is not re-judged): no `resolved` and not `inBundle: true` → manifest-fail, "npm would fetch it from whatever registry the environment configures". `inBundle: true` entries are exempt — bundled code ships inside its parent's tarball, which is itself checked (stated in the docstring). The pin tuple widened to `(name, version, resolved, integrity, bundled)` so an `inBundle` flip also counts as a change. Arms (r2e) a version bump with `resolved` dropped refuses with the reason; (r2f) an `inBundle` entry without `resolved` passes, registry-checked by name. Mutations M32 (refusal removed) and M33 (the exemption removed — a stricter mutant, proving the exemption is pinned) RED.
- **R2-3 (HIGH) — package.json is compared like pyproject: the four maps are parsed, everything else dependency-bearing escalates.** `parse_package_json` now returns `(deps, other_surface)` where `other_surface` is the canonical JSON of the document minus the four maps minus an explicit inert allowlist (`_INERT_PACKAGE_JSON_KEYS`: name/version/description/main/type/module/types/typings/exports/files/browser/os/cpu/engines/private/scripts/keywords/author/contributors/license/repository/homepage/bugs/funding/sideEffects/unpkg/jsdelivr — metadata with no install consequence); `overrides`, `resolutions`, `bundle(d)Dependencies`, `workspaces` and every unknown key are NOT inert — a change there escalates, fail closed (an override can install a package the maps never name). Arms (r2g) overrides, (r2h) resolutions, (r2i) an unknown key each escalate with the reason; (r2j) a scripts-only change still passes. Mutations M34 (the surface check removed) and M35 (overrides smuggled into the inert list) RED.
- **R2-4 (MED) — the registry-redirecting config files are protected by the schema-level default control.** `project.schema.json` `$defs.defaultProtectedControl.const` gains `.npmrc`, `.yarnrc`, `.yarnrc.yml`, `pip.conf`, `pip.ini`, `uv.toml`, `.pypirc` (root and any depth — the two-entry pattern the runner-config default uses), beside `.factory/**`; `default_protected_control()` reads the const, so the loader union and L0's independent re-union both pick it up with no code change — a diff that points the registry somewhere else is `protected_path_touched` on every project, whatever the profile says. Arm (r2k): an `.npmrc` added to a diff is a protected-path FINDING naming `.npmrc` plus the escalation, on the bare default profile. Mutation M36 (the files dropped from the const) RED. Docs swept: VALIDATOR-SPEC's protected-path bullet and TASK-CONTRACT §5's `paths.protected` row state the list.

**Owner decision to revisit (recorded per the reviewer's request):** the 30-call registry cap means a lock regen with more than 30 changed pins ALWAYS escalates `new_dependency` (fail closed, count and cap named in the report). JobFinderOS's locks carry ~500 entries between them, so a full regen will trip it; the exits are slicing the change, the owner accepting via F-72's recover, or raising the cap (a named constant plus, if the happy path hurts, the class-4 disk-cache proposal in the round-1 section).

Existing fixture re-seats this round (each ★, each mechanical, honest): the lock arms in tests/test_f71.py gained canonical `resolved`/`integrity` fields (a real v3 lock carries them, and R2-2 refuses pin changes without one) and the shared stub answers gained npm lodash; the (q2) reason assertion follows the new canonical-tarball message.

## F-72 — Escalations keep the work, retry first, and never stop the batch (guarded retries, arbiter, escalation draft PR, one-command accept, parking loop)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-72 (drafted in #129, owner-approved by merging it 2026-11-11). The F-13 pilot's run 2 passed L0 (765 tests green) and escalated `new_dependency` + `risk_path_touched`; the factory deleted the clone and pushed nothing — a green, tested change was lost with no diff to judge. 13 of 19 escalation reasons had no recovery command; `factory loop` stopped the whole batch at the first escalation; protected-path and sentinel hits escalated on the first round even when one revert would have fixed them. F-72 makes an escalation cheap: two classes retry as findings before they ever reach the owner, a disputed review goes to an arbiter first, whatever still escalates arrives as a draft PR with a one-command answer, and the loop carries on. Built 2026-11-12 in `feat/f72-escalations-keep-work`.

**Built per AC:**

- **AC-1 — guarded changes retry before they escalate:** `factory/orchestrator.py` ★ at the L0-escalation site: when EVERY escalation in the round's set is `protected_path_touched`/`sentinel_failed` (`_GUARDED_RETRIES`) and NO strike key has repeated, the parent converts the hits into fix-round findings (`_guarded_strike_keys` computes class+path / class+sentinel keys from the PARENT's L0 report; `_guarded_instruction` names the path or the sentinel+output tail with "revert this; this contract may not change it") and the round takes the normal failed-round path — nothing pushed, no PR. **Any immediate class beside them (credential, manifest, risk) keeps today's at-once escalation** — a retry under an immediate class would be a gate weakened. The strike key's count is per run, never reset by a round that misses it (a different path is a NEW first strike: A, B, A escalates on round 3 — the alternation attack), bounded by the run's existing round/budget limits (never adds rounds). **Integrity on history:** a run that converted a guarded strike publishes its validated tree as ONE squashed commit on the base (`_squash_round_history`, tree equality asserted — the push fence's history scan re-proves secrets), so a reverted protected change leaves no trace in ANY published commit. `credential_added` untouched (immediate, never retried, never pushed); F-65 scope behaviour untouched (request escalates at once; unrequested out-of-scope is a finding, its own repeat rule unchanged); `new_dependency`/`risk_path_touched` escalate as today — now with the work kept (AC-3).
- **AC-2 — an arbiter settles a non-converging review:** where `review_not_converging` fires (the F-23 `_round_stuck` stop and the F-41 exhausted final review) and findings survive to rule on, the parent starts **one arbiter session per run** (`ARBITER_TURN_CAP = 20` beside the reviewer cap, same `_REVIEW_TURN_FLOOR` assert): the rendered contract, the diff under the same `_REVIEW_DIFF_CAP`, and ONLY the repeated findings' id+text (`_arbiter_prompt`; the finding text rides fenced as data under the untrusted-content heading — never the implementer's or reviewer's argument, no session output). The seat: `factory/session.py` ★ gains the `arbiter` SEATS entry (the verifier's exact read-only contract: no edit tools, the reviewer/verifier integrity guard — it runs in the WORK clone, where a write would ride the next round's `git add -A`); `factory/provider.py` ★ resolves it through the ONE resolver with `SEAT_ROLES += arbiter` and `SEAT_DEFAULTS = {"arbiter": "reviewer"}` (an un-overridden arbiter IS the reviewer's provider+model; the owner may override with `seat_providers.arbiter` under F-59's providers_allowed rule and F-63's re-approval — the schema ★ admits the seat). Rulings parse through `factory/l2.py` ★ `parse_arbiter_rulings` (the verdict parse's candidate machinery; unknown id, missing/duplicate ruling, wrong enum, no reason, or TWO disagreeing ruling objects each fail the whole arbitration). Parent handling: `invalid` drops the finding (recorded — `arbiter_dropped` + the reason ride the L2 report, rendered "for the record, not to fix"); all-invalid settles the dispute and the round publishes through the normal gate chain (the F-41 final review still runs when due); `valid` grants exactly one more fix round carrying only the surviving findings (a final-review stop routes the extra round as the final repeat); `contract`, a failed arbiter session, or a second stop after a ruling escalates `review_not_converging` with the rulings attached (report + note). The arbiter can never pass L0/L1, approve or merge; its tokens/turns count in the attempt's usage with `model` seat-labelled `arbiter=…`.
- **AC-3 — an escalation after a session keeps the work as a draft PR:** `_keep_work` (orchestrator ★), called from every post-session needs_human site (L0 immediate/repeated, scope request + repeats, both review_not_converging sites, the arbiter escalation sites, attempts_exhausted, budget_exhausted ×5): for every escalation except `credential_added`/`quota_exhausted`/`scope_missing` (`_ESCALATION_PR_NEVER`), when the run branch holds ≥1 commit beyond the base, the EXISTING publish path — `check_pr_text`, `check_push_content` over the branch's WHOLE history, `push_branch`, then the PR seam with `draft=True` + the `factory-escalation` label (`factory/publish.py` ★: `PullRequest.draft/.label`, `gh_pr_create --draft/--label`; gh's unknown-label refusal lands in `pr_create_failed`, honest). Title prefixed `[escalation]`; the body (`_escalation_pr_body`) names the escalation, the stored report path, the L0/L1 summaries under the existing caps, and both one-command answers; session text passes the egress seam before the body is built. `links.pr`/`links.branch`/`links.escalation_pr` (schema ★) are set with the attempt in one save; the clone is cleaned by the run's own finally — AFTER the push. A refused/failed push records `publish_refused`/`pr_create_failed` with the original escalation in the note, reports stay on disk. Crash windows (`_finish_unrecorded_publish` ★, extended): a pass attempt's unrecorded links complete as before; an ESCALATED attempt with an unmatched `pr` completes its links and returns the task to `needs_human` (a crash between the links save and the transition leaves it re-queued with the PR open — the owner closes the orphan; window documented). The sweep and the concurrency count exclude `links.escalation_pr` PRs (`_pr_urls`, `_count_open_prs`, the sweep's open_prs filter): never re-validated, never reported `ready`, never holding the writer slot; accept clears the marker (the PR becomes a normal in_review PR).
- **AC-4 — the owner answers in one command:** `factory recover <task-id> --accept --evidence "…"` / `--reject` (cli ★, `factory/approve.py` ★ `_recover_escalation_pr`, both interactive y/N, `--yes` refused). Eligible only for `new_dependency`/`risk_path_touched` holding an escalation draft PR (`factory/state.py` ★ `escalation_accept_eligible`; every other escalation named in the refusal). **Accept:** read the PR head SHA (`factory/publish.py` ★ `gh_pr_head_sha`), show it with the escalation, the stored L0 summary and the rendered contract; on y record the waiver in the owner ledger (`factory/approvals.py` ★ + approvals.schema.json ★: the `escalation_waiver` kind — task, escalation, SHA, contract hash, evidence, who, when; idempotent per key so a crashed re-run reuses it), then `factory/orchestrator.py` ★ `recover_accept_round` (under the queue's idle_lease — the F-45 pin proves the lease covers its run dirs): fresh clone at that EXACT SHA (fetch the branch, `cat-file -e` the commit — a force-push refuses), merge-base as base, L0 with ONLY the named class waived (`surviving = escalations − {class}` — any other class, or findings, fails the re-run), L1, then the review as a normal full-diff round (fan-out at `REVIEWER_TURN_CAP`, one parse retry per seat, verifier over findings at `VERIFIER_TURN_CAP`, the ONE shared `settle_review_verdict` chain), then the head RE-READ before anything is marked ready (a moved head voids the waiver — "the same accept after a new commit refuses"), `gh pr ready`, and `TaskQueue.recover_accept` writes the gated `needs_human → in_review` edge (the ledger waiver for exactly (task, class, SHA, contract) is re-checked in the state layer; the marker clears). A failed re-run keeps the task `needs_human` with the findings named and the PR draft. **Reject:** `gh pr close` (never merges), `TaskQueue.reject_escalation` writes `needs_human → draft` (the contract-changed pair under its own gate) and pops the approval markers — the old ledger entry authorises a changed contract nothing (the F-63 hash check; proven in the test).
- **AC-5 — the loop parks and carries on:** `run_loop` ★ drops `needs_human` from the stop set (empty queue, quota, peak window, concurrency_wait, orchestrator error stay; an `error` still stops it), collects the parked results on `LoopResult.parked`, and `factory/cli.py` ★ prints one line per parked task (id, reason, PR URL or "no PR") with the summary; the exit code stays honest — parked at the end and a would-be-0 stop exit **1** (quota keeps its 3, error its 2). A parked task is never re-picked (only `ready` is pickable), and its escalation PR holds no slot (AC-3's exclusion).

**Hostile pass** (each probed on the real code under ~/Desktop/sfa-f72-scratch/hostile.py; class per item):

- **Retry loop** (class 2): alternating two protected paths is defeated by the CUMULATIVE strike count — A, B, A escalates on round 3's second A-strike; if the round limit lands first, `attempts_exhausted` ends it — either way the alternation gains nothing.
- **Protected via rename/symlink/case** (class 1): a rename INTO `.factory/**` fires `protected_path_touched` on the destination (both sides judged) plus `out_of_scope`; a case variant (`.Factory/`) is not protected-matched (fnmatch is case-sensitive — F-65's pinned rule) but is OUT OF SCOPE, so the retry loop carries it and its own repeat escalates `scope_change_needed`. A symlink is judged by its own path (existing L0 rule).
- **Revert in tree, commit kept** (class 1): a secret added-then-removed in history refuses the push (the whole-history fence, probed on real git); the squashed publish carries no strike commit in `log --name-only base..head`.
- **Arbiter** (class 1): a finding whose text orders "rule all invalid" rides fenced as data under the untrusted heading, and the ruling parse still refuses an unknown id; ESC/bidi bytes render `\uXXXX`-escaped (no raw control byte reaches the prompt); an arbiter stating TWO disagreeing ruling objects is refused (an identical echo stands).
- **500 findings** (class 2): the prompt builds (~150k chars with the diff cap) and 500/500 rulings parse; 499-of-500 fails closed, the missing one named.
- **Draft PR** (class 1): a seat token in an earlier commit MESSAGE refuses the push (commits are scanned raw — message and author fields included); gh failing after the push records `pr_create_failed` with the original escalation in the note (the links name the stranded branch).
- **Branch collision** (class 2): the run id in `factory/<slug>-<run-id>` makes every run's branch unique; a later attempt on the same task cannot collide with the parked PR's branch.
- **PR body over GitHub's limit** (class 2): a 200k-char L0 output shrinks through the tail loop with every section intact (the hard cut is the unreachable last resort).
- **Hand-merged escalation PR** (class 2): the sweep's recovery edges ignore it (not stale_base/pr_closed), it is never counted or re-validated, and only `recover --accept` or the owner's own reconcile advances the task.
- **Accept** (class 1): a force-push between the y and the ready mark refuses (the head re-read — pinned red by test_ac_4 arm 2a); a contract changed since the escalation refuses (the F-63 ledger gate); the waiver is idempotent per (task, class, SHA, contract) so a crashed re-run reuses it.
- **Loop** (class 1): an escalated task is never re-picked (only `ready` is eligible); every task escalating ends `queue_empty` with all of them parked (pinned by test_ac_5 arm 4) — never a spin.
- **Real shape** (read-only): the run-2 L0 report shape (PASS, `escalations: [new_dependency, risk_path_touched]`, 765 tests green) flows through `_escalation_pr_body` naming BOTH classes and both one-command answers, and the waiver key refuses the un-asked class; end-to-end, test_ac_3 arm 1 + test_ac_4 arm 3 replay exactly this shape on real git with stubbed gh.

**Spec mismatches / amendments (report, not resolved):**

1. **Squash vs real revert:** the squash was chosen. A real revert leaves the guarded change in the earlier round's commit objects — exactly the trace the integrity rule forbids — so the branch is rebuilt as one commit on the base whose TREE is asserted equal to the validated head's. Cost: the published history loses the per-round commits (the PR body and the progress log keep the round record); only runs that converted a guarded strike are squashed.
2. **`attempts_exhausted`/`budget_exhausted` escalation PRs are NOT `--accept`-able** (the spec says no): their exit stays `--reset-attempts` (budget) or a fresh contract. The draft PR still opens for them (the general rule — the work is kept for the owner's judgement); a hand-merge is the owner's business. Not a dead end beyond the existing paths, but the PR is answerable only by hand for these two classes.
3. **`risk_path_touched` needs a ledger re-record before `--accept`** when the run added the risk flag to the task (today's behaviour): the factory's own flag mutation changes the contract hash, so the accept's F-63 gate demands `factory adopt` first — one command for `new_dependency` (the AC's own happy path), two for `risk_path_touched`. The accept prompt renders the current contract so the owner sees the added flag.
4. **`scope_change_needed` and `scope_missing` keep draft PRs** under the general post-commit rule (the brief's exception list does not name them): a scope-request escalation with earlier-round commits keeps the work. The exit stays the F-65 widen-adopt-recover path; the PR is context, not an answer surface.
5. **One arbiter session per RUN, not per attempt-record**: the spec says "per attempt" but also "a second non-convergence after an arbiter ruling escalates" — the run (the spec's own "attempt" in AC-1's wording) is the unit that satisfies both; the per-record reading would allow an infinite arbiter-per-round ladder.
6. **The arbiter runs only when findings survive to rule on** (`l2.findings` non-empty): the `all_dropped` no-progress case (every finding dropped, the declared fail standing) has nothing to rule and escalates as today. Recorded as a design choice.
7. **The accept round's reviewer wall budgets are fixed** (60 min fan-out / 30 min verifier): it is owner-driven and outside the task's attempt budget, so F-64's wall arithmetic does not apply; the caps are the F-64 constants.
8. **The arbiter's prompt version is not in `prompt_versions`** (the attempt's `model` field carries `arbiter=<model>` and its usage counts): the arbiter's user prompt is built dynamically per stop, so there is no stable assembled-prompt hash to record at run start; F-19 credit assignment reads the seat label.

**What could NOT be verified:** a real `gh pr create --draft --label` against GitHub (every test stubs the seam; the label must exist on the repo or the escalation lands in `pr_create_failed` — an owner creates `factory-escalation` once per repo before the first escalation, or the first escalation PR names it); a real paid arbiter seat (the stub seats prove the plumbing; the reviewer-default resolution and the seat_providers.arbiter override are proven at the resolver); the accept round's re-review with a real model; `gh pr ready`/`gh pr close`/`gh pr view --json headRefOid` on a real PR (seams only); and a loop parking a LIVE paid escalation (the quota/peak interactions with parked state are unit-proven, not run live).

**Round 1 (review findings F2–F10 fixed 2026-11-12, red-first per arm; F1 HELD for the owner's decision — escalation draft PRs run L0-refused content in CI (`on: push` fires on the push, `pull_request` workflows fire on the draft, same-repo PRs get secrets; before F-72 none of this was ever pushed). No design change made for F1 beyond F2's no-push rule, which stands either way):**

- **R1-F2 (HIGH) — a guarded strike on the LAST round is never converted.** The conversion promised a next fix round; with none left it masked the class behind `attempts_exhausted` AND pushed the refused content. Two halves: (a) `attempt_no < max_attempts` gates the conversion (the strike escalates as its own class); (b) `guard_pending` (this round's L0 still sees a guarded hit) with `guard_repeated` False makes `_keep_work` push NOTHING under any class — only the adjudicated repeat (the 2-strike escalation, whose class names the content) keeps its work. This also covers the budget-death-after-conversion window. Arm: test_ac_1 (8) — max_attempts=1 + a protected touch gives `protected_path_touched`, no PR, no pushed path. Mutations M25/M26 RED.
- **R1-F3 (HIGH) — `_keep_work` squashes exactly as publish does.** A converted-then-reverted strike rode the escalation PR's round history. `_squash_round_history` (same tree assertion) now runs in `_keep_work` when `guard_retried`; the pushed escalation history is one commit. Arm: test_ac_1 (9) — revert-then-stay-wrong at max_attempts=2 gives an `attempts_exhausted` draft PR whose pushed history never names the protected file (1 commit). Mutation M27 RED.
- **R1-F4 (HIGH) — control characters can neither break the attempt write nor orphan the PR.** (1) `_esc` (scrub_session_text → escape_approval_text, the scope-request precedent) wraps every session-written string that reaches a note/finding: the arbiter's reason (rulings note, contract note, drop findings), the failed-session reason. The CSI attack sequence is stripped by the seam, the bidi override renders `‭`-visible. (2) `_keep_work` persists links.pr/branch/escalation_pr the moment the PR exists — a later failure records the attempt around durable links; `_link_escalation_pr` folded away. (3) Arm: test_ac_2 (8) — the P4 shape gives `needs_human` with the PR linked, the note and body escaped (no raw ESC/bidi, `‭` visible). Mutation M28 RED.
- **R1-F5 (HIGH) — the sweep consults the accept waiver.** An accepted PR no longer bounces to `stale_base` on the first unrelated merge: the re-validation drops escalation classes the ledger holds a waiver for — matched on (task, class, PR head SHA, current contract hash); the PR head is unchanged when only the base moved, and any new commit on the branch voids the key. Arm: test_ac_4 (8) — accept, advance main, sweep → the task stays `in_review` with a ready line; a new PR-branch commit then escalates `stale_base`. Mutation M29 RED.
- **R1-F6 (MED) — the attempts_exhausted attempt keeps its note.** The round-bottom `_keep_work` note now rides the attempt, so a `pr_create_failed` names the original escalation and the failure. Arm: test_ac_3 (6) (the P3 shape). RED-on-old: note=None; green: both named.
- **R1-F7 (MED) — superseded escalation PRs close.** `_close_superseded_escalation` (module-level, best-effort, progress-log recorded, stderr on failure — never the run) fires at BOTH sites where `links.pr` is overwritten: in `_keep_work` (a new escalation PR, after the new link lands) and at the validated publish (which also CLEARS a stale `escalation_pr` marker — a re-queued task's validated PR must be re-validated and reported ready, not skipped as an escalation PR). Arm: test_ac_3 (7) — park, `recover --reset-attempts` (the legal re-queue), re-run: exactly one open PR, the old URL closed through the seam. Mutation M32 RED.
- **R1-F8 (LOW) — reject writes the state first.** `queue.reject_escalation` runs before `gh pr close`; a close failure raises ApprovalError naming the hand-close, with the task already draft. Arm: test_ac_4 (9) — a failing close leaves the task `draft`. Mutation M30 RED.

**Round 2 (F1 built from the owner's decision 2026-11-12 15:39 CET, verbatim: "1 yes, A with guards"; the owner's reason, verbatim: "we are trying to keep this as automated as possible as well, that is the whole point of the factory to be able to push work through it with me only where absolutely needed." — an owner-approved amendment to AC-3 and AC-4, per /tmp/f72rev/f1-decision.md):**

- **Part 1 — guarded classes never push.** `protected_path_touched` and `sentinel_failed` join `_ESCALATION_PR_NEVER`: no push, no PR, the reports stay on disk and the attempt note names the stored report path (`; L0 report: <path>`). The F2 `guard_pending` rule stays (an unadjudicated guarded hit blocks every other class's push too). Arms: test_ac_1 (2) rewritten — a 2-strike protected escalation pushes nothing, records the class, names a real report file whose stored L0 names the class; the 2-strike sentinel arm gains the same no-push + report-path asserts. RED-old: `env.prs == [PullRequest(...)]` (a draft PR opened); green-new: `env.prs == []`, `remote_branches() == ["main"]`, the report path parseable and its file present.
- **Part 2 — every other escalation PR pushes squashed with CI suppressed.** `_keep_work` now ALWAYS rebuilds the branch as one commit on the base (`_squash_round_history`, tree asserted) with the message `{task_id}: escalation snapshot (CI suppressed until recover --accept) [skip ci]`; the PR body states CI is suppressed until `recover --accept`. Arms: test_ac_3 (1) gained `pushed_commit_count == 1`, `[skip ci]` in the pushed head's message, and the body's suppression line. RED-old: no suppression line and the pushed history carried the round commits; green-new: all three hold.
- **Guard 1 — refuse where `[skip ci]` does not protect.** `_unsafe_workflow_triggers(repo, base_sha)` parses every `.github/workflows/*.{yml,yaml}` AT THE BASE (YAML-parsed, not substring-matched; `on` handled as string, list and map, and the bare-`on`-reads-as-boolean-True key): a `pull_request_target` or `workflow_run` trigger, or an unparseable workflow file, refuses the escalation push as `publish_refused` (the offending `path:trigger` pairs and the original escalation in the note; nothing pushed, reports stay). Arms: test_ac_3 — a base with `pull_request_target` refuses and pushes nothing; `workflow_run` the same; a plain `push`+`pull_request` workflow pushes (the [skip ci] marker's own territory). RED-old: all three pushed (no scan existed); green-new: the first two `publish_refused` with the trigger named, the third opens the draft PR.
- **Guard 2 — accept proves the tree, then releases a clean commit.** After the re-run passes and the head re-check, the accept RELEASES: one new commit on the SAME PARENT with the SAME TREE (asserted; a mismatch is a hard refusal, nothing pushed) and a message without `[skip ci]`, force-pushed with `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<accepted sha>` (`push_branch`'s new `lease_sha` — the remote moving between the acceptance and the release refuses instead of overwriting), then `gh pr ready`, then the state move. The released SHA rides the waiver (a second ledger entry, `released_sha`, append-only) and `has_escalation_waiver` accepts it — the R1-F5 sweep match covers the branch's new head; any OTHER new commit matches neither and voids it. Arms: test_ac_4 (1) extended — the released head differs from the accepted head, its tree equals the accepted tree, its parent equals the accepted parent, its message has no `[skip ci]` and names the release, the waiver matches the released SHA; (1b) — a concurrent push landing mid-accept (between the re-run and the release) refuses via the lease, the racer's commit stands, the task stays `needs_human`, the PR stays draft; the R1-F5 arm's accept now flows through the release (its sweep match uses `released_sha`) and its post-release commit still gives `stale_base`. RED-old: no release existed (the branch kept the skip-ci commit and the ready mark left it there); green-new: the release invariants hold.
- **Mutation battery (round 2, 8 mutants):** M36 drop the marker on push — RED; M37 drop the tree assertion on release — RED; M38 drop the lease — RED; M39 drop the pull_request_target scan — RED; M40 guarded class still pushes — RED; M41 released SHA not accepted by the waiver match — RED; M42 workflow_run dropped from the unsafe set — RED; M27-recheck (the R1-F3 squash made conditional again, i.e. part 2's unconditional-squash rule removed) — RED. All cp-restored, `__pycache__` purged per restore, tree verified byte-identical.
- **Probes + hostile pass on the final tree:** the reviewer's p_r1.py — P1's KeyError is its own `t["links"]` assumption (no PR and no links key exist: the fix); P2 `reverted protected in pushed history: False`; P3's note carries the original escalation and the failure. The 16-probe hostile pass: 0 errors.
- **R2-1 (HIGH, review round 2) — an escalation PR never carries CI/runner config.** Guard 1's base scan cannot see workflows the BRANCH itself adds or edits, and `[skip ci]` does not suppress `pull_request_review`/`pull_request_review_comment` on same-repo PRs (secrets-bearing). So `_keep_work`, after the squash and before guard 1, refuses the push (`publish_refused`, the offending paths named, nothing pushed, reports on disk) when the diff base..squashed-head touches ANY path under `.github/` — added (in scope or not), deleted, or renamed in. The reviewer's P5 (path in scope) and P6 (not in scope) both pushed the head-added workflow before the fix; after it both give `publish_refused`, 0 PRs, a main-only remote. Arms: test_ac_3 (1d) — P5, P6, a `.github/` deletion, a rename INTO `.github/`, and a non-`.github` escalation restated as still pushing. Mutant M43 (the check dropped): RED. All six reviewer probes green on the fixed tree (P1–P4 stay fixed).
- **Residual, documented:** the profile schema has no non-GitHub-Actions CI field, so guard 1 covers GitHub Actions workflows only — a repo whose CI runs outside Actions (an external webhook builder, a Drone/GitLab sidecar) is not scan-protected; when a profile field for that exists, the guard must consult it. Recorded in the spec-mismatch list.

## F-75 — CI is part of done: a red PR gets a fix round (publish-path CI wait, flake re-run, same-PR fix rounds, ci_failed, every-repo ready rule)

**Source:** BUILD-ORDER §F-75 (drafted in #133, owner-approved by merging it 2026-11-12). The F-13 pilot's first passing run (2026-11-12) passed L0, L1 and review and opened a PR whose project CI then failed on GitHub — a contract test's PDF-extracted text depends on fonts that differ between the factory's macOS host and CI's Ubuntu runner. The factory never read its PR's CI: "done" meant "green on the factory's host", the owner received an unmergeable PR, and the fix had to come back through the owner. F-75 makes the run wait for its PR's CI on the exact pushed SHA, turn a surviving red into a fix round on the same branch and PR, park `ci_failed` when the budget runs out, and report nothing `ready` on red or unknown CI — every repo. Built 2026-11-12 in `feat/f75-ci-is-part-of-done`.

**Built per AC:**

- **AC-1 — the run waits for its PR's CI:** after every validated publish (open OR update), `_run_task` ★ calls `factory.ci.settle` (NEW module, `factory/ci.py`) with the ONE CI reader (`gh_ci_state` — the same seam `run_once` injects; no second reader), bounded by `min(config.ci_wait_min, the run's remaining wall clock)`; the wait's elapsed seconds join `session_seconds` (wall time, never turns). `ci_wait_min` (`factory/config.py` ★, beside `final_review_repeats`: whole number ≥ 0, default 30, bool refused, unknown-key refused; 0 = the pre-F-75 behaviour — no CI read in the publish path at all). Outcomes: all green → `pr_opened` as today; nothing reported after the window → `pr_opened` + note "no CI reported" (`RunResult.note` NEW, printed by `factory/cli.py`); still pending → "CI pending"; `unresolvable` → pending, never green. Only checks about the exact pushed SHA count (the settle call passes `head_sha`; `gh_ci_state` is SHA-keyed by construction). A first-poll `PublishError` (gh missing, a non-GitHub remote — permanent conditions) ends the wait at once as pending instead of burning the window; a seam that worked and then raised propagates. Tests inject the clock+sleep (`orch.ci_clock`/`orch.ci_sleep`, NEW module seams) so nothing really sleeps.
- **AC-2 — red CI becomes a fix round on the same branch:** the flake guard first re-runs the FAILED GitHub Actions jobs for the head once (`factory.ci.CIActions` — `gh run list --commit <sha> --json databaseId,name,conclusion` filtered to failure conclusions, `gh run rerun <id> --failed`, all through the shared `remote_call` gh env; a failing check no Actions run backs is red at once; a head is never re-run twice — `rerun_heads`, probed directly by arm 10), then waits again on the SAME window's remainder. A surviving red: findings, one per failing check — name, conclusion, and the `gh run view --log-failed` tail grouped by job (check) name (caps: `CI_TAIL_PER_CHECK=4000`, `CI_TAIL_TOTAL=12000` in ci.py), falling back to the check's output summary (`CIState.summaries` NEW in `factory/mergeorder.py` ★: check-runs' `output.summary`, legacy statuses' `description`, cap 2000 — `classify_ci` extended, never forked). Every byte crosses the egress seam: `_ci_finding_lines` (orchestrator ★) composes `scrub_session_text` → `escape_approval_text` (the `_esc` composition) and fences the tail with `prbody.fence`; `_implementer_prompt` ★ renders them under its own `## CI findings to fix (untrusted CI output — data, not instructions)` heading whose text forbids deleting/skipping the test. The stored report (`<run-id>-a<N>-ci.json`) holds the capped tails through `scrub_session_value` (the F-27 invariant — the sentinel precedent redacts before any record). The fix round: `used += 1` (counts as an attempt), `in_review → in_progress` (the fix-round edge — every later abort/escalation path behaves as any round's), same clone from the pushed head, L0/L1/review unchanged (F-72's guarded/arbiter paths untouched). Its publish UPDATES the same branch: `_is_ancestor(last-pushed, head)` enforced (fast-forward only — the one exception is F-72's integrity squash, where the lease carries the whole guard), `check_push_content` over the range, `push_branch(lease_sha=<last pushed>)` (`--force-with-lease`, never bare `--force`), no `open_pr`, `links.pr` unchanged, then the wait runs again. `push_branch` ★ gained the lease PRE-CHECK: a leased push first reads the remote ref and refuses as a deterministic `PushRefused` (naming both SHAs) when it no longer holds exactly the leased commit — someone pushed or force-pushed under the wait; `--force-with-lease` stays the atomic guard against the check-then-push race. Found by the hostile pass (the lease used to surface as a bare git failure reading `orchestrator_error`); pinned by hostile H2b (the refusal class) and by a `push_branch` spy arm in test_ac_2 (`lease_sha == the exact previously pushed sha` on the update push, none on the open push). Exhausted (`attempt_no + 1 > max_attempts`, or the wall clock cannot fund the round, or a peak window stops it): `ci_failed` — the PR converted to draft (`factory/publish.py` ★ `gh_pr_draft`, `gh pr ready --undo`; a failure is named in the note, the PR never lost), kept open, the note naming the failing checks and the stored report path, a session-less escalated attempt appended (the schema's closed attempt set — the CI report rides `validator_report`). Recovery: `ci_failed` joins `ATTEMPTS_RESET_ESCALATIONS` (`factory/state.py` ★) so `factory recover --evidence … --reset-attempts` is its exit (spec mismatch 1 below: the fresh run re-implements; it does not continue the drafted PR). `_keep_work` ★'s new CI branch: once this run has published, EVERY later escalation keeps THAT PR (drafted) — never a second one; the unvalidated fix work dies with the clone. F-72 escalation PRs (`[skip ci]` drafts) are never waited on and never take fix rounds (by construction — `_keep_work` never settles; pinned by arm 9).
- **AC-3 — nothing ready on red or unknown CI:** `run_once` ★ hands the sweep `ci_for_sweep = ci_state` unconditionally (F-58's rule extended past `branch_protection: unavailable`); `_refuse_over_red_ci` is untouched (its own purpose, unavailable-only). `_pr_ready_or_not` ★: red → `not ready: CI red (<name=conclusion> …)`, pending OR unresolvable → `not ready: CI pending` (an unresolvable answer is never guessed green — the F-45 rule), checkless keeps its own line.

**Files touched** (★ = protected, change directed by this WO's brief or repo-protected): factory/orchestrator.py ★, factory/config.py ★, factory/state.py ★, factory/publish.py ★, factory/mergeorder.py ★, factory/cli.py, factory/data/schema/tasks.schema.json ★ (escalation enum + ci_failed), factory/ci.py (NEW), tests/test_f75.py (NEW ★), tests/_support/f75.py (NEW ★), .factory/project.yaml ★ (the two new test files in paths.protected), TASK-CONTRACT.md ★ (enum; the in_review→needs_human row; the ci_failed paragraph), VALIDATOR-SPEC.md ★ (the rounds diagram + the CI-is-part-of-done paragraph). Honest fixture re-seats in other WOs' protected test files, each the F-75 behaviour change itself: tests/test_f30.py ★ (the trace grammar gains the ci_wait/ci_settled event types), tests/test_f43.py ★ (the AC-6 egress tripwire now WATCHES factory/ci.py — gh stdout/stderr are attacker text there; allow set: the local shapings + CIHelperError, whose message leaves only through the scrubbed trace writer), tests/test_f58.py ★ (the strict-protection arm inverts: the sweep now consults the PR head's CI on protected repos too — green scripted, the call asserted), tests/test_f45.py ★ (green ci_state on the lease-freeze arm's two runs), tests/test_f63.py ★ (green ci_state on the sweep-acts arm), tests/test_f26.py ★ (review43's four runs script green CI; CIState import), tests/test_f72.py ★ (`_run_once` answers green — the accept-path sweep arms' PRs are healthy by construction), tests/test_f51.py ★ (the guard-pin snapshot ignores `__pycache__`: under xdist pytest's assertion rewriter writes a test module's .pyc on its worker's first import, and WHEN that lands relative to the snapshot is scheduling — a latent race the F-75 suite run exposed; the pin's target is stray files). factory/approve.py ★: the accept path's release-failure wrap also catches PushRefused (F-75's lease pre-check refuses a moved remote as PushRefused BEFORE git runs — f72's arm 1b still hears the same ApprovalError). `.factory/tasks.json` untouched; `~/factory`, `~/Desktop/JobFinderOS` and every jfo-* worktree untouched (marker checks below).

**Red first** (tests/test_f75.py on the untouched tree 75c8d50): `test_ac_1`/`test_ac_2` RED as `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'factory.ci'` (the F-71/F-72 ImportError precedent); `test_ac_3` RED as `TypeError: CIState.__init__() takes from 2 to 4 positional arguments but 5 were given` (summaries missing) — the arms' first assertion past that is the protected-repo ready line, which the old rule reports ready.

**Green:** tests/test_f75.py 3 passed (90 assertions). Gates (worktree .venv, `__pycache__` purged first): `ruff check .` clean; `pyright --warnings factory` 0 errors/0 warnings/0 informations; `pytest -q -n auto -p no:cacheprovider` **1368 passed, 2 skipped** (the pre-F-75 tree's 1365 + this WO's 3 contract tests, with the re-seated assertions above; the two live-only f06 halves skip — no FACTORY_LIVE_TESTS, no credential, no GitHub/Z.ai contact), run TWICE on the FINAL tree — once normally (19:30), once with 30 `ANTHROPIC_*`/`AWS_*`/`API_TIMEOUT_MS`/`CLAUDE_CODE_*` names unset AND `HOME=<empty tmp>` + `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`: identical result. Marker check `find ~/factory -newer <marker>` after each suite run: EMPTY. Identity proof: test_ac_2 (whose arm 1 asserts every pushed commit's committer is the factory's explicit identity) run green under the full scrubbed env AND the file green under `HOME=<empty tmp> GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`. The battery and both late fixes (the approve.py wrap, the f51 snapshot filter) predate these two runs; every mutation's target is unchanged by them.

**Mutation table** (each cp-restored from ~/Desktop/sfa-f75-scratch/mut-backup — never `git checkout` — `__pycache__` purged after every restore, the live tree verified byte-identical to the backup after every mutation):

| # | Mutation | Result |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | the wait asks about the base SHA, not the pushed head | RED |
| 2 | the wait declares `unresolvable` settled (skips the window/note) | RED |
| 3 | the wait declares `pending` settled (skips the window/note) | RED |
| 4 | the window ignores the run's remaining wall clock | RED |
| 5 | no flake re-run (first red straight to a fix round) | RED |

| 8b | the log tail not scrubbed (escape only) | RED |
